“Yes, I understood you to say so. Could you let me see the ledger, and also the cheques that were issued?”

In a few seconds the clerk returned with a ponderous tome, which he opened at the name of Mrs. Helen Sadie Root. The account possessed but few items. On the debit side there was but the single entry of £1,500, but on the other side there were six entries, varying from £210 10s. to £295, and totalling £1,495 7s. 9d. Six cancelled cheques corresponded with the entries. As French examined these, he was interested to see that all were made out on fashionable London jewellers.

“Can you lend me these?” he asked, pointing to the cheques.

===Chinese===

「说提走，是这么个意思：几乎全部数额都开了支票付了出去。那位太太本人没有再来过，账户也没有注销。至今尚有一小笔余额。」

弗伦奇点了点头。

「是的，您是这么说的。能不能让我看看账簿，还有那些开出的支票？」

不多几秒，书记员便捧回一部厚重的账簿，翻到海伦·萨迪·鲁特夫人（Mrs. Helen Sadie Root）的名下。这个账户上条目寥寥：借方只有一笔，就是那 1,500 镑；贷方倒有六笔，从 210 镑 10 先令到 295 镑不等，合计 1,495 镑 7 先令 9 便士。六张注销的支票同这些条目一一相合。弗伦奇查验这些支票时，饶有兴味地发现，它们全都是开给伦敦几家时髦珠宝行的。

「这些能借给我吗？」他指着支票问。

===Original===

The clerk hesitated, but Mr. Scarlett intervened.

“Certainly,” he answered readily, “but you will have to give us a receipt for our auditors.”

This was soon arranged, and after French had asked a few more questions, he and Mr. Williams left the bank.

“Now,” he said briskly, before his companion could frame a remark, “I am going round to these six jewellers, but first I want some further information from you. Shall we go back to your office?”

===Chinese===

书记员迟疑了一下，斯卡利特先生代他接了话。

「当然可以。」他爽快地答道，「不过您得给我们出一张收条，好向查账的人交代。」

这事很快办妥。弗伦奇又问了几个问题，便同威廉斯先生一起离开了银行。

「好了，」他不等同伴想好说什么，便干脆利落地开口，「我这就挨家去跑这六家珠宝行，不过先得向您再讨些情况。回您办公室去谈，怎么样？」

===Original===

Mr. Williams assented eagerly. He had lost his air of detached precision, and, like a somewhat spoiled child, plied the other with questions as to his probable chances of success. French answered in his usual cheery, optimistic way, and it was not until they were once more seated in Mr. Williams’s sanctum that he dropped his air of fatherly benevolence and became once more the shrewd and competent officer of Scotland Yard.

“In the first place,” he began, as he took out his notebook, “I want *your* description of the lady. I gather she was a good-looking woman, attractive both in appearance and manner. Did you find her so?”

Mr. Williams hesitated.

“Well, yes, I did,” he admitted, somewhat apologetically, as French thought. “She certainly had a way with her﻿—something different from my usual clients. From her manner I never should have suspected she was other than all right.”

===Chinese===

威廉斯先生忙不迭地应承下来。他已丢了那副淡漠精确的架子，像个多少被宠坏的孩子，连连追问追回款项的胜算能有几成。弗伦奇照例乐呵呵地宽慰作答；直到两人重新在威廉斯先生的私室里坐定，他才卸下那副慈父般的和气，重新变回苏格兰场那个精明干练的警官。

「首先，」他掏出记事本，开口道，「我要听听*您本人*对那位太太的描述。据我料想，她是位好看的女人，容貌举止都招人喜欢。您觉得是这样吗？」

威廉斯先生迟疑了。

「呃，是的，我觉得是。」他承认道，在弗伦奇听来，语气里颇带着几分歉意。「她确实自有一套动人的风致——同我平日的主顾有些两样。单凭她的做派，我怎么也不会疑心她会有什么不对。」

===Original===

“Most women crooks are attractive looking,” French declared smoothly. “It’s part of their stock in trade. Just let me have as detailed a description of her as you can.”

It seemed she was of middle height, and dark, very dark as to hair and eyelashes, but less so as to eyes. They were rather a golden shade of brown. She had a somewhat retroussé nose, and a tiny mouth set in an oval face, with a complexion of extreme, but healthy, pallor. She wore her hair low over her ears, and her smile revealed an unexpected dimple. Mr. Williams had remarked these details so thoroughly that French smiled inwardly, as he solemnly noted them in his book. The moneylender had not particularly observed what she was wearing, but this did not matter as Mr. Scarlett had, and a detailed description of her dress was already entered up.

“Tell me next, please, Mr. Williams, what identification the lady gave of herself, and what inquiries you made to test her statement. She had lost her passport?”

===Chinese===

「女骗子多半生得好看。」弗伦奇圆熟地说。「那是她们看家营生的本钱。请您把她的模样，记得多详细就给我讲多详细。」

据他所说，她中等身材，肤色偏深——头发睫毛都极黑，眼睛却浅些，是一种偏金黄的褐色。鼻尖微微上翘，一张小嘴，衬在一张鹅蛋脸上，肤色苍白之极，却苍白得健康。她的头发低低地掩过双耳，一笑便露出一处出人意料的酒窝。这些细节威廉斯先生观察得如此周详，弗伦奇一面郑重其事地记进本子，一面在心里暗笑。这位放贷人对她当天的衣着倒不曾特别留意，好在无碍——斯卡利特先生留意了，一套详尽的衣着描述早已登记在案。

「接下来请告诉我，威廉斯先生，那位太太出示了什么证明身份的东西，您又做了哪些查证来核实她的说辞。她的护照不是丢了吗？」

===Original===

“Yes, I told you how, or rather I told you what she said about it. She gave me her card, and showed me the envelopes of several letters addressed to her at Pittsburg. She also showed me some photographs of groups in which she appeared which had been taken on board the *Olympic*, as well as a dinner menu dated for the third day out. She explained that her return ticket had been stolen with the passport, so that she could not let me see it.”

“Not very conclusive, I’m afraid,” French commented. “All that evidence might have been faked.”

“I quite see that, and saw it at the time,” declared the moneylender. “But I did not rest there. I applied to Dashford’s, you know, the private inquiry people. I asked them to cable their agents in Pittsburg for a description of Mrs. Root, and to know if she had left for England on the *Olympic*. There is the reply.”

===Chinese===

「是的，经过我告诉过您——不如说，她是怎么说的，我告诉过您。她给了我名片，又给我看了几封信的信封，都是寄到匹兹堡给她本人的。她还给我看了几张合拍的照片，里头都有她，都是在*奥林匹克号*船上拍的，另有一张出海第三天的晚餐菜单。她解释说，回程票同护照一道被偷了，所以拿不出来给我看。」

「只怕算不得什么铁证。」弗伦奇评道。「那些凭据全都可能造假。」

「这我看得明白，当时就看明白了。」放贷人声称。「可我并没有到此为止。我去求助了达什福德公司（Dashford's）——您知道，就是那家私家侦探行。我请他们打电报给他们在匹兹堡的代理人，要一份鲁特夫人容貌的描述，并问明她是否已乘*奥林匹克号*动身来英。喏，这就是回电。」

===Original===

He took a paper from a file and handed it across. It was headed, “J. T. Dashford & Co., Private Inquiry Agents,” and read:

> “Dear Sir,
> **“Mrs. Chauncey S. Root.**
> “In reply to your inquiry of yesterday, we beg to inform you that we have cabled our agents in Pittsburg on the matter in question, and have received the following reply:
> “ ‘Chauncey S. Root, partner local steel firm, wealthy, wife handsome, height middle, hair dark, complexion pale, face oval, mouth small, manner bright and attractive. Left for Europe by *Olympic*. Family OK.’
> “We trust this information will meet your requirements.
> “Yours faithfully,
> “J. T. Dashford & Co.,
> “M.S.”

French whistled thoughtfully.

“That seems right enough,” he said slowly. “I know something about Dashford’s people, and they are reliable enough about a thing like this. It’s beginning to look like impersonation.”

===Chinese===

他从文件夹里抽出一张纸递过来。纸头印着「J·T·达什福德公司（J. T. Dashford & Co.）私家侦探事务所」，内容如下：

> 「敬启者：
> **「昌西·S·鲁特夫人。」**
> 「昨日垂询敬悉。兹奉告：我等已就此事电询我方驻匹兹堡代理人，接获复电如下：
> 「『昌西·S·鲁特，本地钢铁公司合伙人，富有。妻貌美，中等身材，深色头发，面色苍白，鹅蛋脸，小口，举止明快可亲。已乘*奥林匹克号*赴欧。家中安好。』
> 「谨此奉达，尚祈合用。
> 「敬上，
> 「J·T·达什福德公司，
> 「M.S.」

弗伦奇若有所思地打了个呼哨。

「看来是够确实的了。」他缓缓说道。「达什福德手下的人我略知一二，办这类事情他们靠得住。这桩案子，开始像是冒名顶替了。」

===Original===

“Ah,” Mr. Williams ejaculated. “Impersonation! I hadn’t thought of that.” He paused in his turn, then continued, “But yet I don’t see how it could be. I didn’t stop with an application to Dashford’s. I rang up the White Star offices, and they told me there that Mrs. Root had actually made the journey. I also rang up the Savoy, and they told me there that she had arrived at the hour she had told me, with trunks bearing *Olympic* labels. Finally, to make the matter, as I thought, sure, I phoned the Southampton police and found out from them that the story of the stolen despatch case was true. It had happened just as Mrs. Root described. When I got all this information I felt absolutely satisfied.”

“I’m not surprised at that, sir,” French admitted. “It would have satisfied most people. You see, it’s quite different with us now, because our suspicions have been aroused. There was nothing in the circumstances of this lady’s call to make you doubt her story. I quite sympathise with you, though I’m afraid that doesn’t help the situation much.﻿ ﻿… But you see now, of course, that none of the information you collected is really conclusive. I have no doubt that there is a Mrs. Chauncey S. Root of Pittsburg who travelled to Europe in the *Olympic*, and that, generally speaking, she resembles your friend, but I very gravely doubt that she was the lady who negotiated the loan. You see, the real identifications, the passport, the return ticket, on which her name would be inscribed, were missing. Moreover, she refused to allow Mr. Root to be consulted. No, I think we may take it that the woman who came here was not Mrs. Root. But, on the other hand, she must either have been acquainted with Mrs. Root personally or have known a thundering lot about her. How does that strike you, sir?”

“It sounds right, it certainly sounds right, Inspector. I fear it must be as you say. But if so, what chance is there of getting back my money?”

French shook his head.

===Chinese===

「啊，」威廉斯先生失声道。「冒名顶替！这一层我可没有想到。」他也顿了一顿，又继续道：「可是我又不明白，怎么可能是冒名。我并没有止步于求达什福德公司查询。我还打电话给白星公司（White Star）的办事处，他们那边告诉我，鲁特夫人确实作过这趟航行。我又打电话到萨伏伊饭店，他们那边说，她到达的钟点正如她对我讲的，行李上还贴着*奥林匹克号*的标签。末了，为把这事——照我当时的想法——办得万无一失，我又挂电话给南安普敦警方，从他们那里查明，小公文箱失窃一节属实，事情正如鲁特夫人所讲的那样发生过。得了这一应凭证，我便觉得十足地放心了。」

「这我一点不奇怪，先生。」弗伦奇承认道。「换了大多数人也会放心的。您瞧，如今我们大不相同了，因为我们的疑心已经起来了。这位太太来访的种种情形里，原没有什么教您怀疑她的说辞。我十分体谅您——只怕这于事无补。……可是您现在自然看出来了：您收集的那些情况，没有一样真正算得结论性的凭据。我毫不怀疑，匹兹堡确有一位昌西·S·鲁特夫人，也确曾乘*奥林匹克号*来欧；大体而论，她同您那位主顾形貌相仿。可是她是否就是商谈借款的那位太太，我却疑心得很。您想，真正的身份凭据——护照、回程票，上面本来都会写着她的名字——偏偏一概缺席。再者，她又拒不让人去请教鲁特先生。不，依我看，到这儿来的那个女人，多半不是鲁特夫人。可话又说回来，她要么同鲁特夫人本人相识，要么就得对她知之甚详。这话您听来如何，先生？」

「听着在理，一定在理，探长。只怕事实只能如您所说。可若是这样，我的钱还有几分指望追回来呢？」

弗伦奇摇了摇头。

===Original===

“I’m afraid the prospects are not very rosy,” he admitted. “But you never know. We’ll try to get our hands on the woman, of course, and we may find she has not spent the money. Now, sir, if there is nothing more that you can tell me, I think I shall get along to the Savoy and to those shops where she paid the cheques.”

Inspector French walked slowly down Cockspur Street, his brain bemused by this unexpected development. The impersonation of Mrs. Root was easy﻿—or, at least, comparatively easy﻿—to understand. He could see that it would present no serious difficulties to a resourceful woman, though the application to the Southampton police was certainly staggering. But what he could not form the slightest idea of was how this woman could possibly have got hold of Mr. Duke’s diamonds. The impersonation must have actually been arranged *before* the robbery took place, and if this were so, it pointed to a much more far-reaching crime than he had had any conception of. And there must have been more than one in it, too﻿—unless this mysterious woman had actually committed the murder, which he found hard to believe. He smiled with satisfaction as he thought of the vistas of possible information which were opening out before him, and by which he might hope to retrieve the loss of prestige which he had suffered.

Suddenly he thought of Miss Duke. Was there a connection between her and this mysterious woman? Was Mrs. Root the woman in the waterproof? Was she Miss Duke herself? Here were far-reaching questions. As he considered them, he saw that his work for the next few days was cut out for him.

===Chinese===

「只怕前景不算乐观。」他承认道。「不过世事难说。我们当然要设法抓住那个女人，说不定还能发现钱不曾花掉。好了，先生，您要是再没有什么可告诉我的，我想我就该到萨伏伊饭店和那几家兑付支票的铺子走一趟了。」

弗伦奇探长缓步走下科克斯珀街，脑子被这桩意外的进展搅得昏昏沉沉。冒充鲁特夫人一事，倒还容易理解——至少比较起来算容易。他看得出，对一个机变百出的女人来说，这未必构成多大的难关，虽说南安普敦警方那一关竟也查得实有其事，委实叫人咋舌。可他丝毫理不出头绪的是：这个女人究竟从哪里弄到了杜克先生的钻石？这冒名顶替，必定是在劫案发生*之前*便已布置停当；倘若如此，它指向的就是一桩远比他此前所设想的更加深广的罪案。而且其中必定不止一个人——除非这个神秘女人当真亲手行凶，而这他很难相信。他想到眼前正一道道展开的可供追索的门径，想到凭这些或许能挽回自己已折损的声望，不由得满意地笑了。

突然，他想起了杜克小姐。她同这个神秘女人之间，有没有什么牵连？鲁特夫人莫非就是那个穿雨衣的女人？她莫非就是杜克小姐本人？这些问题，个个牵连深远。他一路思量，明白今后几天的活计，已然排定在他面前了。

## I Murder! / 一、凶案

===Original===
The back streets surrounding Hatton Garden, in the City of London, do not form at the best of times a cheerful or inspiring prospect. Narrow and mean, and flanked with ugly, sordid-looking buildings grimy from exposure to the smoke and fogs of the town and drab from the want of fresh paint, they can hardly fail to strike discouragement into the heart of anyone eager for the uplift of our twentieth century civilisation.

But if on a day of cheerful sunshine the outlook is thus melancholy, it was vastly more so at ten o’clock on a certain dreary evening in mid-November. A watery moon, only partially visible through a damp mist, lit up pallidly the squalid, shuttered fronts of the houses. The air was cold and raw, and the pavements showed dark from a fine rain which had fallen some time earlier, but which had now ceased. Few were abroad, and no one whose business permitted it remained out of doors.

Huckley Street, one of the narrowest and least inviting in the district, was, indeed, deserted save for a single figure. Though the higher and more ethical side of civilisation was not obtrusive, it was by no means absent. The figure represented Law and Order, in short, it was that of a policeman on his beat.

===Chinese===
伦敦城（City of London）哈顿花园（Hatton Garden）四周的后街，即便在光景最好的时节，也谈不上赏心悦目、令人振奋。街道又窄又寒酸，两侧尽是丑陋邋遢的房屋，叫城里的煤烟浊雾熏得污黑，又因久无新漆而灰扑扑的。凡热切盼望我们二十世纪文明蒸蒸日上的人，见了这景象，很难不心头一凉。

若说晴日之下这景致已如此凄清，那么到了十一月中旬某个阴郁夜晚的十点钟，便更是不堪了。一轮水蒙蒙的月亮隔着湿雾半隐半现，惨淡地照着那些上了门板、肮脏破败的临街门面。天气阴冷砭骨，先前落过一阵细雨，此刻虽已停歇，人行道上还留着湿黑的一层。街上行人寥寥，但凡事情容许，没有一个肯留在门外。

赫克利街（Huckley Street）算是这一带最窄、最不招人待见的街道之一，此刻确确实实空无一人，只剩一条孤零零的身影。不过，文明中较高的、更讲道德的那一面虽不显眼，却也绝非缺席。这条身影代表的正是法律与秩序——简单说，那是个巡夜的警察。

===Original===
Constable James Alcorn moved slowly forward, glancing mechanically but with practised eye over the shuttered windows of the shops and the closed doors of the offices and warehouses in his purview. He was not imaginative, the constable, or he would have rebelled even more strongly than he did against the weariness and monotony of his job. A dog’s life, this of night patrol in the City, he thought, as he stopped at a cross roads, and looked down each one in turn of the four dingy and deserted lanes which radiated from the intersection. How deadly depressing it all was! Nothing ever doing! Nothing to give a man a chance! In the daytime it was not so bad, when the streets were alive and fellow creatures were to be seen, if not spoken to, but at night when there was no one to watch, and nothing to be done but wait endlessly for the opportunity which never came, it was a thankless task. He was fed up!

But though he didn’t know it, his chance was at hand. He had passed through Charles Street and had turned into Hatton Garden itself, when suddenly a door swung open a little way down the street, and a young man ran wildly out into the night.

===Chinese===
巡警詹姆斯·奥尔康（Constable James Alcorn）缓步前行，机械却不失老练地扫过辖区内店铺上了门板的橱窗，和写字楼、货栈紧闭的大门。这位巡警不是个富于想象力的人，否则，对这份差事的疲惫与单调，他只怕还要生出远不止于此的怨怼。在伦敦城值夜巡，真是狗过的日子——他这样想着，在一处十字路口停下脚步，把由此辐射开去的四条昏暗冷清的巷子挨条望过去。这一切多么死气沉沉、多么熬人啊！永远没有事干！永远没有一星半点轮得着人的机会！白天倒还不算糟：街上有人气，好歹看得见同类，虽说搭不上话；可到了夜里，无人可看，无事可为，只有没完没了地等一个永远等不来的机会，真真是桩吃力不讨好的差事。他受够了！

可他虽然不知道，他的机会已经到了跟前。他刚穿过查尔斯街（Charles Street），拐进哈顿花园正街，忽然间，前头不远处一扇门豁然打开，一个年轻人没命地冲进了夜色之中。

===Original===
The door was directly under a street lamp, and Alcorn could see that the youth’s features were frozen into an expression of horror and alarm. He hovered for a moment irresolute, then, seeing the constable, made for him at a run.

“Officer!” he shouted. “Come here quickly. There’s something wrong!”

Alcorn, his depression gone, hurried to meet him.

“What is it?” he queried. “What’s the matter?”

“Murder, I’m afraid,” the other cried. “Up in the office. Come and see.”

===Chinese===
那扇门正在一盏街灯底下，奥尔康看得分明：年轻人的五官已僵成一副惊骇惶恐的神情。他迟疑地打了个旋，随即望见巡警，便一路朝他奔来。

「警官！」他喊道，「快过来。出乱子了！」

奥尔康满腹的郁闷霎时无影无踪，急忙迎上前去。

「什么事？」他问，「出了什么乱子？」

「恐怕是凶杀。」对方叫道，「就在楼上办公室。来看吧。」

===Original===
The door from which the young man had emerged stood open, and they hastened thither. It gave on a staircase upon which the electric light was turned on. The young man raced up and passed through a door on the first landing. Alcorn, following, found himself in an office containing three or four desks. A further door leading to an inner room stood open, and to this the young man pointed.

“In there,” he directed; “in the Chief’s room.”

Here also the light was on, and as Alcorn passed in, he saw that he was indeed in the presence of tragedy, and he stood for a moment motionless, taking in his surroundings.

The room was small, but well proportioned. Near the window stood a roll-top desk of old-fashioned design. A leather-lined clients’ armchair was close by, with behind it a well-filled bookcase. In the fireplace the remains of a fire still glowed red. A table littered with books and papers and a large Milner safe completed the furniture. The doors of this safe were open.

===Chinese===
年轻人跑出来的那扇门还敞着，两人急忙赶了过去。门内是一道楼梯，电灯已亮。年轻人一口气奔上去，穿过二楼平台上的一扇门。奥尔康跟进去，发觉自己置身一间办公室，里头摆着三四张办公桌。再往里另有一扇门通向内室，也敞开着，年轻人正指着那儿。

「在里头。」他指路道，「老板的房间里。」

这间屋里灯也亮着。奥尔康一跨进门，便看出自己当真面对着一桩惨祸。他怔立片刻，一动不动，把四下打量了一遍。

房间不大，却匀称合度。窗边立着一张老式卷盖办公桌，近旁摆着一把衬皮的客椅，椅后是一座塞得满满的书柜。壁炉里残火未熄，兀自泛着红光。一张堆满书籍文件的桌子，外加一只米尔纳（Milner）牌大保险柜，便凑齐了全部家什。保险柜的柜门敞开着。

===Original===
Alcorn mechanically noted these details, but it was not on them that his attention was first concentrated. Before the safe lay the body of a man, hunched forward in a heap, as if he had collapsed when stooping to take something out. Though the face was hidden, there was that in the attitude which left no doubt that he was dead. And the cause of death was equally obvious. On the back of the bald head, just above the fringe of white hair, was an ugly wound, as if from a blow of some blunt but heavy weapon.

With an oath, Alcorn stepped forward and touched the cheek.

“Cold,” he exclaimed. “He must have been dead some time. When did you find him?”

“Just now,” the young man answered. “I came in for a book, and found him lying there. I ran for help at once.”

The constable nodded.

===Chinese===
这些细情奥尔康都下意识地一一记下，可最先攫住他注意的并不是它们。保险柜前躺着一个男人，身子向前蜷作一堆，仿佛俯身取物时骤然瘫倒下去的。脸虽看不见，单凭那副姿势便可断定：人已经死了。死因也同样一目了然：光秃秃的后脑勺上、恰在白发发缘的上方，有一道狰狞的创口，像是挨了什么钝而沉重的家伙一击。

奥尔康咒骂了一声，上前碰了碰死者的面颊。

「凉的。」他失声道，「人死了有一阵子了。你什么时候发现他的？」

「就刚才。」年轻人答道，「我回来取一本书，进门就见他躺在那儿。我立刻跑出来喊人了。」

巡警点了点头。

===Original===
“We’d best have a doctor anyway,” he decided. A telephone stood on the top of the desk, and he called up his headquarters, asking that an officer and a doctor be sent at once. Then he turned to his companion.

“Now, sir, what’s all this about? Who are you, and how do you come to be here?”

The young man, though obviously agitated and ill at ease, answered collectedly enough.

“My name is Orchard, William Orchard, and I am a clerk in this office﻿—Duke & Peabody’s, diamond merchants. As I have just said, I called in for a book I had forgotten, and I found﻿—what you see.”

“And what did you do?”

===Chinese===
「不管怎么说，先得请个医生。」他拿定主意。桌台上有一部电话，他摇通本部，请他们立刻派一名警官和一名医生过来。然后他转向同伴。

「好了，先生，这究竟是怎么回事？你是谁，又怎么会在这儿？」

年轻人虽然显然心绪激荡、局促不安，回答得倒还够镇定。

「我叫奥查德（Orchard），威廉·奥查德（William Orchard），是这家商行的职员——杜克与皮博迪公司（Duke & Peabody），钻石商行。我刚才说了，我是回来取一本落下的书，结果就看见了——您眼前这情形。」

「那么你做了些什么？」

===Original===
“Do? I did what anyone else would have done in the same circumstances. I looked to see if Mr. Gething was dead, and when I saw he was I didn’t touch the body, but ran for help. You were the first person I saw.”

“Mr. Gething?” the constable repeated sharply. “Then you know the dead man?”

“Yes. It is Mr. Gething, our head clerk.”

“What about the safe? Is there anything missing from that?”

“I don’t know,” the young man answered. “I believe there were a lot of diamonds in it, but I don’t know what amount, and I’ve not looked what’s there now.”

“Who would know about it?”

===Chinese===
「做什么？换了谁处在同样境地也会那么做。我先看了看格辛（Gething）先生是不是还活着，一看确实死了，我便没碰尸首，径直跑出去喊人。您是我见到的头一个人。」

「格辛先生？」巡警敏锐地追问，「这么说你认识死者？」

「认识。死者正是格辛先生，我们的首席职员。」

「保险柜怎么说？里头短了什么没有？」

「这我不知道。」年轻人答道，「我记得里头放着好些钻石，可数目我不清楚，如今剩了什么我也没看。」

「那谁会知道？」

===Original===
“I don’t suppose anyone but Mr. Duke, now Mr. Gething’s dead. He’s the chief, the only partner I’ve ever seen.”

Constable Alcorn paused, evidently at a loss as to his next move. Finally, following precedent, he took a somewhat dog’s-eared notebook from his pocket, and with a stumpy pencil began to note the particulars he had gleaned.

“Gething, you say the dead man’s name was? What was his first name?”

“Charles.”

“Charles Gething, deceased,” the constable repeated presently, evidently reading his entry. “Yes. And his address?”

===Chinese===
「如今格辛先生一死，我看除了杜克先生（Mr. Duke），没人说得上了。他是头儿，也是我见过的唯一的合伙人。」

奥尔康巡警顿了顿，显是拿不准下一步该怎么办。末了，他照老规矩，从口袋里摸出一本卷了角的记事簿，拿一截秃铅笔头，把问来的种种要点一一记下。

「格辛——你说死者叫这个姓？他的名字是什么？」

「查尔斯（Charles）。」

「查尔斯·格辛，已故。」巡警过了一会儿复述道，显然在念自己的记录。「好。他住哪儿？」

===Original===
“12 Monkton Street, Fulham.”

“Twelve﻿—Monkton﻿—Street﻿—Fulham. Yes. And your name is William Orchard?”

Slowly the tedious catechism proceeded. The two men formed a contrast. Alcorn calm and matter of fact, though breathing heavily from the effort of writing, was concerned only with making a satisfactory statement for his superior. His informant, on the other hand, was quivering with suppressed excitement, and acutely conscious of the silent and motionless form on the floor. Poor old Gething! A kindly old fellow, if ever there was one! It seemed a shame to let his body lie there in that shapeless heap, without showing even the respect of covering the injured head with a handkerchief. But the matter was out of his hands. The police would follow their own methods, and he, Orchard, could not interfere.

===Chinese===
「富勒姆（Fulham）蒙克顿街（Monkton Street）12 号。」

「十二号——蒙克顿街——富勒姆。好。你叫威廉·奥查德，对吧？」

这套冗长乏味的一问一答慢慢问了下来。两个人恰成对照。奥尔康沉着务实，只是写字费劲，呼吸粗重，一门心思只想给上司写出一份交代得过去的呈报；报信的那位却压抑着激动，浑身微颤，时时刻刻意识到地板上那具悄无声息、一动不动的躯体。可怜的老格辛！天底下再找不出比他更和善的好心人了！让他那样不成体统地蜷作一堆躺在那儿，连拿块手帕把他那受伤的脑袋遮一遮的敬意都不给，实在说不过去。可这事已不由他做主了。警察自有警察的章程，他奥查德插不上手。

===Original===
Some ten minutes passed of question, answer, and laborious caligraphy, then voices and steps were heard on the stairs, and four men entered the room.

“What’s all this, Alcorn?” cried the first, a stout, clean-shaven man with the obvious stamp of authority, in the same phrase that his subordinate had used to the clerk, Orchard. He had stepped just inside the door, and stood looking sharply round the room, his glance passing from the constable to the body, to the open safe, with inimical interest to the young clerk, and back again to Alcorn.

The constable stiffened to attention, and replied in a stolid, unemotional tone, as if reciting formal evidence in court.

“I was on my beat, sir, and at about ten-fifteen was just turning the corner from Charles Street into Hatton Garden, when I observed this young man,” he indicated Orchard with a gesture, “run out of the door of this house. He called me that there was something wrong up here, and I came up to see, and found that body lying as you see it. Nothing has been touched, but I have got some information here for you.” He held up the notebook.

===Chinese===
一问、一答、再吃力地写，如此过了约莫十分钟，楼梯上忽然响起人声和脚步声，四个人走进了房间。

「这是怎么回事，奥尔康？」走在头里的人喝道。那是个矮胖结实、脸刮得干干净净的汉子，一望便知身负权柄；这话问得，同他手下方才问职员奥查德的如出一辙。他刚跨进门槛便站住，目光犀利地扫视全屋：从巡警扫到尸首，扫到敞开的保险柜，又带着几分敌意盯了盯年轻职员，再回到奥尔康身上。

巡警挺身立正，用平板而毫无起伏的声调回答，仿佛在法庭上宣读正式证词。

「报告长官，我正在自己的段上巡逻。大约十点一刻，我刚从查尔斯街拐进哈顿花园，就看见这位年轻人，」他朝奥查德一摆手，「从这幢房子里跑出来。他喊我说楼上出了事，我便上来看看，发现那具尸首就躺在您眼前的位置。现场一概未曾触动，不过我这里记下一些情况，可供长官过目。」他举了举记事簿。

===Original===
The newcomer nodded and turned to one of his companions, a tall man with the unmistakable stamp of the medical practitioner.

“If you can satisfy yourself the man’s dead, Doctor, I don’t think we shall disturb the body in the meantime. It’ll probably be a case for the Yard, and if so we’ll leave everything for whoever they send.”

The doctor crossed the room and knelt by the remains.

“He’s dead all right,” he announced, “and not so long ago either. If I could turn the body over I could tell you more about that. But I’ll leave it if you like.”

“Yes, leave it for the moment, if you please. Now, Alcorn, what else do you know?”

===Chinese===
新来的警官点了点头，转向随行的一人——高个子，一望便知是行医的。

「医生，你只消验明这人确已身亡，其余的我们暂且不必惊动尸身。这案子多半要归苏格兰场管；果真如此，一切都原样留给他们的来人。」

医生穿过房间，在遗体旁跪下。

「人是死透了。」他宣布道，「而且死了没多久。要是能翻动尸身，我还能说得更多。不过你们要是不愿动，我就不动。」

「对，暂且别动，有劳。好了，奥尔康，你还知道些什么？」

===Original===
A few seconds sufficed to put the constable’s information at his superior’s disposal. The latter turned to the doctor.

“There’s more than murder here, Dr. Jordan, I’ll be bound. That safe is the key to the affair. Thank the Lord, it’ll be a job for the Yard. I shall phone them now, and there should be a man here in half an hour. Sorry, Doctor, but I’m afraid you’ll have to wait.” He turned to Orchard. “You’ll have to wait, too, young man, but the Yard inspector probably won’t keep you long. Now, what about this old man’s family? Was he married?”

“Yes, but his wife is an invalid, bedridden. He has two daughters. One lives at home and keeps house, the other is married and lives somewhere in town.”

“We shall have to send round word. You go, Carson.” He turned to one of the two other members of his quartet, constables in uniform. “Don’t tell the old lady. If the daughter’s not there, wait until she comes in. And put yourself at her disposal. If she wants her sister sent for, you go. You, Jackson, go down to the front door and let the Yard man up. Alcorn, remain here.” These dispositions made, he rang up the Yard and delivered his message, then turned once more to the young clerk.

===Chinese===
不消几句话的工夫，巡警便把自己掌握的情况尽数交给了上司。后者转向医生。

「乔丹医生（Dr. Jordan），我敢担保，这里头光凶杀还打不住，那只保险柜才是全案的枢纽。谢天谢地，这差事归苏格兰场。我这就打电话去，半个钟头内该有人到。对不住，医生，恐怕你得等一等。」他又转向奥查德。「你也得等着，年轻人，不过苏格兰场的探长多半不会让你久等。对了，这位老人家里还有什么人？他成过家吗？」

「成过，可他太太常年卧病在床。他有两个女儿：一个住在家里操持家务，另一个嫁了人，住在城里什么地方。」

「总得去送个信儿。卡森（Carson），你去。」他转向一行四人中另外两名穿制服的警员之一。「别告诉老太太。小姐要是不在，就等她回来。一切听她调遣。她若要人去接她那位出嫁的姊妹，也还是你去。杰克逊（Jackson），你下楼到前门去，苏格兰场的人来了领他上来。奥尔康，你守在这儿。」部署已定，他摇通苏格兰场的电话交代了一番，然后再度转向年轻职员。

===Original===
“You say, Mr. Orchard, that no one could tell what, if anything, is missing from the safe, except Mr. Duke, the sole active partner. We ought to have Mr. Duke here at once. Is he on the phone?”

“Gerard, 1417B,” Orchard answered promptly. The young man’s agitation had somewhat subsided, and he was following with interest the actions of the police, and admiring the confident, competent way in which they had taken charge.

The official once again took down the receiver from the top of the desk, and put through the call. “Is Mr. Duke there?﻿ ﻿… Yes, say a superintendent of police.” There was a short silence, and then the man went on. “Is that Mr. Duke?﻿ ﻿… I’m speaking from your office in Hatton Garden. I’m sorry, sir, to tell you that a tragedy has taken place here. Your chief clerk, Mr. Gething, is dead.﻿ ﻿… Yes, sir. He’s lying in your private office here, and the circumstances point to murder. The safe is standing open, and﻿—Yes, sir, I’m afraid so﻿—I don’t know, of course, about the contents.﻿ ﻿… No, but you couldn’t tell from that.﻿ ﻿… I was going to suggest that you come down at once. I’ve phoned Scotland Yard for a man.﻿ ﻿… Very good, sir, we shall be here when you come.” He replaced the receiver and turned to the others.

“Mr. Duke is coming down at once. There is no use in our standing here. Come to the outer office and we’ll find ourselves chairs.”

===Chinese===
「奥查德先生，照你的说法，保险柜里到底短了什么——就算真短了——除了唯一管事的合伙人杜克先生，谁也说不上来。我们得马上把杜克先生请到场。他家里装着电话吗？」

「杰拉德（Gerard）局 1417B。」奥查德应声便答。年轻人的激动已平复了些，正饶有兴味地盯着警方的一举一动，暗暗佩服他们接手现场时那份从容干练。

那位警官又从桌台上拿起听筒，接通了电话。「杜克先生在家吗？……在，就说是一位警司（Superintendent）找他。」短暂的静默过后，他接着说道。「您是杜克先生吗？……我现在是在您哈顿花园的办公室里通话。很抱歉，先生，这里出了一件惨事。您的首席职员格辛先生死了。……是，先生。他就躺在您这间私人办公室里，种种情形都指向凶杀。保险柜敞着，还有——是，先生，恐怕如此——至于柜内物件，我自然说不上。……不，这个您可想不出来。……我正要建议您立刻赶来。我已经打电话到苏格兰场叫人了。……很好，先生，您来的时候我们都在。」他挂上听筒，转向众人。

「杜克先生马上就到。咱们杵在这儿也没用。到外间办公室去，各自找把椅子坐。」

===Original===
It was cold in the general office, the fire evidently having been out for some time, but they sat down there to wait, the Superintendent pointing out that the furniture in the other room must not be touched. Of the four, only the Superintendent seemed at ease and self-satisfied. Orchard was visibly nervous and apprehensive and fidgeted restlessly, Constable Alcorn, slightly embarrassed by the society in which he found himself, sat rigidly on the edge of his chair staring straight in front of him, while the doctor was frankly bored and anxious to get home. Conversation languished, though spasmodic attempts were made by the Superintendent to keep it going, and none of the quartet was sorry when the sound of footsteps on the stairs created a diversion.

Of the three men who entered the room, two, carrying black leather cases, were obviously police constables in plain clothes. The third was a stout man in tweeds, rather under middle height, with a clean-shaven, good-humoured face and dark blue eyes which, though keen, twinkled as if at some perennially fresh private joke. His air was easygoing and leisurely, and he looked the type of man who could enjoy a good dinner and a good smoke-room story to follow.

“Ah, Superintendent, how are you?” he exclaimed, holding out his hand cordially. “It’s some time since we met. Not since that little episode in the Limehouse hairdresser’s. That was a nasty business. And now you’ve some other scheme for keeping a poor man from his hard-earned rest, eh?”

===Chinese===
大办公室里冷冰冰的，炉火显然熄了许久，但他们还是坐下来等候，警司特别指出，里屋的家什一概不得挪动。四人之中，唯有警司显得安然自得、踌躇满志。奥查德分明紧张惶惑，烦躁得坐立不安；奥尔康巡警跻身这等人物中间，颇觉局促，直挺挺地坐在椅子边上，两眼直望前方；医生则干脆百无聊赖，一心只想回家。谈话有一搭没一搭，警司偶尔撑着说上两句，也提不起兴致。待到楼梯上响起脚步声，总算来了个解闷的岔子，四人无不暗暗称幸。

进屋来的三个人里，有两个提着黑皮箱，一望便知是便衣警员。第三个是个穿花呢衣裳的胖子，个头在中等以下，脸刮得精光，一团和气，一双深蓝色的眼睛虽不失锐利，却总忽闪忽闪的，仿佛惦着一桩永远新鲜的自家笑话。他神态随和悠闲，看那样子，是个既能受用一顿好饭、饭后又能接着受用一段吸烟室好故事的人。

「啊，警司，你好哇？」他热络地伸出手来，大声招呼。「咱们有些日子没见了。打莱姆豪斯（Limehouse）那家理发店里的小插曲之后，就没照过面。那可是件够呛的差事。这一回，你又有什么新章程，要生生耽误一个可怜人去睡他辛苦挣来的觉，啊？」

===Original===
The Superintendent seemed to find the other’s easy familiarity out of place.

“Good evening, Inspector,” he answered with official abruptness. “You know Dr. Jordan?﻿—Inspector French of the C.I.D. And this is Mr. Orchard, a clerk in this office, who discovered the crime.”

Inspector French greeted them genially. Behind his back at the Yard they called him “Soapy Joe” because of the reliance he placed on the suavity of his manners. “I know your name, of course, Doctor, but I don’t think we have ever met. Pleased to make your acquaintance, Mr. Orchard.” He subsided into a chair and went on: “Perhaps, Superintendent, you would just give me a hint of what this is all about before we go any further.”

The facts already learned were soon recited. French listened carefully, and annexing the constable’s notebook, complimented that worthy on his industry. “Well,” he beamed on them, “I suppose we’d better have a look round inside before Mr. Duke turns up.”

===Chinese===
警司似乎觉得对方这股熟不拘礼的劲儿有些不合时宜。

「晚上好，探长。」他以官腔的生硬还礼。「你认识乔丹医生吧？——这位是刑事调查部（C.I.D.）的弗伦奇探长。这位是奥查德先生，本行的职员，凶案就是他发现的。」

弗伦奇探长热络地向二人致意。在苏格兰场，背地里人们都叫他「肥皂乔（Soapy Joe）」，因为他行事全凭那副温文圆滑的做派。「医生，您的名字我自然知道，只是咱们怕还不曾谋面。奥查德先生，幸会。」他一屁股坐进椅子，接着说道：「警司，趁我们还没往下进行，可否先给我透个底，这案子究竟是怎么回事？」

已经查明的情形很快陈述完毕。弗伦奇仔细听着，顺手把巡警的记事簿收了过来，还夸了那位好警员几句，说他勤勉尽职。「好吧，」他笑吟吟地环视众人，「我看咱们最好趁杜克先生没到，先进里头转一圈。」

===Original===
The party moved to the inner room, where French, his hands in his pockets, stood motionless for some minutes, surveying the scene.

“Nothing has been touched, of course?” he asked.

“Nothing. From what they tell me, both Mr. Orchard and Constable Alcorn have been most circumspect.”

“Excellent; then we may go ahead. Get your camera rigged, Giles, and take the usual photos. I think, gentlemen, we may wait in the other room until the photographs are taken. It won’t be long.”

===Chinese===
一行人移步内室。弗伦奇双手插在口袋里，一动不动站了好几分钟，把现场整个收在眼里。

「东西当然都没动过？」

「什么都没动。据他们讲，奥查德先生和奥尔康巡警两位都谨慎得很，分毫未犯。」

「好极了，那就可以动手了。贾尔斯（Giles），把相机架起来，照老规矩拍照。诸位先生，拍照这会儿，我们先到那屋去等一等。耽搁不了多久。」

===Original===
Though French had tactfully bowed his companions out, he did not himself follow them, but kept prowling about the inner office, closely inspecting its contents, though touching nothing. In a few minutes the camera was ready, and a number of flashlight photographs were taken of the body, the safe, every part of both offices, and even the stairs and hall. In the amazing way in which tales of disaster travel, news of the crime had already leaked out, and a small crowd of the curious hung, open-mouthed, about the door.

Scarcely had the camera been put away, when the proceedings were interrupted by a fresh arrival. Hurried steps were heard ascending the stairs, and a tall, thin, extremely well-dressed old gentleman entered the room. Though evidently on the wrong side of sixty, he was still a handsome man, with strong, well-formed features, white hair, and a good carriage. Under normal circumstances he would have presented a dignified and kindly appearance, but now his face was drawn into an expression of horror and distress, and his hasty movements also betokened his anxiety. On seeing so many strangers, he hesitated. The Inspector stepped forward.

“Mr. Duke, sir? I am Inspector French of the Criminal Investigation Department of New Scotland Yard. I very much regret to confirm the news which you have already heard, that your head clerk, Mr. Gething, has been murdered, and I fear also that your safe may have been burgled.”

===Chinese===
弗伦奇虽然有眼色，把众人都客气地送了出去，自己却没跟着走，只在内室里来回逡巡，把屋里的东西细细察看，却一件不碰。几分钟后相机架好，镁光对着尸首、保险柜、两间办公室的每一处，连楼梯和门厅都拍了照。灾祸的消息传起来总有股匪夷所思的快劲儿，凶案的风声竟已走漏出去，一小群看热闹的张着嘴，聚在门口不肯散。

相机刚一收起，一阵新的来客又打断了进行中的事项。楼梯上响起急促的脚步声，一位瘦高个、衣着一丝不苟的老绅士走进屋来。他分明已年过六旬，却依然仪表堂堂：五官刚毅端正，一头白发，腰背挺直。若在寻常时候，他必是一副庄重慈祥的风度，可此刻他满面惊骇悲戚，行色仓皇，无处不透着焦灼。一见这么多生人，他迟疑了。探长迎上前来。

「您是杜克先生吧？我是新苏格兰场（New Scotland Yard）刑事调查部的弗伦奇探长。我非常遗憾地向您证实您已听到的消息：您的首席职员格辛先生遭人谋害，而且我担心，您的保险柜也失了窃。」

===Original===
It was evident that the old gentleman was experiencing strong emotion, but he controlled it and spoke quietly enough.

“This is terrible news, Inspector. I can hardly believe that poor old Gething is gone. I came at once when I heard. Tell me the details. Where did it happen?”

French pointed to the open door.

“In here, sir, in your private office. Everything is still exactly as it was found.”

Mr. Duke moved forward, then on seeing the body, stopped and gave a low cry of horror.

===Chinese===
老绅士显然正忍受着强烈的情绪，但他强自克制，说话还算平静。

「这是可怕的消息，探长。可怜的老格辛竟然去了，我几乎不敢相信。一得信我就立刻赶了来。请把经过讲给我听。事情出在哪里？」

弗伦奇指了指那扇敞开的门。

「就在这里面，先生，您的私人办公室。一切都还是发现时的原样。」

杜克先生举步上前，一眼看见尸首，登时站住，发出一声低低的惊呼。

===Original===
“Oh, poor old fellow!” he exclaimed. “It’s awful to see him lying there. *Awful!* I tell you, Inspector, I’ve lost a real friend, loyal and true and dependable. Can’t he be lifted up? I can’t bear to see him like that.” His gaze passed on to the safe. “And the safe! Merciful heavens, Inspector! Is anything gone? Tell me at once, I must know! It seems heartless to think of such a thing with that good old fellow lying there, but after all I’m only human.”

“I haven’t touched the safe, but we’ll do so directly,” the Inspector answered. “Was there much in it?”

“About three-and-thirty thousand pounds’ worth of diamonds were in that lower drawer, as well as a thousand in notes,” groaned the other. “Get the body moved, will you, and let us look.”

French whistled, then he turned to his men.

“Get that table cleared outside there, and lift the body on to it,” he ordered; then to the doctor he added, “Perhaps, Doctor, you could make your examination now?”

===Chinese===
「哦，可怜的老伙计！」他叫道，「眼看他这样躺在那儿，太惨了。*真是惨极了！*告诉你，探长，我失去了一位真正的朋友，忠诚、可靠、靠得住。不能把他抬起来吗？我不忍看他这副样子。」他的目光又移向保险柜。「还有那保险柜！老天爷，探长！丢了东西没有？快告诉我，我非知道不可！好老伙计躺在那儿，我却盘算这种事，未免太没心肝——可我到底只是个凡人。」

「保险柜我还没碰过，不过马上就开。」探长答道。「里头东西多吗？」

「下层抽屉里放着约莫三万三千镑的钻石，另外还有一千镑钞票。」对方呻吟道。「先把尸首抬开，劳驾，让我们瞧一瞧。」

弗伦奇打了个唿哨，随即转向手下。

「把外头那张桌子腾出来，把尸首抬上去。」他吩咐道；接着又对医生说：「医生，您这会儿可以验了吗？」

===Original===
The remains were lifted reverently and carried from the room. Mr. Duke turned impatiently to the safe, but the Inspector stopped him.

“A moment, sir, if you please. I am sorry to ask you to stretch your patience a little longer, but before you touch the safe I must test it for finger prints. You see the obvious necessity?”

“I would wait all night if it would help you to get on the track of the scoundrels who have done this,” the old gentleman answered grimly. “Go on in your own way. I can restrain myself.”

With a word of approval, Inspector French fetched one of the cases brought by his assistants, and producing little boxes of French chalk and of lampblack, he proceeded to dust over the smooth portions of the safe, using white powder on a dark background and vice versa. On blowing off the surplus powder, he pointed triumphantly to a number of finger prints, explaining that the moisture deposited from the skin held the powder, which otherwise dropped off. Most of the marks were blurred and useless, but a few showed clearly the little loops and whorls and ridges of thumbs and fingers.

“Of course,” French went on, “these may all be quite useless. They may be those of persons who had a perfect right to open the safe﻿—your own, for instance. But if they belong to the thief, if there was one, their importance may be incalculable. See here now, I can open this drawer without touching any of them.”

===Chinese===
遗体被肃然抬起，抬出了房间。杜克先生急煎煎地转身奔保险柜去，探长却拦住了他。

「请稍候片刻，先生。抱歉还要请您把耐心再多抻一抻——在您碰保险柜之前，我必须先在上面取指纹。这中间的必要，您自然看得出？」

「只要能帮你们追上那班干出这事的恶棍，等一整夜我也甘愿。」老绅士咬着牙答道。「按你们的路数办吧，我忍得住。」

弗伦奇探长赞许了一声，取来助手带来的皮箱中的一只，掏出几小盒滑石粉和灯烟黑粉，便往保险柜的平滑部位上扑粉：深底子用白粉，浅底子便反其道用黑粉。吹去浮粉，他得意地指着显出的若干指纹解释道：是皮肤沁出的潮气把粉吸住了，不然粉早掉了。大多数印迹模糊无用，可有几枚清清楚楚地显出拇指与手指上的箕形纹、斗形纹和一道道细嵴。

「当然，」弗伦奇接着说，「这些也可能全然无用。留下印子的，也许是名正言顺有权开这柜子的人——比方说您本人。可它们若是那窃贼留下的——倘若真有窃贼——那意义就无法估量了。瞧好了，我不碰其中任何一枚，就能把这抽屉打开。」

===Original===
Mr. Duke was clearly at the end of his patience, and he kept fidgeting about, clasping and unclasping his hands, and showing every sign of extreme impatience and uneasiness. As the drawer opened, he stepped forward and plunged in his hand.

“Gone!” he cried hoarsely. “They’re all gone! Thirty-three thousand pounds’ worth! Oh, my God! It means ruin.” He covered his face with his hands, then went on unsteadily. “I feared it, of course. I thought it must be the diamonds when the officer rang me up. I have been trying to face it ever since. I shouldn’t care for myself. It’s my daughter. To think of her exposed to want! But there. It is wicked of me to speak so who have only lost money, while poor old Gething has lost his life. Don’t mind me, Inspector. Carry on. What I want most now is to hear of the arrest of the murderer and thief. If there is anything I can do to help in that, command me.”

He stood, a little stooped and with haggard face, but dignified even in his grief. French in his pleasant, kindly way tried to reassure him.

===Chinese===
杜克先生显然忍到了头，不停地来回折腾，双手握了又松、松了又握，把极度的焦灼难安都写在了身上。抽屉一开，他抢上前去，一把将手插了进去。

「没了！」他声音嘶哑地喊道，「全没了！三万三千镑的货啊！哦，上帝！这是要倾家荡产啊。」他双手掩面，随即又颤颤地往下说：「这我自然早怕了。警官打电话来的时候，我就想多半是钻石。打那以后我一直逼着自己往这上头想。我自己倒不放在心上，是我女儿啊。想想她要落到缺衣少食的地步！可是——瞧我，只丢了几个钱的人竟这样抱怨，可怜的老格辛丢的却是性命，我这么说太罪过了。别管我，探长。你们只管查下去。如今我最想听见的，就是那凶手兼窃贼落网的消息。但凡有我能出力的地方，尽管差遣。」

他站在那里，微微伛偻着，面容枯槁，可纵在悲恸之中，仍不失尊严。弗伦奇以他那亲切和善的态度温言宽慰。

===Original===
“Now, you don’t need to give up heart, sir,” he advised. “Diamonds are not the easiest things to dispose of, and we’re right on to the loss at once. Before the thief can pass them on we shall have all the channels under observation. With any ordinary luck, you’ll get them back. They were not insured?”

“Part of them only. About nineteen thousand pounds’ worth were insured. It was my cursed folly that the rest were not. Gething advised it, but I had never lost anything, and I wanted to save the money. You understand our trade has been difficult since the war, and our profits were not the same as formerly. Every little has counted, and we have had to economise.”

“At worst, then, that is £14,000 gone?”

“If the insurance companies pay in full, that is all, besides the thousand in notes. But, Inspector, it is too much. To meet my share of the loss will beggar me.” He shook his head despondently. “But never mind my affairs in the meantime. Don’t, I beg of you, lose any time in getting after the criminal.”

===Chinese===
「喏，先生，您别灰心。」他劝道。「钻石可不是顶容易脱手的东西，失窃的事我们一上来就咬住了。窃贼还没来得及把货转手，我们就会把所有销路的口子都盯死。只要运气不离谱，您能把它们找回来。这批货没投保吧？」

「只保了一部分。投了保的大约一万九千镑，其余的没保——都怪我一时糊涂作下的孽。格辛劝过我，可我从来没失过贼，又想省下那笔保费。您知道，大战以来生意难做，利润不比从前，一个子儿都得算计，我们只好处处节省。」

「那么，往最坏处说，也就是损失 14,000 镑？」

「保险公司如数照赔的话，就这些，外加那一千镑钞票。可是探长，这数目太大了。要我凑出自己该担的亏空，我就得破产。」他灰心丧气地摇头。「不过眼下先别管我的事。求您千万莫要耽搁，立刻就去追那凶犯。」

===Original===
“You are right, sir. If, then, you will sit down there for a few minutes I’ll get rid of the others, and then I shall ask you for some information.”

The old gentleman dropped wearily into a chair while French went to the outer office. The policeman who had been sent to inform Gething’s family of the tragedy had just returned. French looked at him inquiringly.

“I called, sir, at the address you gave me,” he reported. “Miss Gething was there, and I told her what had occurred. She was considerably upset, and asked me if I could get a message to her sister and brother-in-law at 12 Deeley Terrace, Hawkins Street, in Battersea. I said I would fetch them for her. The brother-in-law, name of Gamage, was from home in Leeds, being a traveller for a firm of fur dealers, but Mrs. Gamage was there and I took her across. It seemed the old lady had wanted to know what was up, and Miss Gething had told her, and she had got some kind of stroke. They asked me to call a doctor, which I did. The two daughters say they can’t get across here on account of being occupied with the mother.”

“So much the better,” French commented, and having added the names and addresses of Mr. and Mrs. Gamage to his list, he turned to the doctor.

===Chinese===
「您说得对，先生。那么，就请您在那边坐几分钟，我先把旁人打发走，随后还要向您请教一些情况。」

老绅士疲惫不堪地跌坐进椅子，弗伦奇则往外来办公室去了。奉命去给格辛家里报凶信的警员恰好在此时回来。弗伦奇探询地望着他。

「报告长官，我按您给的地址去了。」他汇报道。「格辛小姐（Miss Gething）在家，我把事情原委对她讲了。她伤心得很，问能不能给她住在巴特西（Battersea）霍金斯街（Hawkins Street）迪利台 12 号的姐姐姐夫捎个信。我说我替她去接。姐夫名叫加米奇（Gamage），出门在利兹跑生意，替一家皮货商行做旅行推销员；好在加米奇太太在家，我便把她接了过来。据说老太太追问出了什么事，格辛小姐说了，她便中了风。她们请我去叫医生，我叫了。两位小姐说母亲脱不开身，到不了这边来。」

「那倒更好。」弗伦奇评了一句，把加米奇先生夫妇的姓名住址添进单子，便转向医生。

===Original===
“Well, Doctor,” he said pleasantly, “how do you get on?”

The doctor straightened himself up from his position over the corpse.

“I’ve done all I can here,” he answered. “I don’t think there’s any doubt the man was killed instantaneously by the blow on the head. The skull is fractured, apparently by some heavy, blunt weapon. I should think it was done from behind while the old fellow was stooping, possibly working at the safe, though that, perhaps, is your province.”

“I’m glad of the hint anyway. Now, gentlemen, I think that’s all we can do tonight. Can your men remove the body, Superintendent? I want to stay for a moment to take a few measurements. You’ll let me know tomorrow about the inquest? Mr. Orchard, you might stay a moment also; there is a question or two I want to ask you.”

The Superintendent had sent one of his men for a stretcher, and the remains were lifted on and carried slowly down to the waiting taxi. With an exchange of good nights, the local men withdrew, leaving Inspector French, Mr. Duke, Orchard, and the two plainclothes men from the Yard in charge of the premises.

===Chinese===
「怎么样，医生？」他愉快地问。「办得如何？」

医生从尸体上头直起身来。

「我能在这儿做的都做了。」他答道。「此人是被那一击打在头上、当场毙命的，我看毫无疑义。颅骨骨折，凶器显系某种沉重的钝器。我揣想是那老先生弯着腰的时候从背后挨的打，说不定正俯身在保险柜上干活——不过那一层，或许该归你们管了。」

「不管怎样，多谢提点。好了，诸位，今晚能做的我看就到此为止。警司，您的人能把遗体运走吧？我想再留几分钟，量几个尺寸。验尸审讯（inquest）的事，明天劳您通知我一声？奥查德先生，您也请再留片刻；我还有一两个问题要问您。」

警司早已差人取来一副担架，遗体被抬上去，缓缓抬下楼，送进候着的出租车里。彼此道过晚安，本地警员们便告退了，留下弗伦奇探长、杜克先生、奥查德，还有苏格兰场的两名便衣警员，接管了这处地方。

## X Some Pairs of Blankets / 十、几床毛毯

===Original===
During Inspector French’s brief lunch hour he continued turning over in his mind the immediate problem which Mr. Williams’s story had raised for him, namely, at what point he had best attack his new inquiry.

The facts postulated a good deal of obvious detailed investigation, and he felt he should carry this through in his usual systematic way before attempting to evolve a comprehensive theory of the crime. He had first to learn what he could of the mysterious Mrs. Root, and in this connection he foresaw inquiries at Pittsburg, from the White Star people, from the Southampton police, at the Savoy, and at the various firms of jewellers to whom the cheques had been made out. He had, if possible, to find the lady, or her impersonator. These things accomplished, he could turn his attention to an attempt to connect the person found with Miss Duke, or at all events with Mr. Duke’s jewels, and subsequently with the murder of Charles Gething.

By the time his meal was ended he had decided that he would commence operations at the Savoy, and ten minutes later he turned into the courtyard, and making his way to the office, inquired for the manager.

In due course he explained his business to the great man, but the latter shook his head when he heard what was required of him, and asked French for suggestions as to how he could help.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇探长短短一顿午饭的工夫，脑子里翻来覆去掂量的，仍是威廉斯先生那番叙述给他出的眼前难题：这新一轮的调查，究竟从哪一点下手最好。

案情摆在那里，明摆着需要大量细密的查证功夫，他觉得应当先照素来的章法把这一层做扎实，再去尝试勾画一幅贯通全局的罪案理论。他首先得尽其所能摸清那位神秘的鲁特夫人的底细；为此他预见到须向匹兹堡方面问访，向白星公司的人查询，向南安普敦警方问询，到萨伏伊饭店查访，还要到那几张支票抬头所开的几家珠宝行去一一问过。其次，倘然有可能，他还得找到那位夫人本人，或是冒充她的人。这些办妥之后，他才能把心思转往一处：设法把寻着的这个人与杜克小姐牵上线，至少与杜克先生的钻石牵上线，进而与查尔斯·格辛的命案牵上线。

一顿饭吃完，他已打定主意，先从萨伏伊饭店下手。十分钟后，他拐进饭店前庭，一路走到账房，说要见经理。

待来意说明白，这位大人物却连连摇头，说难从命，反请弗伦奇指点一二：他究竟怎样才能帮上这个忙。

===Original===
“First I should like to see the register,” French explained.

“That, at least, is easily done.”

The manager led the way to the office and introduced French to the radiant young woman who presided at the reception counter. Then turning over the pages of the register, he presently exclaimed, “That looks like it, Inspector, I fancy.”

The entry read: “Nov. 24. Mrs. Chauncey S. Root, Pittsburg, U.S.A. 137.”

French drew out the cheques he had obtained from Mr. Scarlett and carefully compared the signatures. “That’s it,” he declared. “There’s not a doubt those are in the same handwriting. Now the question is, Can this young lady remember the woman?”

The clerk hesitated.

===Chinese===
「头一件，我想看看旅客登记簿。」弗伦奇说明来意。

「这个嘛，倒不难办。」

经理在前头引路，把他领到账房，介绍给主持接待柜台的那位容光焕发的年轻小姐。随后他翻开登记簿的纸页，不一会儿便叫出声来：「我看这一条像是了，探长。」

那一条写着：「11 月 24 日。昌西·S·鲁特夫人，美国匹兹堡。137 号。」

弗伦奇取出从斯卡利特先生那里拿来的支票，细细比对签名。「就是它。」他断言道。「两处的笔迹同出一手，毫无疑义。现在的问题是：这位小姐还记不记得那个女人？」

女职员迟疑起来。

===Original===
“We had a lot of Americans in that day,” she said slowly, as she ran her eyes down the list of names. “It is not easy to keep track of them all. And this is six weeks ago.” She paused again, then shook her head. “I’m afraid I can’t just place her.”

“It was the day the *Olympic* got to Southampton,” French prompted. “There would no doubt have been a number of people off the steamer special.” He glanced once more at the book. “See, here is a crowd of Americans all together. New York, Boston, New York, New York, Philadelphia, and so on. That represents the special. But﻿—” He paused and ran his finger down the column. “Now, this is really rather interesting. Mrs. Root’s name is not among them. Here it is, down near the end of the list. That means that she came in late in the evening, doesn’t it? Does that help you at all, Miss Pearson?” He waited, but the girl not replying, he continued, “Or the room? Does No. 137 bring anything to your mind?”

The girl shook her pretty head.

“Turn up the account, Miss Pearson,” the manager suggested.

The girl produced another huge book, and all three went through the items. Mrs. Root, it appeared, had paid for the rooms﻿—No. 137 was a suite consisting of one bedroom, bathroom and sitting-room﻿—for the three nights, the 24th, 25th, and 26th of November. She had had seven meals in the hotel, dinner on the night of arrival, and breakfast, lunch, and dinner on the next two days. All these meals she had had served in her private room.

“Avoiding publicity,” French thought, continuing aloud, “Then she didn’t breakfast on the morning she left?”

===Chinese===
「那天我们接待了一大批美国人。」她缓缓说道，一面把目光顺着那一列名字往下溜。「要个个都对上号，可不容易。这又已经是六个星期前的事了。」她又顿了顿，随后摇了摇头。「只怕我想不起她来了。」

「那正是*奥林匹克号*抵达南安普敦的日子。」弗伦奇提示道。「想必有一批人是坐邮船专列过来的。」他又瞥了一眼簿子。「您瞧，这儿一口气全是一群美国人。纽约、波士顿、纽约、纽约、费城，一路排下去。这一拨便是那趟专列的了。可是——」他顿了顿，手指顺着那一栏往下划。「喏，这可真有点意思。鲁特夫人的名字不在这些人当中。在这儿呢，排在名单靠后的地方。这就是说，她是当天夜里很晚才进店的，对不对？这能不能帮您想起一点什么，皮尔逊小姐（Miss Pearson）？」他等了等，见姑娘不作声，便又往下说：「要不然，是房间？137 号能让您想起什么吗？」

姑娘摇了摇她那漂亮的脑袋。

「把账单调出来看看，皮尔逊小姐。」经理提议道。

姑娘又搬出一本大簿子，三个人把各项账目一一过了一遍。原来鲁特夫人为那几间房——137 号是个套间，一间卧室、一间浴室、外加一间起居室——付了三夜的房钱，即 11 月 24、25、26 三夜。她在饭店里总共用过七顿饭：到达当晚一顿晚餐，随后两天每日早、午、晚各一顿。这些饭菜，她一概叫人送到自己房里单独用。

「避人耳目。」弗伦奇心里想着，嘴上接着问：「这么说，她动身那天早上没有用早餐？」

===Original===
At his remark Miss Pearson gave an exclamation.

“I remember her now,” she cried. “It was your saying that brought her to my mind. No, she didn’t breakfast the morning you mean because she left on the previous night. I remember the whole circumstances now. She came in on the night”﻿—she glanced at the register﻿—“of the 24th﻿—pretty late﻿—it was between seven and eight, I should think﻿—and asked for a suite for three or four weeks. She was dark-haired and pale complexion and very American in her speech. I fixed her up with No. 137, and she said she wanted dinner sent up to her room. Two evenings later, shortly before eight o’clock, she came back to the office and said she had had an urgent wire from Paris, and that she had to go over that night. She hoped to be back in about a week, but she would not keep the rooms on, as she was not certain of her plans. I made out the bill, and what brings the thing back to my mind is that I had to charge her for that night in accordance with our rule. She didn’t seem to mind, the way some people do in such a case. She left then, and I never saw her since.”

This being all the pretty clerk could tell him, French asked next to see the chambermaid who had attended No. 137 on the night in question.

From this woman he at first learned nothing. For a quarter of an hour he prompted fruitlessly, then, just as in the case of the clerk, a chance word brought a ray of light. Asked if she could remember having seen luggage with *Olympic* labels and the name Mrs. Root, she suddenly admitted that she could. Her attention had been attracted by the name Root, as she had been reading in the papers of a distinguished American of the same name, and she had wondered if the owner of the luggage was any connection. She remembered the luggage distinctly. There were two big, new-looking American trunks, labelled on steamer labels, Mrs. Something Root. Yes, she thought it was Chauncey. Something like that anyway, some queer, foreign name that only an American would bear. But though she remembered the luggage, the chambermaid could not recall anything about the lady herself.

After fruitlessly interrogating several other of the hotel servants, French retreated into a deserted corner of the lounge and set himself to think the thing out. And presently it occurred to him that the trunks might represent a clue. Did their removal not involve a taxi, and if so, could he find it?

===Chinese===
听他这么一说，皮尔逊小姐叫出了声。

「我想起她来了！」她喊道。「全亏您那句话把我勾起来的。对，您问的那个早上她没吃早餐，因为她头天夜里就动身走了。前后情形我如今全想起来了。她是那天夜里——」她瞥了一眼登记簿——「24 号——挺晚——我估摸着是在七点到八点之间——进来的，开口要一套房间，一住三四个星期。她黑头发，脸色苍白，一开口满是美国腔。我给她安排了 137 号，她说晚餐要送到房里去用。过了两个晚上，将近八点的光景，她回到账房，说收到巴黎来的急电，当夜就得动身过去。她指望一个星期左右就回来，但不愿把房间继续留下，因为自己的行程拿不准。我开了账单，而教我把这事记起来的，是照店里的规矩，那一夜的房钱我不得不照收。她倒毫不在意——换了有些客人遇上这种事，往往还要计较。她随即便走了，打那以后我再没见过她。」

这位漂亮的女职员能说的就这么多，弗伦奇接着求见案发那晚伺候 137 号的客房女侍。

从这个女人嘴里，他起初一无所获。整整一刻钟，他的启发毫无成效；随后恰如方才对女职员那样，一句凑巧的话透进一线亮光。问到她可记得见过贴着*奥林匹克号*标签、写着鲁特夫人名姓的行李，她忽然承认记得。她是因为「鲁特」这个姓才留了意的——那阵子她正在报上读到一位同姓的美国名人，心里便纳闷这行李的主人是不是那家的亲戚。那几件行李她记得清清楚楚：两口崭新的大号美式衣箱，轮船行李标签上印着「某某·鲁特夫人」一类的字样。对，她记得是个「昌西」，反正差不离的这么个名字，一个古里古怪的洋名字，只有美国人才会起。只是行李虽记得真切，那位夫人本人是什么模样，客房女侍却怎么也想不起来了。

又盘问了几名别的饭店仆役，一无所获，弗伦奇便退到休息厅一个冷僻的角落里，定下神来把整件事从头想透。想着想着，他忽然转到一个念头上：那几口衣箱，未尝不是一条线索。把衣箱运走总得用出租车；倘然如此，那辆车找得着么？

===Original===
He went back to the head porter to make inquiries. Vehicles were usually obtained from the rank in the street adjoining. Of course it frequently happened that a driver looking for a fare would pass at the critical moment and be employed, but seven out of ten were obtained from the rank.

French left the hotel, and, sauntering down to the cab rank, engaged the driver of the leading car in conversation. All the taxis on the rank, the man stated, were the property of one firm, Metropolitan Transport, Ltd. The men returned the runs they had made on their daily journals, and French could, if he chose to apply to the office in Victoria Street, learn all there was to be known about it.

French did choose, and a quarter of an hour later was in conversation with the manager. But that gentleman was dubious that he could supply the desired information. It was true they kept a pretty complete record of the runs made and these had to balance with the readings of the meters and with the money handed in, but obviously no note was made of the names or descriptions of the fares. He could find out if a car had gone from the Savoy to Victoria about 7:45 p.m. on the night of the 26th November, but he could not say who might have travelled in it.

“If you could let me have a note of the cars which left the hotel between 7:40 and 8:10, irrespective of their destinations, I should be obliged,” French declared. “I could see all the drivers, and possibly some one of them might remember the woman.”

“I can give you that,” the manager assented, “but it will take a little time to get out.” He rang for a clerk and gave the necessary instruction, then leaned back in his chair and went on conversationally, “What’s the trouble? Is it indiscreet to ask?”

===Chinese===
他回头去找门房领班打听。车辆通常是从紧邻的那条街边的候车点上叫的。当然也常有这种情形：哪个司机正寻活儿，恰在节骨眼上打门前经过，被就地雇了去；不过十回里有七回，车还是从候车点上雇的。

弗伦奇出了饭店，信步走到候车点，同排头那辆车的司机攀谈起来。据这人说，候车点上的出租车全归一家公司所有，唤作大都会运输公司（Metropolitan Transport, Ltd.）。司机们每天把自己跑的活儿记在出车簿上交回公司；弗伦奇若肯去维多利亚街（Victoria Street）的办公处申请，凡有案可查的，尽可以查个明白。

弗伦奇果然去了，一刻钟后便同经理谈上了。可这位先生对能否供出所需的情报，心里没底。他们确实保存着相当完备的出车记录，这些记录还须同计程器读数、上缴的进款两相轧平；但显而易见，乘客的姓名相貌是一概不记的。他可以查出 11 月 26 日晚上 7 点 45 分前后，有没有一辆车从萨伏伊开往维多利亚；可车上坐的是谁，他就无从说起了。

「您若能开一张单子给我，把 7 点 40 分到 8 点 10 分之间离开饭店的车辆统统列上，不论去向，我就承情不过了。」弗伦奇说道。「司机我可以挨个儿见一遍，说不定他们哪一位还记得那个女人。」

「这个可以给您。」经理应承下来，「只是调出来得花点工夫。」他按铃唤来一名职员，交代了必要的吩咐，随后往椅背上一靠，闲谈似的接着说：「出了什么事？我这样问不算冒昧吧？」

===Original===
French smiled benevolently.

“Certainly not,” he assured the other. “I’ll tell you the whole thing. We believe that the lady I’m after is a crook﻿—a diamond thief. She gave out that she was the wife of a wealthy American steel magnate, but we believe she’s no more that than you are. She left the hotel that night with two trunks and some small luggage, to go to Paris by the 8:20 from Victoria, and has vanished. I’m trying now to trace her.”

The manager seemed interested.

“Well,” he said, “that’s a useful hint you have given just now. Our drivers record the luggage, that is, outside luggage for which there is a charge. It’ll narrow the thing down a bit if we’ve only to count vehicles with two packages outside.”

“That’s a point,” French admitted, “and a good one. But I only know that there were two large trunks besides hand luggage. There might have been more than two packages outside.”

“It’s not likely. If there was only one lady she would have taken the hand stuff in with her. Ah, here’s the list.”

===Chinese===
弗伦奇和蔼地一笑。

「不算不算。」他向对方担保。「我把整个事情讲给您听。我们相信，我要找的这位太太是个骗子——一个钻石窃贼。她放出话来，说自己是美国一位钢铁大亨的太太，可我们相信，她同您一样，压根儿就不是。那天夜里，她带着两口衣箱和几件随身行李离了饭店，去赶 8 点 20 分从维多利亚开出的火车赴巴黎，从此没了踪影。我眼下就是在追她的下落。」

经理似乎来了兴致。

「嗳，」他说，「您方才那句话，倒是个有用的提示。我们的司机是要记行李的——记的是搁在车外、须另加钱的那类行李。倘若只消数车外带两件行李的车，范围就可以缩小一圈了。」

「这话在理。」弗伦奇承认，「而且在理得很。只是我晓得的那会儿，随身小件之外还有两口大箱。车外搁的行李，未必就止两件。」

「不至于。倘然只有太太孤身一人，随身的小件她自然就带进车厢里去了。啊，单子来了。」

===Original===
From the tabulated sheet handed to the manager, it appeared that between the hours of 7:40 and 8:10 on the night in question, no less than twenty-eight taxis had left the Savoy. Of these, twenty had gone to theatres. Of the remaining eight, two had gone to Euston, one to King’s Cross, one to Hampstead, one to Kensington, and three to Victoria.

“There you are,” said the manager, pointing to the second to Victoria. “See under extras, ‘Two packages.’ That’s what you want.”

It looked as if the manager was right. The first of the three vehicles to Victoria had no outside luggage, and the third was for a party of five. No. 2 had left at 7:55 with one passenger and two outside packages.

“It’s promising enough,” French admitted. “If you could tell me where to find the driver of that car I should be much obliged.”

“John Straker.” The manager picked up his desk telephone. “Where is John Straker at present?” he called, and in a moment to French, “He’s out at work. He’s on the stand beside the Savoy, and if you go there now, and don’t mind waiting, you’ll see him. I’ll give you a note to him. It will make him more ready to talk. He’s a peculiar-looking man, clean-shaven, with a thin white face and hooked nose and very black eyes; you’ll recognise him at once. Better take his time-book also. It may bring the trip back to his memory.”

French, having thanked the manager, returned to the cab rank. As he walked down it glancing at the drivers, a taxi drove up and took its place at the tail of the line. Its driver answered the description, and when he had switched off his engine and seemed at liberty, French accosted him and explained his business.

===Chinese===
从递到经理手里的那张表格上看，案发当晚 7 点 40 分到 8 点 10 分之间，离开萨伏伊的出租车竟有 28 辆之多。其中 20 辆去的是各家戏院。余下的 8 辆里，两辆去了尤斯顿，一辆去国王十字车站，一辆去汉普斯特德，一辆去肯辛顿（Kensington），三辆去维多利亚。

「您瞧。」经理指着开往维多利亚的第二辆说。「看『附加』一栏：『行李两件』。这正是您要的。」

看来经理说得不错。去维多利亚的三辆车里，头一辆车外没有行李，第三辆载的是五人结伴的一伙。第 2 辆 7 点 55 分离店，乘客一人，车外行李两件。

「希望不小。」弗伦奇承认。「您若能告诉我上哪儿找得着那辆车的司机，我就感激不尽了。」

「约翰·斯特雷克（John Straker）。」经理拿起桌上的电话。「约翰·斯特雷克这会儿在哪儿？」他冲着话筒问，随即转向弗伦奇：「他出车去了。他就在萨伏伊旁边那个候车点上，您这就去，肯等一等的话，准能见着。我给您写张条子给他。有了条子，他话就好说了。此人模样生得特别：脸刮得精光，一张瘦白的脸，鹰钩鼻，一双眼睛黑得出奇，您一眼就能认出来。顶好把他的出车簿也带去，兴许能勾起他对那趟活的记忆。」

弗伦奇谢过经理，回到候车点。他顺着车列边走边打量两边的司机，正走着，一辆出租车驶了进来，排在队尾。那司机正与所说的模样相符，等他熄了引擎、看样子得了空，弗伦奇便上前搭话，说明来意。

===Original===
For some seconds the man pondered, scratching his head and turning over the leaves of his time-book. At last he looked at French.

“I remember the trip,” he said. “It’s a strange thing, but that was the only trip I made to Victoria that week. It’s a place we’re at pretty often, as you’ll understand. But I remember going that night. It was with a lady, and she had two big boxes; I remember them because they were rather big for the space on the car. But I got them fixed up all right.”

“Where did you go to?”

“I believe the main line departure side of Victoria, though I’m not just certain.”

“Good!” said French heartily. “Now, could you describe the lady?”

This, however, was beyond the driver’s powers. He had not noticed her specially, nor could he describe the porter who had taken the luggage. But French had not expected that, indeed, he was surprised and delighted at having got so much.

===Chinese===
那人挠着头皮，一页页翻着出车簿，寻思了好几秒钟。末了他抬眼看着弗伦奇。

「这趟活儿我记得。」他说。「说来也怪，那个星期我去维多利亚，就这一趟。那地方我们是常跑的，这您明白。可那天夜里这一趟我记得。拉的是位太太，带着两口大箱子；我记得清楚，因为箱子同车顶那点地方比，偏大了些。不过我到底把它们安置妥当了。」

「您拉到哪儿？」

「大概是维多利亚车站干线出发那一侧，不过我不敢说死。」

「好！」弗伦奇由衷地说道。「那么，您能说说那位太太的模样吗？」

这却超出了司机的能力。他没特别留意过她，连搬运行李的搬运工是什么模样也说不上来。不过弗伦奇原没指望这个；说实在的，能问到这么多，他已经又惊又喜了。

===Original===
The rest of that day and most of the next he spent at Victoria, interviewing porters, inspectors, ticket collectors, and any other officials he could find, who might by chance have seen the quarry. But nowhere had he any luck. The unknown remained unknown.

As he continued turning the matter over in his mind, a further possible clue in connection with the trunks occurred to him. They were large; they could not be taken in the carriage. It was nearly certain, therefore, that they must have been registered through. Were there records, he wondered, of such registration?

He went to the registration office and saw the clerk in charge. Yes, there were records; they were kept for a while and then destroyed. He could with a little trouble turn up those for the Newhaven boat train on the 26th November, and he would certainly do so to oblige the Inspector.

But the records, when at last they were produced, revealed neither the name of Mrs. Root nor the fact that anyone had registered two large trunks by that train.

French discussed the possibility of those in question having been taken unregistered. It seemed that this was possible, but most unlikely. In any case, had it been done, the clerk believed the Customs people would have noted it. But it would take some time to find out.

“Don’t trouble about it,” French told him; “at least, not in the meantime.”

===Chinese===
那天余下的工夫和第二天的大半天，他都耗在维多利亚车站，找搬运工、问查票员和收票员，凡是他寻得着的、可能碰巧见过追查对象的工作人员，一一访过。可是处处运气不济，那个无名女人依旧无名。

他脑子里继续翻腾这件事，又想到一条与那几口衣箱有关的可能线索。衣箱又大，是带不进客车车厢的，因此几乎可以断定，它们必定办了托运。这样的托运，他寻思着，可会留下记录？

他去托运处见了主管职员。有记录，对方答道；记录留存一阵子，随后便销毁。11 月 26 日纽黑文邮船列车那一批的记录，他愿意费点事翻出来，为帮探长这个忙，他一定照办。

可等记录终于调了出来，里面既没有鲁特夫人的名字，也看不出有什么人托运过两口大箱上那趟车。

弗伦奇又同他探讨：那两口箱子会不会没办托运就带上了车。据说这倒也可能，只是可能性极小。何况真要是这么办了，这位职员相信，海关那边必定会有记录。只不过要查个水落石出，得费些时日。

「这个事不必费心了。」弗伦奇对他说，「至少眼下不必。」

===Original===
Suppose this woman crook was impersonating Mrs. Root, as he believed she was, would she not, as soon as she had disposed of the diamonds, seek to vanish and to resume her real personality? If so, did this not involve getting rid of the trunks? Did she really require them, or had they served their purpose when they reached Victoria?

As a forlorn hope, he decided he would act on this idea. Suppose she wanted to get rid of them, how would she do it?

There were several ways, but he felt satisfied that the easiest and best would be simply to leave them in the left luggage office. A considerable time would elapse before any question would arise about them, and it would then probably only take the form of their being opened by the railway company, and their contents sold for what they would bring.

He went round to the left luggage office and propounded his inquiry. And immediately he received a pleasant surprise. The clerk to whom he was referred smiled, and turning over some papers, pointed to an item. It read, “Two large American trunks: White Star labels, S.S. *Olympic*. Mrs. Chauncey S. Root, passenger to Southampton.”

“Bit of luck for you, sir,” the clerk remarked. “I was looking over the list only today, and I noticed the item. Boxes were left in on 26th of last month, and have not been claimed.”

“I want to open them and perhaps take them to the Yard.”

===Chinese===
假定这个女骗子真如他所信，冒充的是鲁特夫人，那么钻石一旦脱手，她岂不是马上就要销声匿迹、恢复本来面目？倘然如此，岂不就得甩掉那几口衣箱？这几口衣箱她果真还用得着么，还是说，到了维多利亚，它们的用处便已经使尽？

死马权当活马医，他决意照这个念头做下去。假定她要甩掉衣箱，她会怎么做？

法子不止一种，但他认定，最省事也最稳妥的，莫过于把衣箱往行李寄存处一撂。往后要隔上好一阵子才会有人过问，届时多半也不过是铁路公司开箱，把里面的东西估价变卖而已。

他绕到行李寄存处，道明来意，立刻得了一个惊喜。接待他的职员微微一笑，翻开几页纸，指着其中一行。那上面写着：「美式大箱两口：白星公司轮船标签，*奥林匹克号*。昌西·S·鲁特夫人，赴南安普敦旅客。」

「您运气好，先生。」职员说道。「就在今天，我还翻看过这份清单，正注意到这一条。箱子是上月 26 日存进的，至今没人来取。」

「我要开箱查看，兴许还要把它们带回苏格兰场。」

===Original===
The necessary authority was soon obtained, and French followed the clerk to a huge room stored with luggage of all descriptions. Calling the porter in charge, they were conducted to a corner in which stood two large boxes, and French, looking at the labels, found they were those of which he was in search.

“Pull those out, George,” the clerk directed, “so as this gentleman can open them, and let him take them away if he wants to. That all you want, sir?”

French, left to himself, began by satisfying himself that the handwriting on the labels was the same as that of the cheques. Then, taking a bunch of skeleton keys from his pockets, he set to work on the locks. In a few moments both stood open.

For a space he stood staring down in amazement at their contents. They were full of blankets! Just new, thin blankets of a poor cheap quality. They were fairly tightly packed, and completely filled the trunks.

He took out the blankets, and opening each out, shook it to make sure that no small article was concealed in the folds. But there was nothing.

Nor was there any smooth surface within the empty trunks upon which finger impressions might have been left. They were lined with canvas, fine as to quality, but still too rough to carry prints.

===Chinese===
必要的手续很快办妥，弗伦奇跟着职员走进一间大屋，里头存放着五花八门的行李。职员唤来主管搬运的工人，领他们到一处角落，那儿立着两口大箱。弗伦奇一看标签，正是他要寻的那两口。

「乔治（George），把那两口拖出来。」职员吩咐道，「好让这位先生开箱；他要带走，就让他带走。您要的就这些吗，先生？」

屋里只剩弗伦奇一人。他先验看标签上的笔迹，确证与支票上的同出一手；然后从口袋里掏出一串万能钥匙，动手对付锁头。不多时，两口箱子都敞开了。

有好一阵子，他站在那儿朝箱里直望，惊诧不已。箱子里满满当当，全是毯子！崭新的、薄薄的毯子，料子粗劣廉价。毯子塞得相当紧实，把两口箱子填得严丝合缝。

他把毯子一条条取出来，每条抖开摇过，验看褶层里没有藏什么小物件。可是什么也没有。

掏空的箱子内里，也寻不出一处光滑的表面能留下指印。箱内衬着帆布，布质虽然细密，却仍嫌粗糙，吃不住指纹。

===Original===
Inspector French felt more puzzled and baffled than ever. What, under the sun, were the blankets for? And where was the woman who had carried them about?

He was certainly no further on as to finding her, whether she had crossed to France, or travelled to some other point on the Southern system, or had simply walked out of the station and been swallowed up in the wilderness of London, she was just as completely lost to him as ever. Hard luck that so unexpected a lift as the finding of the trunks should have led to so little.

But there was one thing it had led to. It settled the question of the impersonation. On no other hypothesis could the abandonment of the trunks be explained.

A point of which he had already thought recurred to him. If the unknown had impersonated Mrs. Root she either knew her or knew a great deal about her. The chances, therefore, were that Mrs. Root knew the unknown. It also seemed pretty certain that Mrs. X, as he began to call the unknown in his mind, had really crossed in the *Olympic*. How else would she obtain the labels and the dinner menu? Granted these two probabilities, it almost certainly followed that the real Mrs. Root and Mrs. X had met on board. If so, would it not be worth while interviewing Mrs. Root in the hope that she might by the method of elimination suggest the names of one or more persons who might have carried out the trick, and thus provide French with another point of attack.

Thinking it would be worth while to investigate the matter, he returned to the Yard and sent a cable to the Pittsburg police asking them to obtain Mrs. Root’s present address.

He glanced at his watch. It was not yet five o’clock, and he saw that he would have time to make another call before going off duty. Fifteen minutes later he pushed open the door of Dashford’s Inquiry Agency in Suffolk Street, off the Strand.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇探长比先前愈发如坠五里雾中。这些毯子，天底下到底派什么用场？携带毯子四处走的那女人，如今又在哪里？

至于寻她，他确确实实没有寸进。她或是渡海去了法国，或是去了南部铁路沿线的别的什么车站，再不然干脆步行出了站，一头没进伦敦这座大迷宫——无论哪种情形，她都同先前一样，从他手里溜得干干净净。寻着衣箱本是出乎意料的一步意外之助，到头来竟只落得这么一点点，也算晦气。

不过此事终究敲定了一桩：冒名顶替已无可置疑。若不是冒名，抛弃这几口衣箱便无从解释。

他先前想到过的一点，此刻又浮上心头。倘若那无名氏冒充的是鲁特夫人，她要么认得鲁特夫人，要么就对鲁特夫人知之甚详。因此，多半鲁特夫人也认得这个无名氏。再者，这位他心里已开始唤作X太太的无名氏，看来确凿是乘*奥林匹克号*渡海来的——不然，那些行李标签和那份晚餐菜单，她又从何处得来？这两层若都成立，便几乎可以断言：真正的鲁特夫人与X太太在船上碰过面。倘然如此，去见一见鲁特夫人，岂不值得一试？指望她用排除法点出一两个有可能做下这套手脚的人名，也好给弗伦奇再添一处下手的地方。

他觉得此事值得查一查，便回到苏格兰场，给匹兹堡警方拍了一封电报，请他们查明鲁特夫人如今的住址。

他看了看表，还不到 5 点，估量着下班前还来得及再走一处。一刻钟后，他推开了达什福德侦探事务所的门——那所在萨福克街（Suffolk Street），打斯特兰德街拐进去不远。

===Original===
“Mr. Parker in?” he demanded of the bright young lady who came to the counter, continuing in response to her request for his name, “Inspector French from the Yard, but Mr. Parker’s an old friend and I’ll just go right in.”

The girl eyed him doubtfully as he passed through the counter, and, crossing the office, tapped at a door in the farther wall. Without waiting for a reply, he pushed the door open and passed within, shutting it behind him.

Writing at a desk in the centre of the room was an enormously stout man. He did not look up, but grunted impatiently “Well?”

“Well yourself,” French grunted, mimicking the other’s tone.

The fat man looked up, then a smile dawned on his rubicund countenance, and he got heavily to his feet and held out a huge hand. “Why, Joe, old son, I’m glad to see you. It’s a long time since you blew in. Bring the chair around to the fire and let’s hear the news.”

French did as he was told, as he answered, “All’s well, Tom? Busy?”

===Chinese===
「帕克先生（Mr. Parker）在吗？」他向走到柜台前来的那位伶俐的年轻小姐问道，又应着她请他通报姓名的话头接着说：「苏格兰场的弗伦奇探长。不过帕克先生是老朋友了，我这就自己进去。」

他穿过柜台往里去，那姑娘狐疑地打量着他。他横过办公室，在对面墙上一扇门上叩了两下，不等回话便推门而入，随手把门带上了。

屋子正中的书案后坐着个胖得出奇的人，正伏案写着什么。他头也不抬，不耐烦地哼了一声：「什么事？」

「你什么事。」弗伦奇学着他的腔调哼道。

胖子抬起头来，红润的脸上慢慢绽开笑意，随即吃力地站起身，伸出一只蒲扇般的大手。「嗳哟，乔（Joe），老伙计，见到你真高兴。你可是有些日子没照面了。把椅子搬到炉子边来，讲讲新闻。」

弗伦奇依言把椅子搬了过去，一面答道：「一切都好么，汤姆（Tom）？忙吗？」

===Original===
“Not too busy for a chat with you. How’s the Yard?”

“The Yard’s going strong; same old six and eightpence. I often think you did wisely to chuck it up and start in here. More your own boss, eh?”

The fat man shook his head.

“I don’t know,” he said slowly, handing a tobacco pouch to his visitor. “I don’t know. More your own boss, perhaps; but more worry. If you don’t get jobs here, you don’t get your pay, and no pension at the end except the interest on what you save up. I’ve thought of that pension many a time since I left.”

“Rubbish!” French exclaimed genially as he filled his pipe. “You’re too young to be talking of pensions. I was here looking for you about a week ago, but you were in Scotland.”

“Yes, I was at that Munro case. Acting for old Munro. I think he’ll pull it off.”

===Chinese===
「再忙，同你聊天的工夫总还是有的。苏格兰场怎么样？」

「苏格兰场干得照样起劲，一切照旧，还是那老一套。我常想，你当年撂下那摊子不干、到这儿来开业，真是明智之举。自己当自己的主了，是不是？」

胖子摇了摇头。

「难说。」他慢慢说着，把烟丝袋递给客人。「难说。自己当自己的主，许是；可操心也多。在这儿接不着活，就挣不着钱，到老也没有养老金，只有自家攒下的那点钱生利息。打从离职起，我不知念叨过多少回那笔养老金。」

「胡说！」弗伦奇一面往烟斗里装烟丝，一面和气地嚷道。「你还嫩得很，谈不上什么养老金。约莫一星期前，我到这儿来找过你，可你去苏格兰（Scotland）了。」

「可不，我办门罗（Munro）那桩案子去了，替老门罗当代表。我看他能成事。」

===Original===
“I dare say.” The talk drifted on, then French turned it to the object of his call.

“I’m on a case that you people have had a finger in. I wish you’d tell me what you can about it. It’s that business of Mrs. Root of Pittsburg that Williams & Davies of Cockspur Street put you on to six weeks ago. They wanted you to find out what she was like, and if she crossed by the *Olympic*.”

“Huh,” said the fat man. “Well, we told ’em. I handled it myself.”

“Did they tell you why they wanted to know?”

“Nope. Only asked the question.”

“That’s where they made the mistake. A woman called on Williams, saying she was Mrs. Root and had crossed by the *Olympic*. She said she had lost her despatch case with her passport and tickets and money, and she wanted a loan of £3,000 on the security of diamonds she had in her trunk.”

===Chinese===
「大概吧。」闲话扯了一阵，弗伦奇才把话头转到此行的正题上。

「我手头有件案子，你们这号人也插过一腿。我想请你把知道的都告诉我。就是匹兹堡鲁特夫人那件事——六个星期前，科克斯珀街的威廉斯与戴维斯商行托你们查的那件。他们要你们查明她的长相如何、是否乘*奥林匹克号*渡的海。」

「唔。」胖子说。「我们答复他们了。那件事是我亲手经办的。」

「他们说过为什么要查吗？」

「没有。光问了个问题。」

「毛病就出在这儿。有个女人去找威廉斯，自称是鲁特夫人，说是乘*奥林匹克号*渡海来的。她说自己把小公文箱丢了，护照、船票和现款都在里头，想拿她衣箱里的钻石作押，借 3,000 镑。」

===Original===
“Well? Was it not right?”

“It was perfectly right so far. Williams was satisfied from what you told him that she was the woman, and he lent the money.”

French paused, smiling, and his friend swore.

“Confound it, man! Can’t you get on? Were the stones paste?”

“Not at all. They took them to Stronge, of Hurst & Stronge’s, and he valued them. They were perfectly all right, worth £3,300 odd, *but*”﻿—French paused and became very impressive﻿—“they were all stolen from Duke & Peabody the night before!”

The fat man was visibly impressed. He stared fixedly at French, as he might had that philosopher turned into Mrs. Root before his eyes. Then heavily he smote his thigh.

===Chinese===
「哦？难道不是实情？」

「到此为止，桩桩属实。威廉斯听了你们的答复，认定她正是其人，便把钱借出去了。」

弗伦奇顿住不说了，笑吟吟的；他那位朋友却骂开了。

「该死的，伙计！你就不能一口气说完？那些钻石是糊的假货？」

「一点不假。他们把钻石拿到赫斯特与斯特朗商行的斯特朗先生那里，他作了估价。货色完全地道，值 3,300 多镑。*可是*——」弗伦奇顿了顿，换上一副郑重的神色——「这些钻石，全都是头天夜里从杜克与皮博迪公司偷出来的！」

胖子显然被震住了。他直勾勾地盯着弗伦奇，那神情，仿佛眼见这位贤哲当着他的面摇身变成了鲁特夫人似的。随后他沉沉地一巴掌拍在自己大腿上。

===Original===
“Je﻿—hosaphat!” he observed slowly. “The night before! Some crook that! Tell me.”

“That’s about all there is to tell,” French declared. “The woman arrived at the Savoy about eight o’clock, the night before, ostensibly from the *Olympic*, and she left next night and has vanished. No clue so far. I traced her to Victoria and there lost the trail.”

The fat man thought profoundly.

“Well, if Williams & Davies want to blame us for it, they can look elsewhere,” he presently announced. “They asked us a question, and we gave them a correct and immediate reply.”

“I know that,” French agreed. “Williams asked you the wrong question. Mrs. Root was impersonated; at least, that’s my theory. But what I wanted to know from you was how you got your information. Between ourselves, are you satisfied about it?”

===Chinese===
「我的——老天爷！」他慢条斯理地说。「头天夜里！好家伙，这贼的手段！讲给我听听。」

「要讲的也就这么些了。」弗伦奇说道。「那女人头天夜里八点来钟住进萨伏伊，明面上是从*奥林匹克号*下来的；第二夜就走了，从此杳无音信。至今没有一点线索。我追到维多利亚，线索就断在了那儿。」

胖子深深地思索着。

「哼，威廉斯与戴维斯商行要是想把这事儿记到我们头上，那就另请高明吧。」他隔了一会儿宣布道。「他们问了一个问题，我们给了他们一个又准又快的答复。」

「这我知道。」弗伦奇表示同意。「是威廉斯把问题问错了。鲁特夫人是被人冒充的；至少，我的理论如此。可我想向你讨教的，是你们这份情报怎么弄来的。咱们私底下说说，你对它放心么？」

===Original===
The fat man shook his fist good-humouredly.

“Now, young man,” he advised, “don’t you get fresh with me. But I’ll tell you,” he went on, suddenly grave. “It was through Pinkerton’s. We have an arrangement with them. I cabled their New York depot and they got the information.”

“I knew it would be all right,” French answered, “but I was curious to know how you worked.”

The two men chatted for some time, then French said he must go. Half an hour later he reached his house, and with a sigh of relief at the thought of his slippers and his armchair, let himself in.

===Chinese===
胖子好脾气地朝他晃了晃拳头。

「听我说，年轻人。」他告诫道，「别跟我要贫嘴。不过我告诉你。」他忽然正色起来，接着说道：「是走的平克顿（Pinkerton's）的路子。我们同他们有协作。我给他们纽约的总部拍了电报，情报是他们弄来的。」

「我就知道错不了。」弗伦奇答道，「不过是好奇你们是怎么运作的。」

两人又闲谈了一阵，随后弗伦奇说该走了。半小时后他到了家，一想到拖鞋和扶手椅，便宽慰地舒了口气，开门进了屋。

## VIII Sylvia and Harrington / 八、西尔维娅与哈林顿

===Original===
Taximan Tomkins was a wizened-looking man with a surly manner and the air of having a constant grievance, but he was evidently overawed by the situation in which he found himself, and seemed anxious to do his best to answer the Inspector’s questions clearly.

He remembered the evening in question. He had been hailed by a gentleman near Liverpool Street, and told to drive to the Curtis Street Girls’ Club. There, after some delay, they had picked up a young lady.

“What address did you get?” French asked.

“I don’t just remember,” the man said slowly scratching his head. “Somewhere in Hampstead it was, but I’m blest if I could tell you where.”

“The Cedars, Hampstead, perhaps?”

===Chinese===
出租汽车司机汤姆金斯（Tomkins）面相干瘪枯瘦，举止乖戾，浑身透着一股终年怀怨、牢骚满腹的神气；可眼下这处境显然把他震住了，他似乎一心想尽力把探长的问话答个清楚明白。

他记得案发那晚的事。他在利物浦街（Liverpool Street）附近被一位先生招呼停车，吩咐开往柯蒂斯街女子俱乐部（Curtis Street Girls’ Club）。在那里，耽搁了一阵之后，他们接上了一位年轻小姐。

「你接到的地址是哪儿？」弗伦奇问。

「一时记不真了，」那人慢吞吞地说，一边挠着脑袋。「反正是汉普斯特德（Hampstead）那一带，可要说出个准地方，见鬼，我说不上来。」

「汉普斯特德的雪松居（The Cedars），也许是？」

===Original===
“That’s right guv’nor. That was it.”

“And the two started off together?”

“Yes, the other young lydie just saw them off.”

“Now tell me, did they meet anyone else on the way home?”

“Not while they were in the keb, they didn’t.”

===Chinese===
「没错儿，长官。就是那儿。」

「随后他们两位就一道动身了？」

「是的，另外那位小姐只送了送他们。」

「那我问你，回家的路上，他们可还遇上过旁的什么人？」

「在车里的那阵子，倒没遇上。」

===Original===
“Or buy a paper, or stop for any purpose whatever?”

“They stopped and got out for a ’arf a mo’, but I can’t say if it was to buy a paper.”

“Oh, they stopped, did they? Where was that?”

“In Holborn, just past the end of Hatton Garden.”

“What?” cried French, surprised out of his usual calm superiority. “Tell me about that.”

===Chinese===
「可曾买报，或者为了什么事由停过车？」

「停过，还下去待了一忽儿，是不是买报我可说不上。」

「哦，停过车，是吧？在哪儿？」

「在霍尔本（Holborn），刚过哈顿花园（Hatton Garden）口子的地方。」

「什么？」弗伦奇叫出声来，素来的从容笃定竟为之一夺。「把这一节细细讲来。」

===Original===
The driver was stupid and suspicious, but in time the details came out. The most direct route led along Holborn, and he had taken it, but when he reached the point in question the young man had hailed him through the speaking tube. “Hold on a minute, driver,” he had called. “Look sharp, please.” He had pulled over to the kerb, but almost before he had come to a stand the young man had jumped out and had hurried across the street. The lady had then alighted, had told Tomkins to wait, and had followed him. Tomkins had at first feared he was going to lose his money, but after a couple of minutes they had both returned and the girl had got in. She had bidden good night to her friend, and he, Tomkins, had driven her off, leaving the man standing on the pavement. On arrival at Hampstead, the lady had paid him and entered the house. As far as the driver had noticed neither of the young people was excited or upset.

This information gave French cause for thought. On obtaining Harrington’s statement on the morning after the murder, he had imagined the young man was keeping something back. And now he found that he had been right. The young fellow had not mentioned the fact that he had been within a few yards of the scene of the crime at the time at which it had taken place. He had stated that he had seen Sylvia home, and now it appeared he had not done so, but had accompanied her only halfway. French reminded himself with satisfaction that his instinct on such a point was seldom far astray.

Furthermore, this news confirmed his growing suspicion that Miss Duke also knew something about the affair. It seemed too far-fetched a coincidence that this unexpected stop near the scene of the crime, the mental upset of both herself and Harrington, and the postponing of the wedding, were unconnected with the tragedy. What the connection might be he could not imagine but he could not but believe it existed.

===Chinese===
这司机脑筋迟钝，又疑神疑鬼，好在磨了一阵，细节总算一件件挤了出来。最近便的路线走的是霍尔本，他照直开了，可一到那个地方，那位先生就从传话筒里喊他：「等一等，司机师傅，」他喊道，「劳驾，抓点紧。」他把车靠向路边，几乎还没停稳，那位先生已经跳下车，匆匆穿过街去。接着小姐也下了车，吩咐汤姆金斯等着，跟了过去。汤姆金斯起先还怕车钱要泡汤，不料两分钟后两人一道回来了，小姐上了车。她向同伴道了晚安，他——汤姆金斯——便载着她开走，把那位先生撂在人行道上。到了汉普斯特德，小姐付了车钱，进了宅门。据这司机所留意到的，那两个年轻人谁也不曾激动，也不曾失态。

这些情报很值得弗伦奇寻味。命案次日早晨录哈林顿（Harrington）口供的时候，他就觉得这年轻人有话藏着。如今果然证明他没想错。案发之时，这小伙子距现场不过几步之遥，而他对此事只字未提。他声称送了西尔维娅（Sylvia）回家，现在看来并非如此——他只送了一半路程。弗伦奇颇为自得地想，自己在这一类关节上的直觉，向来少有走偏的时候。

再者，这一消息也坐实了他日渐加深的疑心：杜克小姐（Miss Duke）对此事同样知情。在案发现场近旁突然停车、她本人和哈林顿双双失态、婚期又告推迟——若说这些同惨案全不相干，那巧合也未免太牵强了。其间关联究竟是什么，他想象不出，却又不能不信其中必有牵连。

===Original===
Determined to put the matter to the test without further delay, he drove to the Hatton Garden office and asked for Harrington. The young fellow received him politely, though French thought he could sense an air of strain in his manner. After the briefest greeting he came directly to the point.

“Mr. Harrington,” he began, “I want to ask you one question. In our conversation on the morning after the crime you told me you had seen Miss Duke home on the previous night. Why did you state this when you had only seen her as far as Hatton Garden?”

The young man paled somewhat. He did not seem taken aback, rather he gave French the impression of feeling that he was now face to face with a crisis he had long expected. He answered without hesitation and with an evident attempt at dignity.

“I quite admit that I left Miss Duke near the end of Hatton Garden, but I don’t admit that that was in any way inconsistent with what I told you. Certainly I had no intention of deceiving you.”

“I don’t appreciate your point, Mr. Harrington,” French said sternly. “There is a very considerable difference between seeing Miss Duke home and not doing so.”

===Chinese===
他打定主意即刻验证此事，便驱车前往哈顿花园的商行，求见哈林顿。小伙子接待得倒客气，只是弗伦奇觉得从他举止间能觉出一股强撑的紧张。寒暄不过一两句，他便直奔正题。

「哈林顿先生，」他开口道，「我想问你一个问题。案发次日早晨我们谈话时，你告诉过我，头天晚上你送了杜克小姐回家。既然你分明只送她到哈顿花园，为什么那样说？」

年轻人脸色微微发白。他似乎并不慌乱，倒更像是终于迎上了早有预料的一场关口——弗伦奇从他身上得到的是这样的印象。他毫不迟疑地作答，还显然竭力端着几分体面。

「我完全承认，我是在哈顿花园尽头附近同杜克小姐分手的，但我不承认这同我对您说的话有什么出入。我绝对没有存心欺骗您。」

「你这套说法，我可不敢苟同，哈林顿先生，」弗伦奇正色道。「送杜克小姐回家和不送她回家，这中间的分别可大得很。」

===Original===
The young man flushed.

“I got a cab, drove to the club to meet Miss Duke, picked her up, and accompanied her a considerable part of the way home. I consider I was perfectly justified in saying I saw her home.”

“Then our ideas of the meanings of words are strangely different. I shall be glad if you will now tell me why you both alighted from your taxi near this street, and why you then allowed Miss Duke to proceed alone.”

This time Harrington seemed taken aback, but in a moment he pulled himself together, and he answered coherently enough:

“Certainly, there is no secret or mystery about it. As we were driving along, Miss Duke suddenly pointed to a tall girl in one of those glossy blue waterproofs, and told me to stop the cab, as she wished to speak to her. I shouted to the driver, and when he drew in to the kerb I jumped out and ran after the girl. Unfortunately she had disappeared, and though I searched round I could not find her.
