When I came back I found that Miss Duke had also alighted. I explained that I had missed her friend, but she only said, ‘Never mind, it can’t be helped.’ She got into the cab again, and I was about to follow, but she said No, that there was no use in taking me farther out of my way, and that she would go home alone.”

===Chinese===
年轻人涨红了脸。

「我叫了一辆车，开到俱乐部去接杜克小姐，接上她，又陪她走了回家路上挺长的一段。我认为，我说送了她回家，是完全站得住脚的。」

「这么说来，你我两人对词语含义的理解，可真是天差地别。现在就请你告诉我，你们两位为什么在这条街附近下了出租汽车，之后又为什么放杜克小姐一个人走完剩下的路。」

这一回哈林顿像是吃了一惊，但片刻之间便定住神，答话也还条理分明：

「当然可以，这事没什么可保密的，也无神秘可言。我们一路行驶的时候，杜克小姐忽然指着一个穿亮闪闪蓝色雨衣的高个子姑娘，吩咐我停车，说她想同那姑娘讲话。我朝司机喊了一声，车一靠边我就跳下去追那姑娘。不巧她已经不见了，我四下里找了一圈也没找着。等我回来，发现杜克小姐也下了车。我说明没追上她的朋友，她只说了句：『不要紧，追不上就算了。』她重新上了车，我正要跟上去，她却说不用了，犯不着叫我再绕远路，她自个儿回家就行。」

===Original===
“Did you know the girl?”

“No, Miss Duke did not tell me who she was.”

“You might describe her.”

“I really could not, except that she was tall and wearing the blue waterproof and carrying an umbrella. You see, it was dark, and I only got a glimpse of her by the street lamps. She was swinging along quickly towards Oxford Street.”

“What did you do after Miss Duke drove off?”

“I went home, as I have already told you.”

===Chinese===
「你认识那个姑娘吗？」

「不认识，杜克小姐也没告诉我她是谁。」

「那么请描述一下她的模样。」

「实在描述不出，只知道她个子高，穿一件蓝色雨衣，带着一把伞。您想，天黑着呢，我只借着路灯的光瞥了她一眼。她正甩开大步，急急朝牛津街（Oxford Street）方向走去。」

「杜克小姐坐车走了之后，你做了什么？」

「我回家了，这我先前已经说过。」

===Original===
And that was all Inspector French could get out of him. In spite of all his questions, the young man stuck absolutely to his story.

It was obvious to French that he must next get Miss Duke’s statement, and with this in view he drove out to The Cedars. He asked Harrington to accompany him, so as to prevent his telephoning to the young lady to put her on her guard, and on reaching the house he bade him good day with a somewhat sardonic smile.

Miss Duke was at home, and presently joined him in the breakfast-room to which he had been shown.

She was a comely maiden, slightly given to plumpness, perhaps, but pretty and kindly and wholesome looking, a sight indeed to warm a man’s heart. But she looked pale and worried, and French felt that her experience, whatever it was, had hit her hard.

===Chinese===
任凭弗伦奇探长怎样盘问，能从他嘴里掏出来的，也就只有这些了。这年轻人把他那套说法咬得死死的，纹丝不动。

弗伦奇心里明白，下一步必须拿到杜克小姐的口供，于是驱车前往雪松居。他叫哈林顿同车随行，免得他抢先打电话给那位小姐、教她有了防备；到了宅门前，他带着几分揶揄的笑意同哈林顿道了日安。

杜克小姐正在家中，不多时便来到他被引进的早餐室里。

她是个标致的姑娘，兴许略显丰腴，却生得秀美、和善、健旺，端的是叫人打心眼里喜欢的模样。只是眼下她面色苍白，愁容满面；弗伦奇感觉到，她那番遭遇——不管是什么——着实打击了她。

===Original===
“I am sorry to trouble you, Miss Duke, but I am inquiring into the recent crime at your father’s office, and I find I require to ask you a few questions.”

As he spoke he watched her sharply, and he was intrigued to notice a flash of apprehension leap into her clear eyes.

“Won’t you sit down?” she invited, with a somewhat strained smile.

He seated himself deliberately, continuing:

“My questions, I am afraid, are personal and impertinent, but I have no option but to ask them. I will go on to them at once, without further preamble. The first is, What was it that upset you so greatly on the day after the crime?”

===Chinese===
「杜克小姐，很抱歉打扰你。我正在调查近日令尊商行里发生的那桩罪案，有几件事需要问你。」

说话间他锐利地打量着她，却饶有兴味地注意到，一丝惊惧跃进了她清亮的眼睛。

「您请坐。」她招呼道，笑容里带着几分勉强。

他不慌不忙地落座，接着说道：

「我的问题，恐怕既涉私事，又嫌冒昧，可我别无选择，只能问。长话短说，这就开门见山。第一问：案发次日，究竟是什么事使你那样方寸大乱？」

===Original===
She looked at him in evident surprise, and, he imagined, in some relief also.

“Why, how can you ask?” she exclaimed. “Don’t you think news like that was enough to upset anyone? You see, I had known poor Mr. Gething all my life, and he had always been kind to me. I sincerely liked and respected him, and to learn suddenly that he had been murdered in that cold-blooded way, why, it was awful﻿—*awful*. It certainly upset me, and I don’t see how it could have done anything else.”

French nodded.

“Quite so, Miss Duke, I fully appreciate that. But I venture to suggest that there was something more in your mind than the tragic death of your old acquaintance; something of more pressing and more personal interest. Come now, Miss Duke, tell me what it was.”

The flash of apprehension returned to her eyes, and then once again the look of relief.

“You mean the loss of the diamonds,” she answered calmly. “I deplored that, of course, particularly on my father’s account. But it was Mr. Gething’s death that really, as you call it, upset me. The diamonds we could do without, but we could not give the poor old man back his life.”

===Chinese===
她望着他，显然吃了一惊，而且——他料想——还松了一口气。

「哎呀，这怎么还能问呢？」她叫起来。「您不觉得那样的消息尽够教任何人乱了方寸么？您想，可怜的格辛先生（Mr. Gething）我打小就认识，他待我一向很好，我是真心喜欢他、敬重他。猛然间听说他竟叫人那样冷血地谋害了，哎呀，太可怕了——*太可怕了*。这自然教我心神大乱，我想不出这样的事还能教人怎么着。」

弗伦奇点了点头。

「正是如此，杜克小姐，这一点我完全体谅。不过我冒昧说一句，那令你萦怀的，恐怕还不止老熟人惨死这一桩；还有一件事，更急切，也更切乎你自身。好了，杜克小姐，告诉我那是什么。」

那抹惊惧又回到她眼中，随即再度换成了如释重负的神色。

「您是指钻石失窃，」她平静地答道。「这事我自然痛惜，尤其是替我父亲难过。可真正叫我——按您的说法——『心神大乱』的，是格辛先生的死。钻石没了，我们还撑得住；可怜老人的命，却再也还不回来了。」

===Original===
“I did not mean the loss of the diamonds, Miss Duke. I meant something more personal than that. I’m afraid you must tell me about it.”

There was now no mistaking the girl’s uneasiness, and French grew more and more hopeful that he was on the track of something vital. But she was not giving anything away.

“You must be mistaken,” she said in a lower tone. “It was the news of the murder, and that alone, which upset me.”

French shook his head.

“I would rather not take that answer from you. Please reconsider it. Can you tell me nothing else?”

“Nothing. That is all I have to say.”

===Chinese===
「我指的不是钻石失窃，杜克小姐。我指的是比那更切乎你自身的事。恐怕你必须告诉我了。」

姑娘的不安此刻已掩饰不住，弗伦奇越来越存着指望，觉得自己正循着某件紧要东西的踪迹。可她就是滴水不漏。

「您一定弄错了，」她放低了声音说。「叫我心绪不宁的，是凶案的消息，仅此而已。」

弗伦奇摇了摇头。

「我不愿从你口中得到这样的回答。请再斟酌一下。此外当真没有什么可告诉我的了？」

「没有了。我要说的就这些。」

===Original===
“Very well. I trust it may not be necessary to reopen the matter. Now I want you to tell me why you postponed your wedding with Mr. Harrington.”

Miss Duke flushed deeply.

“I will tell you nothing of the sort, Mr. Inspector!” she declared with some show of anger. “What right have you to ask me such a question? That is a matter between Mr. Harrington and myself alone.”

“I hope you are right, Miss Duke, but I fear there is a chance that you may be mistaken. Do you absolutely decline to answer me?”

“Of course I do! No girl would answer such a question. It is an impertinence to ask it.”

“In that case,” French said grimly, “I shall not press the matter﻿—for the present. Let me turn to another subject. I want you next to tell me why you stopped at Hatton Garden on your way home from the Curtis Street Girls’ Club on the night of the crime.”

===Chinese===
「很好。但愿这事往后不必重提。现在我要你告诉我，你和哈林顿先生的婚事为什么推迟了。」

杜克小姐的脸腾地红透了。

「这种事我一个字也不会告诉您，探长先生！」她带着几分怒气宣称。「您有什么权利问我这样的问题？那是我和哈林顿先生两个人之间的事。」

「但愿你是对的，杜克小姐，只怕你也有弄错的可能。你是断然拒绝回答我了？」

「当然拒绝！哪个姑娘也不会回答这种问题。问出这种话来，本身就是冒犯。」

「既然如此，」弗伦奇冷冷地说，「这件事我就不追问了——眼下不追问。换个话头吧。接下来我要你告诉我，案发当晚，你从柯蒂斯街女子俱乐部回家的路上，为什么在哈顿花园停了车。」

===Original===
For a moment the girl seemed too much surprised to reply, then she answered with a show of indignation: “Really, Mr. French, this is too much! May I ask if you suspect me of the crime?”

“Not of committing it,” French returned gravely, “but,” he leaned forward and gazed keenly into her eyes, “I do suspect you of knowing something about it. Could you not, Miss Duke, if you chose, put me on the track of the criminal?”

“Oh, no, no, no!” the girl cried piteously, motioning with her hands as if to banish so terrible a thought from her purview. “How can you suggest such a thing? It is shameful and horrible!”

“Of course, Miss Duke, I can’t make you answer me if you don’t want to. But I put it to you that it is worth your while thinking twice before you attempt to keep back information. Remember that if I am not satisfied, you may be asked these same questions in court, and then you will have to answer them whether you like it or not. Now I ask you once again, Why did you leave your taxi at Hatton Garden?”

“I think it is perfectly horrible of you to make all these insinuations against me without any grounds whatever,” she answered a little tremulously. “There is no secret about why I stopped the taxi, and I have never made any mystery about it. Why it should have any importance I can’t imagine.” She paused, then with a little gesture as if throwing discretion to the winds, continued: “The fact is that as we were driving home I suddenly saw a girl in the street whom I particularly wished to meet. I stopped the cab and sent Mr. Harrington after her, but he missed her.”

===Chinese===
姑娘一时间像是惊得答不上话，随后带着一脸愤慨答道：「真的，弗伦奇先生，这也太过分了！我可不可以问一句，您莫非疑心这案子是我干的？」

「不是疑心你干的，」弗伦奇郑重答道，「不过——」他探身向前，目光锐利地逼视着她的眼睛，「我确实疑心你知情。杜克小姐，只要你愿意，你难道不能把凶手的一条线索交到我手上吗？」

「哦，不，不，不！」姑娘凄然叫道，双手直摆，仿佛要把这可怕的念头从眼前驱走。「您怎么能说这样的话？这可耻，太可怕了！」

「当然，杜克小姐，你不想答，我也不能逼你。不过我把话放在这里：想扣住消息不报之前，值得自己掂量掂量。记住，我若不满意，这些问题到了法庭上还会照原样问你，到那时不管你愿不愿意，都得回答。现在我再问你一遍：你为什么在哈顿花园下了车？」

「您毫无凭据地对我百般影射，我认为这实在太过分了，」她的回答微微发颤。「我为什么停车，这里头没有任何秘密，我也从没把它神秘化。这事究竟有什么要紧，我实在想不出。」她顿了顿，随即做了个小手势，像是把谨慎抛到了九霄云外，接着说下去：「实情是，我们坐车回家的路上，我忽然在街上看见一个姑娘，特别想同她见一面。我让车停下，打发哈林顿先生去追，可他没追上。」

===Original===
“Who was she?”

“I don’t know; that is why I was so anxious to see her. I suppose you want the whole story?” She tossed her head and went on without waiting for him to reply. “Last summer I was coming up to town from Tonbridge, where I had been staying, and this girl and I had a carriage to ourselves. We began to talk, and became quite friendly. When they came to collect the tickets I found I had lost mine. The man wanted to take my name, but the girl insisted on lending me the money to pay my fare. I wrote down her name and address on a scrap of paper so that I could return the money to her, but when I reached home I found I had lost the paper, and I stupidly had not committed the address to memory. I could not send her the money, and I don’t know what she must have thought of me. You can understand, therefore, my anxiety to meet her when I saw her from the cab.”

“But why did you pay your fare a second time? You must have known that all you had to do was to give your name and address to the ticket collector.”

“I suppose I did,” she admitted, “but I preferred to pay rather than have the trouble of explanations and probably letters to the head office.”

===Chinese===
「她是谁？」

「不知道；正因如此，我才那么急着想见她。您怕是想听个前因后果吧？」她把头一扬，不等他答话便说下去。「去年夏天，我在汤布里奇（Tonbridge）小住之后乘火车回城，车厢里只有我和这位姑娘两个人。我们聊了起来，处得颇为投缘。查票的时候，我发现自己的车票丢了。查票员要记我的姓名，那姑娘却执意借钱给我补了车钱。我把她的姓名地址记在一张纸片上，好日后把钱还她，可到了家一摸，纸片也丢了，而我蠢就蠢在没把地址记进脑子里。钱还不出去了，天知道她会把我看成什么样的人。所以您能明白，我从车窗里望见她时，那份想同她见面的急切了。」

「可你为什么甘愿把车钱再付一遍呢？你该知道，只消把姓名地址报给查票员就完事了。」

「我想是知道，」她承认道，「可我宁可掏钱，也省得又是费口舌解释，说不定还得同总公司公文往来的麻烦。」

===Original===
Inspector French was chagrined. Instinctively he doubted the story, but Miss Duke had answered his question in a reasonable way, and if she stuck to the tale, he did not see how he could break her down. After this lapse of time it would be quite impossible to obtain confirmation or otherwise of the details, especially as Miss Duke’s hypothetical fellow-traveller could not be produced. He pointedly made no comment on the statement as he resumed his investigation.

“To whom did you telephone after breakfast on the morning after the murder?”

That Miss Duke was amazed at the extent of the Inspector’s knowledge was evident, but she answered immediately.

“To Mr. Harrington.”

“To say what?”

“If I must repeat my private conversations to my future husband, it was to ask him to meet me at once as I had something to say to him.”

===Chinese===
弗伦奇探长好不懊恼。他本能地不信这套说辞，可杜克小姐的回答合情合理，只要她一口咬定，他就看不出怎样才能攻破。事到如今，那些细节已无从核实——杜克小姐那位子虚乌有的旅伴，更是无处对证。他有意对这份说辞不置一词，径自把查问继续下去。

「凶案次日早晨，你吃过早饭之后给谁打了电话？」

探长的耳目竟灵通到这般地步，杜克小姐的惊愕显而易见，但她应声便答了。

「给哈林顿先生。」

「说了些什么？」

「要是我非得把同未婚夫的私人谈话复述出来，那是要他立刻来见我，因为我有话要对他讲。」

===Original===
“What was the nature of the communication?”

Miss Duke flushed again.

“Really,” she exclaimed, “I protest against this. What possible connection can our private affairs have with your business?”

“It is your own fault, Miss Duke. You are not telling me the whole truth, and I am therefore suspicious. I want to find out what you are keeping back, and I may tell you that I am going to do so. What did you want to see Mr. Harrington about so urgently?”

The girl seemed terribly distressed.

“If you will have it, it was about the postponement of the wedding,” she said in a low voice. “You understand, we had been discussing the matter on the night before, when no conclusion had been come to. But on sleeping on it I had made up my mind in favour of the postponement, and I wanted to tell Mr. Harrington at once.”

===Chinese===
「要讲的是什么性质的事？」

杜克小姐又红了脸。

「真是的，」她叫起来，「我抗议。我们的私事同您的公务能有什么相干？」

「这可是你自己招的，杜克小姐。你没有对我说出全部实情，我因此起疑。我想要查出你在隐瞒什么，而且不妨告诉你，我一定查得出来。你那么急着见哈林顿先生，究竟是为了什么？」

姑娘显得痛苦万分。

「您定要问个明白，那是为了推迟婚期的事，」她低声说。「您知道，头天晚上我们商量过这事，没商量出个结果来。可睡了一夜，我打定主意赞成推迟，想立刻告诉哈林顿先生。」

===Original===
“But why was it so urgent? Could you not have waited until later in the day?”

“I felt I couldn’t wait. It was so important to us both.”

“And you refuse to give the reason of the postponement?”

“I do. You have no right to ask it.”

“You did meet Mr. Harrington that morning?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
「可为什么那样急？等一等，晚些时候再说不行吗？」

「我觉得等不了。这事对我们两人都太要紧了。」

「推迟的理由，你也拒绝说？」

「拒绝。您无权过问。」

「那天早晨你确实见到了哈林顿先生？」

「是的。」

===Original===
“Where?”

“At the entrance to the Finchley Road tube station.”

“Why did you not tell him to call on you instead of yourself going out?”

“In order as far as possible to prevent him from being late at the office.”

French suddenly remembered that Harrington had entered the office during his visit there on the morning after the crime, and had apologised to Mr. Duke for his late arrival. It had not struck French at the time, but now he recalled that when Mr. Duke had spoken to him of the tragedy he had stated he had heard of it already. Where? French now wondered. Was it merely from the morning paper, or *was it from Miss Duke*? Or, still more pressing question, had they both known of it on the previous night?

===Chinese===
「在哪儿见的？」

「在芬奇利路（Finchley Road）地铁站的入口。」

「你为什么不叫他上你家来，反而自己出去？」

「为了尽量不叫他上班迟到。」

弗伦奇猛然想起，案发次日早晨他去商行的时候，哈林顿恰好进门，还为迟到向杜克先生（Mr. Duke）道了歉。当时他并未在意，此刻却记起，杜克先生同他谈起惨案时，说过自己已经听说了。从哪儿听来的？弗伦奇如今纳闷起来。仅是看了晨报，还是*从杜克小姐口中听来的*？抑或——这问题更逼人——他们两位头天晚上就都已经知道了？

===Original===
Suddenly a possible theory flashed into his mind, and he sat for a few moments in silence, considering it. Suppose that on the stop near Hatton Garden, Harrington had mentioned that he wanted for some purpose to call at the office, or suppose Miss Duke had asked him to do so, and that he had left her for that purpose. Next morning at breakfast she hears from her father of the murder, and is at once panic stricken about Harrington. She sees that if he admits his visit he may be suspected of the crime, and she sends for him before he reaches the office in order to warn him. Or could it be that, knowing of this hypothetical visit, Miss Duke had herself suspected Harrington, and had sent for him at the earliest possible moment to hear his explanation? French was not satisfied with these suggestions, but he felt more than ever certain these two young people had conspired to hide vital information.

===Chinese===
一个可能的推想蓦地闪进他脑际，他默然坐了片刻，细细掂量。设想在哈顿花园近旁停车的时候，哈林顿提过他为了什么事要去一趟商行，或者设想是杜克小姐央他去，他因此撇下了她。第二天早晨吃早饭时，她从父亲口中听得凶案，顿时为哈林顿惊慌失色。她明白，他若承认去过商行，便可能沾上凶手的嫌疑，于是在他到行之前把他唤来，好预先警他一句。又或者，会不会是杜克小姐知道这次假想的造访之后，自家先疑上了哈林顿，这才赶在最早的时机唤他来，听他分说？这两个揣想都不能叫弗伦奇满意，但他比先前更加确信：这两个年轻人串通一气，隐瞒着紧要的情报。

===Original===
He left the house profoundly dissatisfied, and returning to Hatton Garden, had another interview with Harrington. He pressed the young man as hard as he could, taxing him directly with having been present in the office on the fatal night. This Harrington strenuously denied, and French could get nothing further out of him. He went again into the man’s movements on the night of the crime, but without getting any further light thrown thereon. Harrington said he had walked to his rooms after parting from Miss Duke, but no direct evidence was forthcoming as to the truth or falsehood of his statement.

===Chinese===
他怀着满腹的不痛快离开宅子，回到哈顿花园，又同哈林顿谈了一场。他竭力向这年轻人施压，当面指摘他案发当晚到过商行。哈林顿矢口否认，弗伦奇再没能从他口中掏出半点新东西。他把那人案发当晚的行踪又盘问了一遍，依旧不得丝毫新的眉目。哈林顿说，同杜克小姐分手后，他是步行回寓所的，可这话真假与否，并无直接证据可资印证。

===Original===
Suddenly another theory leaped into the detective’s mind, but after careful thought he felt he must reject it. If Vanderkemp were guilty, the whole of these mysterious happenings would be cleared up. Harrington was under a deep debt of gratitude to his uncle, and appeared attached to him. Whether Miss Duke shared, or was endeavouring to share, his feelings, French did not know, but it was certainly possible. Suppose he and Miss Duke, driving home from the East End, had seen Vanderkemp at the end of Hatton Garden. Suppose, moreover, something in the man’s appearance had attracted their attention, something furtive or evil, something unlike his usual expression. This, coupled with the fact that the traveller was supposed to be in Amsterdam, might easily have impelled Harrington to stop the cab to have a word with his uncle. But by the time he had reached the pavement, Vanderkemp had disappeared. The incident would have been dismissed by both as trivial, until next morning at breakfast, when Miss Duke learned of the murder, its significance would become apparent. She might not believe the traveller guilty, but she would recognise that the circumstances required some explanation. Immediately the paramount importance of communicating with Harrington would appear, lest he might incautiously mention that he had seen his uncle virtually on the scene of the murder. She would instantly telephone in the hope of catching her lover before he left his rooms. She could not give her message over the telephone, so she would arrange the meeting. She would instruct Harrington to return to her as soon as possible, so as to hear what had taken place at the office. He would therefore call in the afternoon, and at the interview they would decide that in the uncertainty of the situation, the wedding should be postponed. The supposed flight of Vanderkemp would confirm their suspicions, and would account for the perturbed state of mind which both exhibited.

===Chinese===
蓦地，又一条推想跳进这位侦探的脑海，可经过一番细想，他觉得必须放弃。倘若范德肯普（Vanderkemp）确是凶手，这一连串蹊跷事便件件都有了着落。哈林顿对舅父恩深情重，看来也很依恋他。杜克小姐是否也怀有同样的感情、或正努力培植同样的感情，弗伦奇不得而知，但这自然大有可能。设想他同杜克小姐从伦敦东区（the East End）乘车回家的路上，在哈顿花园尽头望见了范德肯普。再设想此人的神色里有什么东西勾住了他们的注意——某种鬼祟、某种凶恶，某种异于他平日神情的东西。这一点，再加上这位旅行经纪人照理当时远在阿姆斯特丹（Amsterdam）的事实，就足以促使哈林顿停车下去同舅父搭话了。可等他踏上人行道，范德肯普已经不见。起初两人多半只当这段插曲无关紧要，直到第二天早晨吃早饭，杜克小姐听得凶案，它的分量才登时显了出来。她未必相信那旅行经纪人是凶手，却会认识到这情形总得有个解释。于是，火速知会哈林顿一事顷刻变得压倒一切——唯恐他一时口快，说出自己险些在凶案现场撞见舅父的事。她会立刻打电话，指望赶在情人出门之前截住他。电话里话又不便明说，她便会安排会面。她会叮嘱哈林顿尽早回来见她，好听一听商行里出了什么事。所以他到了下午便会登门，两人一见，便会商定：局势未明之下，婚事不如推迟。范德肯普那场疑似潜逃，将坐实他们的疑心，也足以解释两人何以都那般心神不宁。

===Original===
The theory was so fascinating that next day French once more interviewed Harrington and Miss Duke and put the question directly to them, Had they seen Vanderkemp? But both denied having done so, and baffled and irritated, he wrathfully watched another promising clue petering out before him. He had the two young people shadowed, and spent a considerable time in investigating their past life, but without result.

So the days began to draw out into weeks, and the solution of the mystery seemed as far off as ever.

===Chinese===
这套推想太诱人了，第二天弗伦奇再度约谈哈林顿和杜克小姐，把问题径直摆到两人面前：他们可曾见到范德肯普？两人却都矢口否认。弗伦奇又碰壁又着恼，眼看着又一条颇有指望的线索在眼前一点点消散，不由得怒从中来。他派人对这两个年轻人盯梢，又花去不少时日查访他们的既往，却毫无结果。

就这样，一天天拖成了一周周，谜底的揭晓却依旧遥遥无期。

## XII The Elusive Mrs. X / 十二、行踪飘忽的X太太

===Original===
Inspector French put up at a small hotel near the town station, and next morning was early at the White Star offices. There he learned that the *Olympic* was even at that moment coming in, and he went down to the quays and watched the berthing of the monster vessel. It was an impressive experience to see her creep up to her place, manoeuvre into position, and make fast. Then from her gangways began to stream the travellers who, for the better part of a week, had journeyed aboard her. Some were hurrying, already intent on business or anxious to catch trains, others leisurely awaiting taxis and motorcars, some smilingly greeting friends or waving farewells to voyage acquaintances, all drifting gradually away, their places taken by others﻿—and still others.﻿ ﻿… French began to think the exodus would never cease, but at last the crowd diminished, and he pushed his way on board and began a search for the purser. Urgent work in connection with the arrival prevented that busy official from attending to him at once, but he sent a steward to show French to his cabin, and presently joined him there.

“Sorry for keeping you waiting, Inspector,” he apologised. “You want some information about our home trip in late November?”

“Yes,” French answered, and he explained his business and produced Mrs. Root’s marked photographs, concluding, “I want to find out the names and addresses of these eight women, and as much information as possible about them.”

“I’m afraid I could scarcely give you that,” the purser answered. “The records of each trip go ashore at the end of the trip, and I have only those of this present run. But some of the staff might remember the names of the ladies, and if so, you could get their addresses at the office ashore.”

“That would do excellently. I have a copy of the passenger list here, if it would be of any use.”

===Chinese===
弗伦奇探长在市内车站附近一家小旅馆落脚，第二天一早便到了白星公司（White Star）的营业处。他在那里获悉，*奥林匹克号*（Olympic）此时正在进港，便下到码头，观看这艘庞然巨轮靠泊。眼见它缓缓挪到泊位，转身就位，系缆固定，端的是一幕壮观的景象。随后，舷梯上开始涌出一股人流——那些在船上度过了大半个星期的旅客。有的行色匆匆，一心惦着公务或是急着赶火车；有的从容不迫，慢悠悠等着出租车和汽车；有的笑吟吟迎向亲友，或同航程中结识的旅伴挥手作别——人潮渐渐四散，空出的位置又被人填上，再填上……弗伦奇开始觉得这场大迁徙简直没有尽头，好在人群终于稀落下来，他这才挤上船去，寻起事务长（purser）来。船舶进港急务缠身，那位忙碌的官员一时无法抽身接待，便先派了一名侍应生领弗伦奇到自己的舱房里，过了一会儿才赶来。

「对不起，让您久等了，探长，」他致歉道，「您是想打听我们十一月下旬那趟回程航次的事吧？」

「正是，」弗伦奇答道，随即说明来意，取出鲁特夫人做了记号的那叠照片，末了说：「我想查明这八位女士的姓名和地址，有关的详情也多多益善。」

「这个只怕我给不了您，」事务长答道。「每个航次一结束，各项记录就都送上岸了，我手头只有本航次的。不过船上的职员里也许有人记得这几位太太的名字；若真记得，您到岸上营业处就能查到她们的地址。」

「那就再好不过了。我这儿有一份旅客名单的抄本，不知用不用得上。」

===Original===
“Yes, it would be a reminder. Let me see now if I can help you myself, and if not, I think I can put you in the way of getting to know.” He began to scrutinise the photographs.

“That’s Mrs. Root,” French indicated, moving round and looking over the other’s shoulder. “She gave me the names of five, but I should like to check her recollection. The other three she couldn’t remember.”

The purser nodded as he turned the pictures over. “That’s a Mrs. Forbes,” he pointed, “and I rather think that is a Miss Grayson or Graves or some name like that. I remember most of these other faces, but not the people’s names.”

“Mrs. Forbes and Miss Grayson are correct according to Mrs. Root.”

The purser laid down the photographs with the air of quiet decision which seemed characteristic.

“I’m afraid that’s my limit.” He touched a bell. “Ask Mrs. Hope to come here,” he ordered, continuing to French, “Mrs. Hope is the chief stewardess. You can go round with her, and I expect she’ll get you what you want all right.”

===Chinese===
「用得上，好歹是个引子。来，我先看看自己帮不帮得上忙；帮不上，我想我也能给您指点一条问得到的路。」他开始细细端详那些照片。

「这位是鲁特夫人，」弗伦奇绕到对方身后，隔着肩膀指给他看。「她向我提供了五位的名字，不过我想核对一下她的记忆。另外三位她记不起来了。」

事务长一面翻动照片一面点头。「这一位是个福布斯夫人（Mrs. Forbes），」他指了指，「这一位，我隐约记得是位格雷森（Grayson）小姐——要么就是格雷夫斯（Graves），差不离的一个姓。其余这些面孔我大半有印象，只是记不起姓名。」

「照鲁特夫人所说，福布斯夫人和格雷森小姐这两个都不错。」

事务长把照片放下，那副沉着果决的神气，看来正是他的本色。

「恐怕我的本事就到这儿了。」他按了按铃。「请霍普太太到这儿来。」他吩咐道，又对弗伦奇说：「霍普太太（Mrs. Hope）是女侍领班。您可以跟她到各处走走，我想她准能帮您把想要的都问出来。」

===Original===
Mrs. Hope was an efficient-looking woman, who quickly grasped what was required of her. She asked French to accompany her to her sanctum, and there looked over the photographs. She was herself able to identify six of the portraits, and on calling on some of her underlings, the names of the remaining two were speedily forthcoming.

French was glad to find that Mrs. Root’s recollection of the names of her fellow travellers had been correct as far as it had gone, and as he left the great vessel he devoutly hoped that she might have been correct also in her belief that Mrs. X was among the eight women she had indicated. If so, he was well on his way to identify that elusive lady.

He returned to the White Star office and explained that he wanted to know the Christian names, addresses, and other available particulars of the eight women whose names were marked on the passenger list which he handed in, as well as to see a specimen of the handwriting of each.

He realised that the only conclusive test was the handwriting. If one of the eight women wrote the hand of the Mrs. X cheques, he had reached his goal. If not, he determined to go through the declarations of every woman who had crossed on the trip in question in the hope of finding what he sought.

===Chinese===
霍普太太看样子是个干练的女人，很快就弄明白了要她做什么。她请弗伦奇随她到自己那间小屋里，就在那里翻看照片。她本人认出了其中六幅肖像，再把手下几个女侍叫来一问，剩下两位的名字也立时有了着落。

弗伦奇欣然发现，鲁特夫人对自己旅伴姓名的记忆，凡记得的都没有错。他离开这艘巨轮时，一心虔望她的另一个信念也没有错——X太太就在她指认的八位女士之中。倘若如此，要查明这位行踪飘忽的女士，他已是走在正道上了。

他回到白星公司营业处，说明来意：他想了解旅客名单上做了记号的八位女士的教名、地址以及其他可查的详情——说着把名单递了进去——此外还想看一看每人笔迹的样本。

他明白，唯一一锤定音的检验就是笔迹。倘若八位女士中有一人写的正是X太太支票上那笔字，他就算大功告成；倘若没有，他打定主意要把那个航次每位女旅客的登船申报单统统翻检一遍，指望从中找出他要找的东西。

===Original===
The clerk who had been instructed to attend to him brought out a mass of papers. “I wonder,” he said apologetically, “if you would mind looking through these yourself? It is our busy day, and I’ve an awful lot to get through. You see, it’s quite simple. These are the embarkation declarations for the trip, and you can turn up anyone you want quite easily. They are arranged in alphabetical order in the different classes. They’ll give you what you want to know straight off.”

“Right you are,” French declared, delighted thus to get a free hand. “Don’t you bother about me. I’ll peg away, and come and ask you if I get into trouble.”

He “pegged away,” looking up the declaration of each of the eight women, noting the name, address, nationality, and other particulars, and then comparing the handwriting with the signatures on the Mrs. X cheques.

He was not a handwriting expert, but he knew enough about the science to recognise the characteristics which remain unchanged when the writing is disguised. He was, therefore, very patient and thorough in his search, never passing a signature because it looked unlike the model at first sight, but testing each by the rules he had learned, and satisfying himself that it really had been written by a different hand.

===Chinese===
受命接待他的那位职员抱出一大摞文件。「不知，」他带着歉意说，「可否劳您自己去翻一翻？今天是我们最忙的日子，我手头有一大摊子事非办完不可。您瞧，这事儿挺简单：这些都是该航次的登船申报单，您要查谁，一翻就着。各舱等都按姓氏字母顺序排好了，您想知道什么，一看便知。」

「成，」弗伦奇满口应承，正乐得能放手自己去干。「您别管我了。我就这么埋头翻下去，碰上麻烦再来请教。」

他果然「埋头翻下去」，把八位女士的申报单一一查出，记下姓名、地址、国籍及其他详情，再把笔迹同X太太支票上的签名逐一比对。

他不是笔迹鉴定专家，但这门学问他也懂得不少，足以辨认出笔迹经过伪装之后仍保持不变的那些特征。因此他查得极有耐心、极为细致：从不因为某个签名乍看与样本不像便径直放过，而是用学来的规则逐一检验，直到确信那真的出自另一只手为止。

===Original===
He went on without incident until he reached the eighth name on his list. But when he turned to the declaration of Mrs. Ward, the lady whom Mrs. Root had thought the most likely of the lot, he gave a sudden little chuckle of delight. There was the hand of the cheques, the same hand unquestionably, and written without any attempt at disguise! There it was! Mrs. Elizabeth Ward, aged 39, British subject, etc., etc., of Oaklands, Thirsk Road, York. He had reached his goal!

But immediately he was assailed by misgivings, Mrs. Root had thought of Mrs. Ward, but had ruled her out because of her nationality. Mrs. Ward, she had said, was English, while all the people who had seen Mrs. X, seventeen or eighteen persons at least, had agreed she was an American. He would have assumed that Mrs. Root had made a mistake, but for the fact that the declaration said English also. French was puzzled, and he decided that he would go back to the ship and ascertain the views of the staff on the point.

But they all supported Mrs. Root. Mrs. Ward was English; undoubtedly and unquestionably English. The stewards and the stewardesses had some experience on the point, and they guessed they knew. Also he came across the doctor, who, it appeared, had spoken on several occasions to Mrs. Ward, and he was equally positive.

It chanced that as he was leaving the ship he encountered the woman to whom Mrs. Root had advised him to apply, the striking-looking stewardess with dark eyes and white hair, and he stopped and spoke to her.

===Chinese===
一路顺顺当当，直到名单上第八个名字。可当他翻到沃德夫人——鲁特夫人认为最有可能的那一位——的申报单时，他忽然发出一声得意的小笑。正是支票上那笔字，千真万确是同一只手，而且丝毫没有伪装！找着了！伊丽莎白·沃德夫人（Mrs. Elizabeth Ward），39 岁，英国籍，等等，等等，住约克（York）市瑟斯克路（Thirsk Road）奥克兰兹（Oaklands）。他如愿以偿了！

可疑虑随即袭上心头。鲁特夫人想起过沃德夫人，却因国籍问题把她排除了。据她说，沃德夫人是英国人，而所有见过X太太的人——至少十七八个——都一口咬定她是美国人。若不是申报单上也写着英国人，他本要认定是鲁特夫人记岔了。弗伦奇纳闷起来，决定回船上去，问问船员们对此有何说法。

可船员们众口一词，都支持鲁特夫人。沃德夫人是英国人；铁板钉钉、不容置疑的英国人。男女侍应生们在这方面多少有些阅历，自认还看得准。他还撞见了船上的医生——据说医生同沃德夫人攀谈过好几回——也同样言之凿凿。

事有凑巧，正要下船时，他遇见了鲁特夫人建议他去请教的那位女侍，就是那个黑眼睛、白头发、相貌惹眼的妇人，便站住同她说起话来。

===Original===
Unfortunately, she could not tell him very much. She remembered Mrs. Ward, both by name and appearance, though she had not attended to her. But it chanced, nevertheless, that her attention had been specially directed to her because of a certain incident which had taken place towards the end of the voyage. Passing down the corridor while lunch was being served, she had seen the door of one of the cabins in her own charge, open slightly, and a lady appear and glance quickly round, as if to see if she was unobserved. The cabin was occupied by a Mrs. Root, an American, but the lady was this Mrs. Ward. Something stealthy and furtive in her appearance had excited the stewardess’s suspicion, and she had drawn back into another cabin to await developments. Mrs. Ward, evidently satisfied that she was unnoticed, had turned to the dining saloon, and taken her place. The stewardess had kept her eye on her, and after the meal she had seen her go up to Mrs. Root and speak to her, as if reporting the result of her mission. This action had lulled the stewardess’s suspicion, but she had returned to Mrs. Root’s cabin and had had a look round to see if anything had been disturbed. So far as she could see, nothing had, nor had Mrs. Root made any complaint about her things having been interfered with.

If further confirmation of his suspicions were needed, French felt that this episode supplied it. Doubtless Mrs. Ward was amassing information as to the other’s clothes and belongings to assist her in her impersonation. Perhaps also she was photographing envelopes or other documents of which to prepare forgeries in case of need.

There still remained the difficulty of her nationality. Obviously it is easy to mimic the accent and manner of a foreigner, but French found it hard to believe that such mimicry could be so perfect as to deceive a large number of persons, many of whom were experts on that particular point. This, however, was only a small part of the general problem, and did not affect his next business, to find Mrs. Elizabeth Ward, Thirsk Road, York.

===Chinese===
遗憾的是，她说不出多少东西。沃德夫人的名字和长相她都记得，虽说没伺候过她。不过凑巧的是，因为航程末尾发生的一桩小事，她当时对这位夫人格外留了心。那天供应午饭的当口，她正沿走廊走过，看见自己分管的一间客舱的门开了条缝，一位太太探出身来，飞快四下一扫，像是看有没有人注意她。那舱里住的是位美国太太鲁特夫人，可探头出来的却是这位沃德夫人。她那副鬼祟躲闪的神气引起了女侍的疑心，她便退进另一间客舱，静观其变。沃德夫人显然确信无人留意，便转身进了餐厅，落了座。女侍一直拿眼睛盯着她，饭后又见她走到鲁特夫人跟前说话，像是在禀报差事的结果。这一来女侍的疑心倒是消了，可她还是回到鲁特夫人的舱里，四下查看有没有东西被动过。就她所见，什么也没动过，鲁特夫人也从未投诉说自己的东西遭了人翻弄。

倘若弗伦奇的怀疑还需要进一步的佐证，他觉得这段插曲恰好补上了。毋庸置疑，沃德夫人当时是在摸对方的衣着物件等底细，好为自己的冒名顶替做准备。说不定她还在拍摄信封或其他文书，以备日后伪造之需。

国籍的疑团依然未解。模仿外国人的口音做派，固然不算难事，可要说这模仿竟能逼真到瞒过一大群人——其中好几位还是这方面的行家——弗伦奇实在难以相信。不过这只是整个疑案里的一小桩，并不耽误他的下一件事：去找约克市瑟斯克路的伊丽莎白·沃德夫人。

===Original===
He went ashore, and, turning into a telegraph office, sent a wire to the chief of police at York, asking him if a lady of that name lived at the address in question and, if so, to wire was she at home.

His next business was at police headquarters, and thither he was directing his steps when a thought struck him, and he turned aside to the sheds in which the transatlantic luggage is examined. Several of the customs officers were still there, and he went up and spoke to one of them.

“Now,” the young fellow answered in surprise, “it’s a darned queer thing that you came to me about that. Quite a coincidence, that is. I know the man who went through those trunks. He told me about it at the time. It seemed a darned silly thing that anyone should want to bring trunks of blankets from America. If you come along I’ll find him for you. And so the lady’s wanted, is she? Say, Jack!” he called a colleague, another clean, efficient young fellow of the same type, “here’s someone wants you. He wants to know about those trunks of blankets you were telling me about two or three trips of the *Olympic* back. A darned queer coincidence that he should come to me about them. That’s what I call it!”

“Yes, you’ve made a lucky shot, haven’t you?” the second man said to French. “I remember the trunks and the lady they belonged to, because I couldn’t understand why anyone should want to bring trunks of blankets across the Atlantic. I’ve never known anyone do it before.”

===Chinese===
他上了岸，拐进一家电报局，给约克警察局长拍了封电报，问该地址是否住着这么一位太太；若有，再请回电告知她可在家。

他接下来要去的是警察总局，正朝那儿走着，忽然想到一件事，便折进那几座查验越洋行李的海关棚屋。几名关员还在那里，他走上前去同其中一个攀谈。

「嘿，」那年轻人吃了一惊，答道，「这事儿您找到我头上，可真是邪门儿了。太巧了，就是太巧了。经手查验那几口衣箱的人我认识，他当时就跟我讲起过。我说，有人偏要从美国运整箱的毯子回来，这不是天大的傻事么。您跟我来，我领您找他去。这么说，那位太太正被通缉着，是吧？喂，杰克（Jack）！」他唤住一个同事——又是个干净利落、同一路数的年轻人——「这儿有人找你。他想打听*奥林匹克号*两三个航次之前你讲过的那几箱毯子。他这事儿偏偏找到我头上，真是邪门儿透顶。我只能说：太巧了！」

「可不是，您这一炮算是打响了，」第二个人对弗伦奇说。「那几口箱子连同箱子的主人我都记得，因为我实在闹不懂，谁会想运几箱毯子横渡大西洋。这种事我以前可从来没见过。」

===Original===
“You didn’t make any remark about them,” French asked.

“No, but she did. She said she reckoned I hadn’t often seen trunks of blankets brought over from America. You see, I was a bit suspicious at first, and was examining the things pretty carefully. I said that was so, and she said she was taking back a small but valuable collection of porcelain ornaments, which she would pack in the blankets, and that when she had to bring the trunks anyway, she thought she might as well bring the packing as well and so save buying new. I thought the whole business a bit off, but there was nothing dutiable in the case, and it wasn’t my job to interfere. Is there anything wrong about it?”

“I don’t know,” French told him. “I think the woman was a crook, but I’m not on to the blanket stunt yet. By the way, is she in one of those groups?”

The young man identified Mrs. Ward without hesitation, and French, finding he had learned all that the customs men could tell him, resumed his way to the police station.

He wondered what this blanket business really did mean. Then as he walked slowly along with head bent forward and eyes vacantly scanning the pavement, a possible explanation occurred to him. These trunks, apparently, were required solely as properties to assist in the fraud. Mrs. Root, the wife of a Pittsburg magnate, would scarcely arrive at the Savoy from America without American trunks. But when Mrs. Root came to disappear, the trunks would become an embarrassment. They would have to be got rid of, and, as a matter of fact, they were got rid of. They must therefore contain nothing of the lady’s, no personal possession which might act as a clue to its owner. But they must contain something. Empty trunks would be too light, and might be observed by the chambermaid, and comments might be occasioned among the hotel staff which might reach the management, and which would become important if Mr. Williams rang up to make his inquiries. But blankets would exactly fill the bill; indeed, French could think of nothing more suitable for the purpose. They would give the trunks a moderate weight, they would not supply a clue to Mrs. Ward, and they would be cheap, while their presence could be accounted for sufficiently reasonably to the customs officers. Yes, French thought, it was a probable enough explanation.

===Chinese===
「您当时没就这事说什么吧？」弗伦奇问。

「我没说，她倒说了。她说，我料你也不常见人从美国往回运整箱的毯子吧。您知道，我起先有点犯疑，把那些东西查得相当仔细。我说正是这么回事，她就说，她这趟要带回去一批瓷器小摆件，件头不大却挺值钱，打算拿毯子当包装裹在里面；又说反正箱子非带不可，索性连包装材料也一并带上，省得再买新的。我总觉得这事儿有点不对味，可箱内并无应税之物，逾份过问也不是我的差事。怎么，这里头有毛病？」

「不好说，」弗伦奇告诉他。「我看这女人是个骗子，不过毯子这手把戏我还没琢磨透。对了，她在这几组照片里吗？」

那年轻人毫不犹豫地认出了沃德夫人。弗伦奇见海关这几位能告诉他的已经问尽了，便重新上路，往警察局去了。

他一路琢磨，这毯子勾当到底是什么名堂。他低着头慢慢走，两眼茫然地扫着路面，忽然想出了一个说得通的解释。这几口箱子，看来压根就是骗局里的一件道具。堂堂匹兹堡巨商的夫人鲁特太太，从美国远道而来住进萨伏伊饭店，总不至于连一口美国衣箱都不带吧。可等到「鲁特夫人」要销声匿迹的时候，这几口箱子就成了累赘，非处理掉不可——事实上它们也的确被处理掉了。因此箱子里不能装任何属于这位太太的东西，不能有一件能指认主人的随身物件。可又总得装点什么。空箱子太轻，容易叫客房女侍看出破绽，旅馆仆役间难免生出议论，传到经理耳朵里；一旦威廉斯先生打电话来查问，这些议论就成了要紧的东西。而毯子恰恰样样合用；说实在的，弗伦奇想不出比它更合适的了。分量给箱子压得不轻不重，不会给沃德夫人留下半点线索，价钱又便宜，而且在关员面前也完全解释得通。是啊，弗伦奇想，这个解释八九不离十。

===Original===
Arrived at the police station, he sent in his name with a request to see the officer in charge.

Superintendent Hayes had been stationed in London before he got his present appointment, and had come across French on more than one occasion. He therefore greeted the Inspector cordially, found him a comfortable chair, and supplied him with an excellent cigar.

“From Trinidad,” he explained. “I get them direct from a man I know out there. And what’s the best news of you?”

They discussed old times for some minutes, then French turned to the business in hand.

“It’s an interesting case,” he said as he gave the other the details, continuing, “The woman must be a pretty cool hand. She could easily invent that tale about losing her passport, for old Williams’s edification, but under the circumstances her coming to you about it was a bit class.”

“She had a nerve, yes,” the Superintendent admitted. “But, you see, it was necessary. She must have known that the absence of the passport would strike Williams as suspicious, and it was necessary for her to remove that suspicion. She couldn’t very well get a bag of that kind stolen without informing the police, so she had to inform them. She would see how easily Williams could check her statement, as indeed he did. No, I don’t see how she could have avoided coming to us. It was an obvious precaution.”

===Chinese===
到了警察局，他通报姓名，请求面见主管官员。

海斯警司（Superintendent Hayes）调任现职之前曾在伦敦任职，同弗伦奇打过不止一回交道，因此热络地招呼这位探长，替他寻了把舒服的椅子，又敬上一支上好的雪茄。

「特立尼达（Trinidad）货，」他解释道。「我在那边有个熟人，直接从他手里弄来的。您这一向可好，有什么新鲜事？」

两人叙了几分钟旧，随后弗伦奇便转到了正题上。

「这案子有意思，」他把详情向对方讲述了一遍，接着说道，「这女人准是个沉得住气的大胆角色。丢了护照那套鬼话，她编起来不费吹灰之力，也好教老威廉斯深信不疑；可在那种情形之下，她竟跑到您这儿来报案，这份气度着实够瞧的。」

「她够有种的，这不假，」警司承认道。「可是您想，这一步非走不可。她一定明白，护照一丢难免叫威廉斯起疑，这层疑心必须设法打消。那样一只提包遭了窃，她总不能不报警——不报不行，索性就报了。她也料得到威廉斯核查她那套说辞有多容易，事实上他后来也果然查了。是啊，要我说，她不到我们这儿来反倒不行。这分明是一步明摆着的预防措施。」

===Original===
“I quite agree with all you say,” French returned, “but it argues a cool customer for all that; not only, so to speak, putting her head into the lion’s mouth, but at the same time calling his attention to its being there. Anyway, I’ve got to find her, and I wish you’d let me have details about her. I’ve got some from the *Olympic* people, but I want to pick up everything I can.”

The Superintendent telephoned to someone to “send up Sergeant McAfee,” and when a tall, cadaverous man entered, he introduced him as the man who had dealt with the business in question.

“Sergeant McAfee has just been transferred to us from Liverpool,” he explained. “Sit down, McAfee. Inspector French wants to know some details about that woman who lost her handbag coming off the *Olympic* some seven weeks ago. I think you handled the thing. Do you remember a Mrs. Root of Pittsburg?”

“I mind her rightly, sir,” the man answered in what French believed was a Belfast accent. “But it wasn’t coming off the *Olympic* she lost it. It was later on that same day, though it was on the quays right enough.”

“Tell us all you can about it.”

The Sergeant pulled out his notebook. “I have it in me other book,” he announced. “If ye’ll excuse me, I’ll get it.”

===Chinese===
「您说的我完全赞同，」弗伦奇应道，「可这恰恰说明她是个镇定得可怕的主顾；打个比方，她不光把脑袋伸进了狮子嘴里，还顺带提醒狮子一声：脑袋在这儿呢。且不说这个，我总归得找到她，还请您把她一案的详情提供给我。*奥林匹克号*那边我已经问到一些，但能捞的，我想一点不剩全捞上来。」

警司打了电话，吩咐「让麦卡菲警士（Sergeant McAfee）上来」，等一个瘦高干瘪的男人进了门，便介绍说他就是经手那桩事的人。

「麦卡菲警士刚从利物浦调到我们这儿，」他解释道。「坐吧，麦卡菲。弗伦奇探长想打听一些细节，是关于约七个星期前从*奥林匹克号*下船时丢了手提包的那位太太的。我记得是你经手的。你可记得一位匹兹堡来的鲁特太太？」

「我记得真真儿的，长官，」那人答道，一口弗伦奇听来像是贝尔法斯特的口音。「不过她不是下*奥林匹克号*的时候丢的，是当天晚些时候，不过也确确实实是在码头上。」

「把你记得的全说出来。」

警士掏出他的笔记本。「记在我另一本里了，」他声明道。「诸位稍待，我去取来。」

===Original===
In a moment he returned, sat down, and turning over the dog’s-eared pages of a well-worn book, began as if reciting evidence in court:

“On the 24th November last at about 3:00 p.m., I was passing through the crowd on the outer quays when I heard a woman cry out. ‘Thief, thief,’ she shouted, and she ran up and caught me by the arm. She was middling tall and thinnish, her face pale and her hair dark. She spoke in an American voice, and seemed upset or excited. She said to me, breathless like, ‘Say, officer,’ she said, ‘I’ve just had my despatch case stolen.’ I asked her where, and how, and what was in it. She said right there where we were standing, and not three seconds before. She was carrying it in her hand, and it was snatched out of it. She turned round and saw a man juke away in the crowd. She shouted and made after him, but he was away before she could get near. I asked her what the case was like, and she said a small square brown morocco leather one with gold fittings. I went and told the two men on duty close by, and we kept a watch on the exits, but we never saw a sign of it.” Sergeant McAfee shook his head gloomily as he concluded. “She hadn’t any call to be carrying a gold fitted case in that crowd anyway.”

“That’s a fact, Sergeant,” the Superintendent agreed. “And you never came on any trace of it?”

“No, sir. I brought her up to the station, and took her name and all particulars. There’s the report.” He unfolded a paper and laid it on the Superintendent’s desk.

In the document was a detailed description of the lady, of the alleged despatch case and its contents, and of the means that had been taken to try to trace it. The pawnbrokers had been advised and a special watch kept on fences and other usual channels for the disposal of stolen goods.

===Chinese===
转眼他就回来了，坐下，翻动那本用旧了的册子里卷角的书页，像在法庭上宣读证词似的开了腔：

「去年 11 月 24 日下午 3:00 许，我正打外码头的人群里穿行，忽听得一个女人喊叫起来。『抓贼啊，抓贼』，她嚷着，跑上来一把抓住我的胳膊。她中等偏高个儿，身量偏瘦，脸色发白，头发深色。她一口美国腔，显得心慌意乱，或是激动难抑。她气喘吁吁地对我说：『哎，警官，』她说，『我的文件包刚才叫人偷了。』我问她是在哪儿、怎么丢的，里头有什么。她说就在我们站的地方，前后不出三秒钟。那包她原是拎在手里的，一下叫人从手里夺了去。她一转身，看见一个男人一头扎进人群溜了。她喊着追上去，可还没等她挨近，人早没影儿了。我问她那包什么样，她说是一只小小的方形棕色摩洛哥皮包，配着镀金配件。我过去告知了就近值班的两个人，我们把各处出口都盯住了，可连个影子也没见着。」麦卡菲警士说完，阴沉沉地摇了摇头。「再说，她也不该在那种人堆里拎一只配金件儿的皮包。」

「这话不假，警士，」警司附和道。「后来就再没寻见那包的一点踪迹？」

「没有了，长官。我把她带回局里，登记了姓名和一切详情。报告就在这儿。」他摊开一页纸，放在警司的桌上。

文件里详细载明了那位太太的模样、报案所称文件包及其内盛物品，以及为追寻此包所采取的种种手段。各家当铺均已获知案情，对销赃贩子和处置赃物的惯常门路也特别布了监视。

===Original===
When French had digested these particulars, he brought out once more his photographs and handed them to the Sergeant.

“Look at those, Sergeant, and tell me if you see the woman among them.”

Slowly the Sergeant turned them over, gazing at them in precisely the same puzzled way as had done Mr. Williams, Mr. Scarlett, and the other London men to whom they had been shown. And with the same doubt and hesitation he presently fixed on Mrs. Ward.

“That would have to be her,” he declared slowly, “that is, if she’s there at all. It isn’t a good likeness, but I believe it’s her all the same.”

“You wouldn’t swear to her?”

“I’d hardly. But I believe it’s her for all that.”

===Chinese===
弗伦奇把这些详情消化过后，再次取出照片，递给警士。

「瞧瞧这些，警士，告诉我您见过的那个女人在不在里面。」

警士慢慢翻看，那副困惑的神情，同威廉斯先生、斯卡利特先生以及伦敦其他看过照片的人一模一样。而他也带着同样的迟疑与踌躇，末了认定了沃德夫人。

「要说有，那就得是她，」他缓缓断言道，「就是说，倘使她真在这一堆里的话。这照片不大像，可我信就是她。」

「您不肯对她起誓作证？」

「我不敢起这个誓。可我还是信是她。」

===Original===
French nodded. The Sergeant’s statement, agreeing as it did with those of Messrs. Williams, Scarlett and Co., seemed capable of but one explanation. Mrs. X was Mrs. Ward all right, but before meeting these men she had made herself up to impersonate Mrs. Root. They saw a likeness to Mrs. Ward because it really was she, but they were doubtful because she was disguised.

The Inspector leaned forward and tapped the photograph.

“Put it this way, Sergeant,” he suggested. “Here is a picture of the lady as she really is. When you saw her she was made up to look like another woman. How’s that, do you think?”

In Sergeant McAfee’s lacklustre eye there shone a sudden gleam. “That’s just what it is, sir,” he answered with an approach to something almost like interest in his manner. “That’s it and no mistake. She’s like the photograph by her features, but not by her makeup.” He nodded his head several times in appreciation.

“Very good.” Inspector French invariably liked as many strings to his bow as he could get. “Now I want some hint from you that will help me trace her.”

===Chinese===
弗伦奇点了点头。警士这份证词同威廉斯、斯卡利特等诸位先生的说法正好互相印证，看来只有一种解释讲得通。X太太确实就是沃德夫人，只是在见到这些人的时候，她已经化装成了鲁特夫人的模样。他们觉得像沃德夫人，因为那本来就是她；他们拿不准，则因为她经过了化装。

探长俯身向前，点了点那张照片。

「这么想好不好，警士，」他提示道。「这张照片是这位太太本来的面目。您见到她时，她已化装成另一个女人的样子。您看这个说法如何？」

麦卡菲警士那双无神的眼睛里倏地闪过一点亮光。「正是这么回事儿，长官，」他答道，神气里透出一丝近乎兴致的东西。「就是它，错不了。论眉眼五官，她像这张照片；论那张脸的化装，她可不像。」他连连点头，深以为然。

「很好。」弗伦奇探长向来喜欢手里的弓多备几根弦。「现在，我想请您给点提示，好帮我把她追查出来。」

===Original===
But this was just what Sergeant McAfee could not supply. The woman had given two addresses, the Savoy in London and Mrs. Root’s home in Pittsburg. There was no help in either, and no other information was forthcoming.

He lunched with his friend the Superintendent, afterwards withdrawing to the lounge of his hotel to have a quiet smoke and to think things over.

While he sat there, a page appeared with a telegram. It was a reply from the police at York and read:

> “Your wire. No one of that name or address known.”

French swore disgustedly. He had, of course, realised that the name might be false, but yet he had hoped against hope that he might really have reached the end of at least this portion of his quest. But here he was, as far from the truth as ever! He would now have to make a fresh start to trace this elusive lady﻿—he used another adjective in his mind﻿—and he couldn’t see that he was any better equipped for the search now than when he had started out from Mr. Williams’s office. It was a confoundedly exasperating case﻿—just bristling with promising clues which one after another petered out as he came to follow them up. Being on it was like trying to cross a stream on stepping-stones which invariably gave way when he came to place his weight on them. It was an annoying thought also that that would scarcely be the view his chief would take of the matter. The chief had not been over-complimentary already in his comments on his handling of the case, and French felt that he would view this new check in anything but a sympathetic spirit.

===Chinese===
可这恰恰是麦卡菲警士给不了的东西。那女人留了两个地址：伦敦的萨伏伊饭店，和鲁特太太在匹兹堡的宅邸。两条线索都帮不上忙，别的消息也再没有一条。

他同警司朋友共进了午餐，饭后退回自己旅馆的休息厅，想安安静静抽袋烟，把前前后后再想一遍。

他正坐在那里，一个旅馆小听差送来一封电报。那是约克警方的回电，写道：

> 「来电收悉。查无此人此址。」

弗伦奇懊恼地骂了一句。他当然明白那名字可能是假的，可他仍抱着万一之望，指望自己至少在这一段追查上真的走到了头。瞧瞧现在，离真相还是同原先一样远！这下他只得从头再来，去追寻这位飘忽难捉的太太——他在心里用的是另一个字眼——而且他看不出，比起当初从威廉斯先生办公室出发的时候，自己眼下多了半分追查的家什。这案子邪门得叫人冒火——满眼都是看似大有可为的线索，可一条条追下去，又一条条断成死路。办这案子，就像踩着踏脚石过河，石头偏偏在你一踩上去的时候就往下翻。更叫他窝心的是，顶头上司对这桩事多半不会是这个看法。上司先前评论他办案的手法时，话里就已经不大客气，弗伦奇料定，对这次的新挫折，他绝不会有什么体谅的神色。

===Original===
However, grousing about it wouldn’t lead anywhere, and with an effort he switched his thoughts back to his problem. As he thought it over a further point occurred to him.

Since his first visit to the Savoy he had wondered why the lady had turned up there so much later than the other passengers from the *Olympic*, and now he saw the reason. The episode of the handbag had taken place some four hours after the vessel’s arrival, long after the special boat train had left. Mrs. X﻿—for she was still Mrs. X﻿—must therefore have travelled up by an afternoon train, probably the 5:26 or 6:22 p.m. from the West Station, which got in 6:58 and 8:20 respectively. Now, why this delay? What had she done during these four hours?

The answer was not far to seek. Was it not to give her time and opportunity to assume her disguise? He felt it must be so.

The lady was her natural self﻿—other than in name﻿—on board the *Olympic*, and having no opportunity to alter her appearance, she had passed through the customs in the same character. Hence the ship’s staff and the customs officer had instantly recognised her photograph. But it was obvious that her impersonation of Mrs. Root must begin before she interviewed the Southampton police, and that accounted for the hesitation of Sergeant McAfee and the people in London in identifying her. She had therefore made herself up between passing through the customs at, say, eleven o’clock, and calling on the Sergeant at three. Where was she during those four hours?

===Chinese===
不过，发牢骚终究无济于事，他强打精神，把心思拉回到问题上来。想着想着，又有一个疑点冒了出来。

打从初次走访萨伏伊饭店起，他就纳闷那位太太为何比*奥林匹克号*的其他旅客晚了那么多才露面，如今他明白了个中缘故。手提包一案发生在船进港之后约四个钟头，那时专用的船客列车早已开走许久。X太太——因为彼时她还是X太太——势必是搭午后的火车上的伦敦，多半是西站（West Station）下午 5:26 或 6:22 的车，两班分别于 6:58 和 8:20 抵达。那么，为何耽搁这半日？这四个钟头她做什么去了？

答案不远。这不正是为了给自己留出时间和机会化装易容么？他断定必是如此。

在*奥林匹克号*上，这位太太——除了名字——乃是本来面目，一路没有机会改换形容，过海关时也是同一副模样。因此船上的职员和那位关员一眼就认出了她的照片。可她冒充鲁特夫人，显然必须赶在面见南安普敦警方之前动手，这也正说明了麦卡菲警士和伦敦诸位为何在指认时满腹迟疑。如此说来，她是在——姑且算十一点钟——通过海关，到三点钟去见警士之间的这段工夫里完成的化装。这四个钟头，她人在哪儿？

===Original===
He put himself in her place. Confronted with her problem, what would he have done?

Gone to a hotel, unquestionably. Taken a room in which to assume the disguise. Had Mrs. X engaged a bedroom in one of the Southampton hotels for that afternoon?

As he thought over the thing, further probabilities occurred to him. The lady would go up to her bedroom as one person and come down as another. Therefore, surely, the larger the hotel, the less chance of the transformation being observed. One of a crowd, she would go to the reception office and engage a room for a few hours’ rest, and pay for it then and there. Then, having accomplished the makeup, she would slip out, unobserved in the stream of passersby. Yes, French felt sure he was on the right track, and, with a fresh accession of energy, he jumped to his feet, knocked out his pipe, and left the building.

He called first at the South Western and made his inquiries. But here he drew blank. At the Dolphin he had no better luck, but at the Polygon he found what he wanted. After examining the records, the reception clerk there was able to recall the transaction. About midday an American lady had come in, and saying she wanted a few hours’ rest before catching the 5:26 to London, had engaged a bedroom on a quiet floor until that hour. She had registered, and French, on looking up the book, was delighted to find once more the handwriting of the lady of the cheques. It was true that on this occasion she figured as Mrs. Silas R. Clamm, of Hill Drive, Boston, Mass.; but knowing what he knew of her habits, French would have been surprised to have found a name he had seen before.

===Chinese===
他设身处地替她想了想。换了是他，面对她那道难题，会怎么办？

上旅馆，这毫无疑问。开个房间，好在里面化装。X太太那天下午，会不会就在南安普敦某家旅馆里开过一间卧室？

这么一路想下去，更多的情况又浮上心头。这位太太上楼进卧室是一个人，下楼出来就得是另一个人。如此说来，旅馆越大，这场改头换面被人瞧破的机会自然越小。混在人堆里，她到接待处开个房间，只说是歇几个钟头，当场把钱付清。等化装完毕，便趁着过往人流，神不知鬼不觉地溜出去。不错，弗伦奇确信自己这条路走对了，于是精神一振，霍地站起身，磕净烟斗，离开了旅馆。

他先到西南旅馆（the South Western）打听，扑了个空。海豚旅馆（the Dolphin）也没交上好运，可在波利贡旅馆（the Polygon），他找到了要找的东西。查阅过记录，那里的接待职员记起了这桩交易。晌午前后，一位美国太太来到店里，说要在赶 5:26 去伦敦的火车之前歇上几个钟头，便在清静的一层楼面开了间卧室，用到开车为止。她登了记，弗伦奇翻开登记簿一查，喜出望外——又是支票女主人的那笔字！不错，这一回她署名希拉斯·R·克莱姆夫人（Mrs. Silas R. Clamm），住址马萨诸塞州波士顿市希尔大道（Hill Drive, Boston, Mass.）；不过，既已领教过她惯换名字的做派，倘若这次翻出个见过的名字，弗伦奇倒要奇怪了。

===Original===
At first he was delighted at so striking a confirmation of his theory, but as he pursued his inquiries his satisfaction vanished, and once more depression and exasperation swept over him. For the reception clerk could not remember anything more than the mere fact of the letting of the room, and no one else in the building remembered the woman at all. With his usual pertinacity, he questioned all who might have come in contact with her, but from none of them did he receive the slightest help. That Mrs. X had made herself up at the hotel for her impersonation stunt was clear, but unfortunately it was equally clear that she had vanished from the building without leaving any trace.

The worst of the whole business was that he didn’t see what more he could do. The special clues upon which he had been building had failed him, and he felt there was now nothing for it but to fall back on the general one of the photographs. One of the portraits was excellently clear as to details, and he decided he would have an enlargement made of Mrs. X, and circulate it among the police in the hope that some member at some time might recognise the lady. Not a very hopeful method certainly, but all he had left.

He took an evening train from the West Station, and a couple of hours afterwards reached his home, a thoroughly tired and disgruntled man.

===Chinese===
起初，他为自己那套推断得到如此有力的印证而大为欣喜，可再查下去，这份满意便烟消云散，沮丧和恼火又一次涌上心头。因为那位接待职员除了开房这一桩，别的什么都不记得，楼里再没有一个人记得这个女人。他拿出素来的锲而不舍，把可能同她照过面的所有人挨个问遍，却没有从谁嘴里得到半点帮助。X太太在这家旅馆里化好了装、备齐了冒名顶替的行头，这一点清楚了；不幸的是，她离开这幢楼时没留下半点痕迹，这一点也同样清楚了。

这桩事情最糟的是，他看不出自己还有什么别的招数。他一路倚仗的那几条专门线索全都靠不住了，眼下他觉得，除了退而依靠照片这条泛泛的线索，再无别法。有一张肖像眉目细节格外清楚，他决定把X太太这一张放大了，在警界四处传发，指望哪位警员有朝一日能认出这位太太。这法子固然希望不大，却也是他仅剩的全部了。

他从西站搭上傍晚的火车，两个钟头后到了家——一身的疲惫，一肚皮的窝火。

## II The Firm of Duke and Peabody / 二、杜克与皮博迪商行

===Original===
When Inspector French ushered the clerk, Orchard, into the inner office, they found Mr. Duke pacing the floor with an expression of utter mystification imprinted on his features.
“I say, Inspector, here’s a puzzle,” he cried. “I happened to look behind the safe door, and I find it has been opened with a key. I thought at first it had been broken or forced or the lock somehow picked. But I see it is unlocked.”
“Yes, I noticed that, sir,” French answered. “But I don’t follow you. What is the mystery about that?”
“Why, the key, of course. To my certain knowledge there were only two keys in existence. One I keep on my ring, which is chained to my belt and never leaves me day nor night. There it is. The other is lodged with my bankers, where no one could possibly get at it. Now, where did the thief get the key that is now in the lock?”
“That is one of the things we have to find out,” French replied. “You may perhaps think it strange, but a point of that kind, which at first seems to deepen the mystery, often proves a blessing in disguise. It provides another point of attack, you understand, and frequently it narrows down the area of inquiry. You haven’t touched the key, I hope?”
“No. I remembered what you said about finger prints.”

===Chinese===
弗伦奇探长把职员奥查德（Orchard）让进里间办公室时，两人只见杜克先生（Mr. Duke）正在屋里来回踱步，满脸一片大惑不解的神色。
「我说探长，这里头有个谜，」他嚷道。「我碰巧往保险柜门后一瞧，发现它是拿钥匙开的。起初我还只当柜门是叫人砸开、撬开的，再不然就是锁让人做了手脚。可我看得出来，锁是好端端开着的。」
「是的，先生，这一点我注意到了，」弗伦奇答道。「不过我没跟上您的意思。这有什么蹊跷？」
「嘿，当然是那把钥匙。据我所知，天底下统共只有两把。一把拴在我的钥匙环上，钥匙环又拿链子系在腰带上，日夜不离我身——喏，就在这儿。另一把存在我的银行里，谁也休想碰到。那么请问，如今插在锁上的这把钥匙，贼是从哪儿弄来的？」
「这正是我们要查个明白的事情之一，」弗伦奇答道。「您或许觉得奇怪，可是这一类细节，起初看似让谜团愈发难解，末了往往是因祸得福。您晓得，它多开出一条进攻的路子，而且常常能把查探的范围收窄。钥匙您没碰过吧，我希望？」
「没有。您交代过的指纹一节，我记得。」

===Original===
“Good. Now, gentlemen, if you will please sit down, I want to ask you a few questions. I’ll take you first, Mr. Orchard. I have your name, and your address is Bloomsbury Square. Now tell me, is that your home?”
The young fellow answered the questions without hesitation, and French noted approvingly his direct glance and the evident candour with which he spoke. The Bloomsbury Square address, it appeared, was that of a boarding house, the clerk’s home being in Somerset. He had left the office at about half-past five that afternoon, Mr. Gething being then almost ready to follow. Mr. Gething was usually the last out of the office. Orchard had noticed nothing unusual in his manner that day, though for the last two or three weeks he had seemed somewhat moody and depressed. Orchard had gone from the office to Liverpool Street, where he had caught the 5:52 to Ilford. There he had had supper with a friend, a man called Forrest, a clerk in a shipping office in Fenchurch Street. He had left about 9:30, getting back to town a little before 10:00. The rain had stopped, and as he did not get as much exercise as he could have wished, he resolved to walk home from the station. Hatton Garden was but little out of his way, and as he approached it he remembered that he had left in his desk a book he had changed at the library at lunch time. He had decided to call in and get it, so as to read for a while before going to sleep. He had done so, and had found Mr. Gething’s body, as he had already explained. The outer street door had been closed, and he had opened it with his latch key. Both the office doors were open, that between the landing and the outer office and that of Mr. Duke’s room. The lights were on everywhere, except that in the outer office only the single central bulb was burning, the desk lamps being off. He had seen no one about the offices.
French, having complimented the young fellow on his clear statement, bade him good night and sent him home. But as he passed out of the room he whispered to one of his men, who promptly nodded and also disappeared. French turned to Mr. Duke.
“That seems a straightforward young fellow,” he observed. “What is your opinion of him?”
“Absolutely straightforward.” The acting partner spoke with decision. “He has been with me for over four years, and I have always found him most conscientious and satisfactory. Indeed. I have been very fortunate in my whole staff. I think I could say the same of them all.”

===Chinese===
「很好。那么二位请坐，我要问几个问题。先从您问起，奥查德先生。大名我已知道，住址是布卢姆斯伯里广场（Bloomsbury Square）。那么请问，那是您府上么？」
这小伙子问一句答一句，毫不含糊，弗伦奇赞许地注意到他目光坦率，言语间一望而知全是实情。据他所说，布卢姆斯伯里广场那处住址是一家供膳宿的公寓，他的本家在萨默塞特（Somerset）。当天下午五点半左右他下班离行，格辛先生（Mr. Gething）那时也差不多就要跟着走了。平日里总是格辛先生最后一个离开办公室。那天格辛的神态并无异样，只是近两三个星期以来，他显得有些郁郁不乐。奥查德出了办公室便去利物浦街（Liverpool Street），赶上 5:52 开往伊尔福德（Ilford）的那班车。在那边他同一位朋友吃了晚饭——那人叫福雷斯特（Forrest），是芬丘奇街（Fenchurch Street）一家航运公司的职员。约 9:30 他动身返回，不到 10:00 便到了城里。雨已经停了，他平日里难得活动，便决意从车站一路步行回家。哈顿花园（Hatton Garden）顺路多不了几步，快走到时，他想起中午在图书馆换的一本书还落在办公桌里，便决定进去取了，好在睡前读上一阵。他进去之后，便发现了格辛先生的尸首，这一节他已经交代过了。临街的外门关着，是他拿弹簧钥匙开的。两道办公室门都敞着，一道是楼梯平台通外间的，一道是杜克先生那间的。各处的灯都亮着，只有外间单剩中央那一盏灯点着，写字台上的台灯都熄了。办公室左近，他一个人影也没见。
弗伦奇夸这小伙子陈述得条理分明，同他道了晚安，打发他回家去了。但他走出房门时，凑到一个手下耳边低声嘱咐了一句，那人会意地点点头，也随即不见了。弗伦奇这才转向杜克先生。
「这小伙子看着是个爽直人，」他说。「您对他怎么看？」
「爽直得没有二话。」这位主持行务的股东说得斩钉截铁。「他跟了我四年多，我一向觉得他办事尽心，样样妥帖。不瞒您说，我这一班人手都称心，可以说个个如此。」

===Original===
“I congratulate you, Mr. Duke. Perhaps now you would tell me something about your firm and your various employees.”
Mr. Duke, though still extremely agitated, was controlling his emotion and answered in calm tones.
“The business is not a large one, and at the present time is virtually controlled by myself. Peabody, though not so old as I am, has been troubled by bad health and has more or less gone to pieces. He seldom comes to the office, and never undertakes any work. The junior partner, Sinnamond, is travelling in the East, and has been for some months. We carry on the usual trade of diamond merchants, and have a small branch establishment in Amsterdam. Indeed, I divide my own time almost equally between London and Amsterdam. We occupy only these two rooms which you have seen. Our staff in the outer office consists, or rather consisted, of five, a chief and confidential clerk, the poor man who has just been killed, a young man called Harrington, who is qualifying for a partnership, Orchard, a girl typist, and an office boy. Besides them, we employ an outside man, a traveller, a Dutchman named Vanderkemp. He attends sales and so on, and when not on the road works in the Amsterdam branch.”
Inspector French noted all the information Mr. Duke could give about each of the persons mentioned.
“Now this Mr. Gething,” he resumed. “You say he has been with you for over twenty years, and that you had full confidence in him, but I must ask the question, Are you sure that your confidence was not misplaced? In other words, are you satisfied that he was not himself after your diamonds?”

===Chinese===
「那要恭喜您了，杜克先生。那么现在，可否请您把贵行和各位雇员的情形给我讲一讲。」
杜克先生虽然仍旧激动难平，却已把情绪按捺下来，语气平静地答道。
「敝行的买卖不大，眼下实际上由我一人主持。皮博迪（Peabody）年纪虽还不及我，却一向被病身子拖累，人差不多垮了，难得来行里一趟，什么事也不担当。那位年轻的股东辛纳蒙德（Sinnamond）在东方跑生意，已经去了几个月。我们做的就是钻石商的本行，在阿姆斯特丹（Amsterdam）设有一个小小的分号。说起来，我自己的工夫也几乎平分在伦敦和阿姆斯特丹两处。我们占用的，就只有您方才见到的这两间屋子。外间的人手——或者说原先的人手——共有五名：一名首席机要职员，就是这位刚刚遇害的可怜人；一名叫哈林顿（Harrington）的年轻人，正在熬资历升股东；奥查德；一名女打字员；还有一名办公室小厮。除他们之外，我们还雇着一个外勤，是跑外揽生意的，荷兰人，名叫范德肯普（Vanderkemp）。他奔走各处的拍卖会等等，不出门的时候，就在阿姆斯特丹分号里做事。」
凡提到的这些人，杜克先生能讲的情形，弗伦奇探长都一一记下。
「再说这位格辛先生，」他又开了腔。「您说他跟了您二十多年，对他信任有加。可这话我不得不问：您敢保这份信任没有放错了地方？换句话说，您能拿得稳，他自己就没打过那批钻石的主意？」

===Original===
Mr. Duke shook his head decisively.
“I am positive he was not,” he declared warmly and with something of indignation showing in his manner. “I should as soon accuse my own son, if I had one. No, I’d stake my life on it, Gething was no thief.”
“I’m glad to hear you say that, Mr. Duke,” the other returned smoothly. “Now, then, your office staff eliminated, tell me is there anyone that you suspect?”
“Not a creature!” Mr. Duke was equally emphatic. “Not a single creature! I can’t imagine anyone who would have done such a thing. I wish I could.”

===Chinese===
杜克先生决然摇头。
「我敢拿稳他没有，」他语气恳切地宣称，神色间还透出几分愤慨。「这就如同诬告我亲生的儿子一样——倘若我有儿子的话。不，我这条命都可以押上：格辛绝不是贼。」
「您能这么说，我很欣慰，杜克先生，」对方婉转地应道。「那么，行内人手既已排除，请问您可曾疑心过什么人？」
「一个人也没有！」杜克先生同样毫不含糊。「连一个也没有！我想象不出谁会干出这种事来。想得出倒好了。」

===Original===
The Inspector hesitated.
“Of course, sir, you understand that if you were to mention a name it would not in any way bias me against that person. It would only mean that I should make inquiries. Don’t think you would be getting anyone into trouble.”
Mr. Duke smiled grimly.
“You needn’t be afraid. If I had any suspicion I should be only too glad to tell you, but I have none.”

===Chinese===
探长沉吟片刻。
「当然，先生，您也明白，您若说出一个名字来，我决计不会因此对那人生出偏见，无非是说我得去查问查问。您别以为这会给人招来麻烦。」
杜克先生冷冷一笑。
「这您尽管放心。我若真有半点疑心，巴不得说出来告诉您，可我实在没有。」

===Original===
“When, sir, did you last see your late clerk?”
“About half-past four this evening. I left the office at that time, about an hour earlier than usual, because I had a business appointment for a quarter to five with Mr. Peters, of Lincoln’s Inn, my solicitor.”
“And you did not return to the office?”
“No. I sat with Mr. Peters for about half an hour, then as my business was not finished and he wanted to square up for the night, we decided to dine together at my club in Gower Street. It was not worth while going back to my own office, so I went straight from Peters’ to the club.”

===Chinese===
「先生，您最后一次见到您那位亡故的职员，是什么时候？」
「今晚四点半前后。我那时离行，比平常早了约莫一个钟头，因为四点三刻我约了林肯律师学院（Lincoln's Inn）的律师彼得斯先生（Mr. Peters）谈事。」
「后来您没有再回行里？」
「没有。我在彼得斯先生那里坐了半个钟头上下，事情没谈完，他又要收工过夜，我们便索性上高尔街（Gower Street）我那家俱乐部一同吃饭。再回自己行里已不划算了，我便从彼得斯那里径直去了俱乐部。」

===Original===
“And you did not notice anything peculiar about Mr. Gething?”
“Not specially on that night. He seemed absolutely as usual.”
“How do you mean, not specially on that night?”
“He had been, I thought, a little depressed for two or three weeks previously, as if he had some trouble on his mind. I asked when first I noticed it if there was anything wrong, but he murmured something about home troubles, about his wife not being so well﻿—she is a chronic invalid. He was not communicative, and I did not press the matter. But he was no worse this afternoon than during the last fortnight.”
“I see. Now, what brought him back to the office tonight?”

===Chinese===
「那您没觉出格辛先生有什么异样么？」
「那天晚上倒谈不上。他看上去同平日一模一样。」
「怎么叫『那天晚上倒谈不上』？」
「我只是觉得，前两三个星期他有点儿闷闷不乐，像是心上压着事。我一察觉便问过他可有什么不顺，他含含糊糊说了些家里的烦恼，说他太太身子不大好——她是个老病号。他不肯多讲，我也就没有追问。不过今天下午，他不比这半个月来更坏。」
「我明白了。那么，今晚他为什么又回行里去？」

===Original===
Mr. Duke made a gesture of bewilderment.
“I have no idea,” he declared. “There was nothing! Nothing, at least, that I know of or can imagine. We were not specially busy, and as far as I can think, he was well up to date with his work.”
“Is there a postal delivery between half-past four and the time your office closes?”
“There is, and of course there might have been a telegram or a caller or a note delivered by hand. But suppose there had been something important enough to require immediate attention, Gething would never have taken action without consulting me. He had only to ring me up.”

===Chinese===
杜克先生做了个茫然无措的手势。
「我毫无头绪，」他说。「没有什么事！至少，凡我知道的、想得到的，都没有什么。我们近来并不特别忙，就我所能想起的，他的活计也绝不曾积压。」
「四点半到贵行下班之间，可有一班邮差？」
「有的。当然，那当口也许来过一封电报、一位访客，或是专差送来的一封信。可就算真有要紧得必须立刻料理的事，格辛也断不会不先同我商量便擅自做主。他只消打个电话给我。」

===Original===
“He knew where you were, then?”
“No, but he could have rung up my home. They knew there where I was, as when I had decided to dine at the club, I phoned home to say so.”
“But were you in your club all the evening? Excuse my pressing the matter, but I think it’s important to make sure the man did not try to communicate with you.”
“I see your point. Yes, I stayed chatting with Mr. Peters until almost 9:30. Then, feeling tired from a long day’s thought about business, I decided a little exercise would be pleasant, and I walked home. I reached my house a minute or two after ten.”
“That seems conclusive. All the same, sir, I think you should make sure when you reach home that no call was made.”
“I shall do so certainly, but my parlourmaid is very reliable in such matters, and I am certain she would have told me of any.”

===Chinese===
「那么他知道您在哪儿了？」
「不知道。可他可以往我家里打电话。家里的人知道我在哪儿，因为我打定主意在俱乐部吃饭之后，曾打电话回家说过一声。」
「可您整个晚上都待在俱乐部里么？恕我刨根问底——依我看，务须弄确实了：那人没有设法同您联络过。」
「我明白您的意思。是的，我同彼得斯先生一直闲谈到将近 9:30。后来，白天为生意劳神了一日，只觉乏得很，心想稍稍活动活动也好，便步行回家。到家时是十点过一两分。」
「看来这就没有疑问了。尽管如此，先生，我想您到家之后，还是应当核实一下，确无电话来寻过您。」
「这我一定照办。不过我家那位客厅女仆办这类事最是可靠，倘若真有电话，我笃定她必定告诉我。」

===Original===
Inspector French sat for a few seconds lost in thought, and then began on another point.
“You tell me that you had £33,000 worth of diamonds in the safe. Is not that an unusually large amount to keep in an office?”
“You are quite right; it is too large. I consider myself very much to blame, both for that and in the matter of the insurance. But I had not meant to keep the stones there long. Indeed, negotiations for the sale of the larger portion were actually in progress. On the other hand, it is due to myself to point out that the safe is of a very efficient modern pattern.”
“That is so, sir. Now can you tell me who, besides yourself, knew of the existence of those stones?”
“I’m afraid,” Mr. Duke admitted despondently, “there was no secret about it. Gething knew, of course. He was entirely in my confidence about such matters. Vanderkemp, my outdoor man, knew that I had made some heavy purchases recently, as he not only conducted the negotiations, but personally brought the stones to the office. Besides, there were letters about them, accessible to all the staff. I am afraid you may take it that everyone in the office knew there was a lot of stuff there, though probably not the exact amount.”

===Chinese===
弗伦奇探长出神地坐了几秒钟，随后又换上一个话头。
「您告诉我，保险柜里存着价值 33,000 镑的钻石。把这么一大笔货存放在办公室里，数目难道不嫌反常地大么？」
「您说得不错，是太大了。为这一点，还有保险一节，我都怨自己得很。可我原也不打算把那些石头久存在那里。事实上，较大一批的洽售事宜已在进行之中。不过话说回来，我也得替自己表白一句：这口保险柜是极牢靠的新式货色。」
「确是如此，先生。那么您能否告诉我，除您本人之外，还有哪些人晓得有这批钻石？」
「恐怕，」杜克先生垂头丧气地承认，「这事压根儿就算不得秘密。格辛自然晓得，这类事务我对他是言无不尽。我的外勤范德肯普知道我近来进了几批大货，因为那几桩买卖不但是他一手经办的，石头还是他亲自送到行里来的。再者，相关的往来信件，行里人人都翻得着。恐怕您只能这么认定：行里人人都知道那儿存着一大批货色，只是未必晓得确数罢了。」

===Original===
“And the staff may have talked to outsiders. Young people will brag, especially if they are ‘keeping company,’ as the Irish say.”
“I fear that is so,” Mr. Duke agreed, as if deprecating the singular habits of the young.
The Inspector changed his position uneasily, and his hand stole to his pipe. But he checked himself and resumed his questioning. He obtained from Mr. Duke a detailed list of the missing stones, then turned to a new point.
“About that thousand pounds in notes. I suppose you haven’t got the numbers?”
“No, unfortunately. But the bank might know them.”
“We shall inquire. Now, Mr. Duke, about the key. That is another singular thing.”

===Chinese===
「职员们还可能说给外人听。年轻人爱夸口，倘若正在『处朋友』，那就更了不得——爱尔兰人是这么说的。」
「我怕正是如此。」杜克先生深以为然，那口气仿佛在为年轻人特有的习气暗暗叹息。
探长不自在地挪了挪身子，手不觉摸向烟斗，却又忍住了，接着发问。他从杜克先生那里讨得一份失窃钻石的详细清单，然后转到一个新的话头。
「说说那一千镑钞票。号码您总没有记下来吧？」
「没有，可惜。不过银行也许知道。」
「我们会去查问。那么，杜克先生，说说那把钥匙。那又是一桩奇事。」

===Original===
“It is an amazing thing. I absolutely cannot understand where it came from. As I said, this one never leaves, nor has left, my personal possession, and the other, the *only* other one, is equally inaccessible in my bank.”
“You always personally opened or closed the safe?”
“Always, or at least it was done by my instructions and in my presence.”
“Oh, well, that is not quite the same thing, you know. Who has ever opened or closed it for you?”
“Gething; and not once or twice, but scores, I suppose I might say hundreds of times. But always in my presence.”

===Chinese===
「岂止是奇事，简直骇人。我全然想不出它是打哪儿来的。我说过，这一把从不曾离开我身，往后也不会；另一把——*独一无二的*另一把——也同样碰不着，锁在我银行里。」
「保险柜向来是您亲手开闭？」
「向来是。至少，也是奉我之命、当着我的面开的。」
「哦，这个嘛，您知道，那可就不全是一回事了。有谁替您开闭过？」
「格辛；还不止一回两回，几十次总是有的——我看说上百次也不为过。但总当着我的面。」

===Original===
“I understand that, sir. Anyone else besides Mr. Gething?”
Mr. Duke hesitated.
“No,” he said slowly, “no one else. He was the only one I trusted to that extent. And I had reason to trust him,” he added, with a touch of defiance.
“Of course, sir. I recognise that,” French answered smoothly. “I am only trying to get the facts clear in my mind. I take it, then, that the deceased gentleman was the only person, other than yourself, who ever handled your key? It was not within reach of anyone in your house; your servants, for example?”
“No, I never let it lie about. Even at night I kept it attached to me.”

===Chinese===
「这个我明白，先生。除格辛先生之外，还有别的人么？」
杜克先生迟疑了一下。
「没有，」他慢吞吞地说，「再没有别人。信得过那个份上的，只有他一个。况且我信他是有道理的。」他补上一句，语气里带几分挑衅。
「当然，先生，这个我认账，」弗伦奇婉转地答道。「我不过是要把事实在脑子里理清楚。那么我这样理解对不对：除您本人之外，过世的那位先生便是唯一经手过您钥匙的人？它在您府上也不曾搁在什么人够得着的地方——比方说，您的仆人们？」
「没有，我从不让它随手乱放。夜里睡觉也拴在身上。」

===Original===
The Inspector rose from his chair.
“Well, sir,” he said politely, “I’m sorry to have kept you so long. Just let me take your finger prints to compare with those in the safe, and I have done. Shall I ring up for a taxi for you?”
Mr. Duke looked at his watch.
“Why, it is nearly one,” he exclaimed. “Yes, a taxi by all means, please.”

===Chinese===
探长从椅子里立起身来。
「好了，先生，」他客气地说，「耽搁您这么久，抱歉得很。再请让我取一份您的指纹，好同保险柜里的比对，取完就完事了。要不要我打电话替您叫一辆出租汽车？」
杜克先生看了看表。
「哎呀，都快一点钟了，」他失声道。「好，务请叫一辆。」

===Original===
Though Inspector French had said that everything possible had been done that night, he did not follow Mr. Duke from the building. Instead, he returned to the inner office and set himself unhurriedly to make a further and more thorough examination of its contents.
He began with the key of the safe. Removing it by the shank with a pair of special pincers, he tested the handle for finger prints, but without success. Looking then at the other end, a slight roughness on one of the wards attracted his attention, and on scrutinising it with his lens, a series of fine parallel scratches was revealed on all the surfaces. “So that’s it, is it?” he said to himself complacently. “Manufacturers don’t leave keys of valuable safes half finished. This one has been cut with a file, and probably,”﻿—he again scrutinised the workmanship﻿—“by an amateur at that. And according to this man Duke, old Gething was the only one that had the handling of the key﻿—that could have taken a wax impression. Well, well; we shall see.”
He locked the safe, dropped the key into his pocket, and turned to the fireplace, soliloquising the while.
The fire had still been glowing red when the crime was discovered shortly after ten o’clock. That meant, of course, that it had been deliberately stoked up, because the fire in the outer office was cold and dead. Someone, therefore, had intended to spend a considerable time in the office. Who could it have been?

===Chinese===
弗伦奇探长嘴上说当晚能做的都已做齐了，自己却没有随杜克先生一同离开这幢楼。他反倒回到里间，不慌不忙地着手，把屋内的东西再从头到尾细细查验一遍。
他先从保险柜的钥匙下手。用一把特制的钳子夹住钥匙杆将它取下，检验匙柄上有没有指纹，却一无所获。再看另一头时，锁瓣上一处轻微的毛糙引起了他的注意，拿到放大镜底下细察，各个面上都现出一道道细而平行的划痕。「原来是这么回事，是么？」他颇为自得地自语道。「厂家给贵重保险柜配钥匙，是不会交出半成品的毛坯的。这把是拿锉刀锉出来的，而且十有八九，」——他把那手艺又端详了一遍——「还是个外行锉的。照这位杜克先生的说法，老格辛是唯一经手过钥匙的人——也就是唯一能打蜡模的人。好，好，走着瞧吧。」
他锁上保险柜，把钥匙揣进衣袋，转向壁炉，一路自言自语。
十点刚过案子被发现时，炉膛里的火还红着。这当然意味着，火是有人存心添旺的，因为外间的炉火早已冷透。可见有人原打算在这间办公室里消磨好一段时候。那会是谁呢？

===Original===
As far as French could see, no one but Gething. But if Gething were going to commit the robbery﻿—a matter of perhaps ten minutes at the outside﻿—he would not have required a fire. No, this looked as if there really was some business to be done, something that would take time to carry through. But then, if so, why had Gething not consulted Mr. Duke? French noted the point, to be considered further in the light of future discoveries.
But as to the identity of the person who had built up the fire there should be no doubt. Finger prints again! The coal shovel had a smooth, varnished wooden handle, admirably suited for records, and a short test with the white powder revealed thereon an excellent impression of a right thumb.
The poker next received attention, and here French made his second discovery. Picking it up with the pincers in the same careful way in which he had handled the key, he noticed on the handle a dark brown stain. Beside this stain, and sticking to the metal, was a single white hair.
That he held in his hand the instrument with which the crime was committed seemed certain, and he eagerly tested the other end for prints. But this time he was baffled. Nothing showed at the places where finger marks might have been expected. It looked as if the murderer had worn gloves or had rubbed the handle clean, and he noted that either alternative postulated a cold-blooded criminal and a calculated crime.
He continued his laborious search of the room, but without finding anything else which interested him. Finally, while his men were photographing the prints he had discovered, he sat down in the leather-covered armchair and considered what he had learned.

===Chinese===
在弗伦奇看来，除格辛之外，不作第二人想。可格辛若是打算行窃——满打满算不过十分钟的勾当——他犯不着生一炉火。不，这情形倒像真有什么正事要办，一桩需要时辰才能料理完的事。可既然如此，格辛何不先同杜克先生商量？弗伦奇把这一点记下，留待日后有了新的发现再作推敲。
至于生火的是谁，那该没有疑问了。又是指纹！煤铲的木柄上了清漆，光滑平整，最宜留存印痕；拿白粉略一刷验，上面便显出一枚清清楚楚的右手拇指印。
接下来轮到火钳，在这里弗伦奇有了第二桩发现。他仍旧拿钳子小心翼翼地把它夹起，一眼看见钳柄上有一块深褐色的污渍。污渍旁边，紧粘在金属上，还有一根白发。
他手里握着的这件东西便是行凶的凶器，看来殆无可疑，他便急切地检验另一头有没有指纹。这一回却碰了壁。本该留有指印的地方，什么也没有显出来。凶手像是戴了手套，或是把钳柄擦抹干净了。他记下：无论哪一种，都指向一个冷血的凶徒、一桩预谋好的罪行。
他继续在这间屋子里苦苦搜检，却再没有找出什么使他感兴趣的东西来。末了，趁手下给他发现的指纹拍照的当口，他在那张皮面扶手椅里坐下，把已有的收获细细想了一遍。

===Original===
Certainly a good deal of the evidence pointed to Gething. Gething knew the stones were there. According to Duke, no one else could have got hold of the key to the safe to make an impression. Moreover, his body was found before the safe with the latter open. All circumstantial evidence, of course, though cumulatively strong.
However, whether or not Gething had contemplated robbery, he had not carried it through. Someone else had the diamonds. And here the obvious possibility recurred to him which had been in his mind since he had heard the Superintendent’s first statement. Suppose Orchard was the man. Suppose Orchard, visiting the office in the evening, arrived to find the safe open and the old man stooping over it. Instantly he would be assailed by a terrible temptation. The thing would seem so easy, the way of escape so obvious, the reward so sure. French, sitting back in the armchair, tried to picture the scene. The old man bending over the safe, the young one entering, unheard. His halt in surprise; the sudden overwhelming impulse to possess the gems; his stealthy advance; the seizing of the poker; the blow, delivered perhaps with the intention of merely stunning his victim. But he strikes too hard, and, horrified by what he has done, yet sees that for his own safety he must go through with the whole business. He recalls the danger of finger prints, and wipes the handles of the poker and of the drawer in the safe from which he has abstracted the diamonds. With admirable foresight he waits until the body grows cold, lest an examination of it by the policeman he intends to call might disprove his story. Then he rushes out in an agitated manner and gives the alarm.
Though this theory met a number of the facts, French was not overpleased with it. It did not explain what Gething was doing at the safe, nor did it seem to fit in with the personality of Orchard. All the same, though his instruction to his man to shadow Orchard had been given as an obvious precaution inevitable in the circumstances, he was glad that he had not overlooked it.
Another point occurred to him as he sat thinking over the affair in the leather-lined chair. If Orchard had stolen the stones, he would never have risked having them on his person when he gave the alarm. He would certainly have hidden them, and French could not see how he could have taken them out of the building to do so. A thorough search of the offices seemed therefore called for.
The Inspector was tired, but, late as it was, he spent three solid hours conducting a meticulous examination of the whole premises, only ceasing when he had satisfied himself beyond possibility of doubt that no diamonds were concealed thereon. Then, believing that he had exhausted the possibilities of the scene of the crime, he felt himself free to withdraw. Dawn was appearing in the eastern sky as he drew the door after him and set off in the direction of his home.

===Chinese===
诚然，有相当多的证据指向格辛。格辛晓得钻石就在那里。据杜克说，再没有第二个人能拿到保险柜钥匙去打模子。何况，他的尸首便是在敞开的保险柜跟前发现的。当然，这些全是间接证据，只是叠加起来分量不小。
然而不论格辛可曾起意行窃，他终究没有做成。钻石在另一个人手里。于是那个显而易见的可能又浮上心头——打他听见警司的第一次通报起，这念头就不曾离开过他。假定奥查德就是那个人。假定奥查德晚间来到行里，进门正撞见保险柜敞着，老人俯身柜前。刹那间，一个可怕的诱惑便会攫住他：事情显得那样轻易，脱身的路那样分明，报酬又那样十拿九稳。弗伦奇靠在扶手椅里，试着在脑子里描出那一幕：老人俯身柜前，年轻人悄没声儿地进来；他愕然收步；攫取宝石的念头突然排山倒海而来；他蹑手蹑脚地逼近；抓起火钳；那一击落下，或者起初只图把人打晕过去。可他下手太重，闯下了大祸，惊惶之余，又明白为自身安全计，索性一不做二不休。他想起指纹的危险，便把火钳的柄，连同他取出钻石的那只柜屉的把手，都擦抹干净。凭着过人的先见，他一直守到尸身凉透，以防他存心要唤来的那个警察检验尸体时，戳穿他那套说辞。随后他才做出一副激动的样子，冲出去报了警。
这套理论虽能对上不少事实，弗伦奇对它却谈不上满意。它说不通格辛伏在保险柜前做什么，也似乎对不上奥查德的为人。话虽如此，他吩咐手下盯奥查德的梢，原不过是此情此景之下必不可少的一道防备，此刻倒庆幸自己没有省去这一步。
他坐在皮面椅子里把这事前后一想，又想起一点来。奥查德若真偷了钻石，决不敢报警的时候把它们带在身上。他必定先把它们藏好，而弗伦奇又看不出，他怎能携着钻石出楼去藏。看来，有必要把这整处办公楼的里里外外彻底搜上一遍。
探长已经乏了，但尽管夜深，他还是足足花了三个钟头，把整处屋宇仔仔细细查验了一遍，直到确凿无疑地认定楼内并无藏匿的钻石，方才住手。随后，他觉得案发之地的可查之处已经查尽，这才得以抽身。他掩上房门，朝自家方向走去时，东方天际已透出曙光。

## VI The Hotel in Barcelona / 六、巴塞罗那的旅馆

===Original===
To a comparative stay-at-home like Inspector French, who considered a run to Plymouth or Newcastle a long journey, the trailing of Jan Vanderkemp across southwest France opened up a conception of the size of the globe whereon he moved and had his being, which left him slightly awestruck. The journey from Savoy to Spain seemed endless, the distances incredible, the expanse of country between himself and home illimitable. Hour after hour he sat in the train, while elms and oaks gave place to cypresses and olives, apples to vines, and corn to maize, and it was not until daylight had gone on the evening of the second day that the train rolled into the Estacion de Francia in Barcelona.

The porter at the Beau-Sejour at Chamonix had written down the names of two or three hotels at which he thought English would be spoken, and passing out of the station, French showed the paper to a taxi driver. The man at first ogled it distrustfully, then with a smile of comprehension he emitted a rapid flood of some unknown language, opened the taxi door, bowed his fare in, and rapidly cranking his engine, set off into the night. French was conscious of being whirled down a great avenue wider than any he had yet seen, brilliantly lighted, and with rows of palms down the centre; they turned through a vast square with what looked like a commemorative column in the middle, then up a slightly narrower, tree-lined boulevard, where presently the vehicle swung into the curb and French found himself at his destination﻿—the Hôtel d’Orient.

To his extreme relief, the head porter spoke English. He got him to settle with the taxi man, and soon he began to forget the fatigues of the journey with the help of a luxurious bath and dinner.

He decided that he had done enough for one day, and presently, soothed by a cigar, he went out into the great street in front of the hotel, with its rows of trees and brilliant arc lamps. He did not know then that this gently-sloping boulevard was one of the famous streets of the world﻿—the Rambla, known as is Piccadilly in London, the Champs Élysées in Paris, or Fifth Avenue in New York.
