For an hour he roamed, then, tired out, he returned to the Orient, and a few minutes later was sunk in dreamless slumber.

===Chinese===
对弗伦奇探长这样一位几乎足不出户的人来说——在他眼里，跑一趟普利茅斯（Plymouth）或纽卡斯尔（Newcastle）就算得上远行了——这一路尾随扬·范德肯普（Jan Vanderkemp）横穿法国西南部，让他对自家安身立命的这颗地球之大有了一番全新的体认，心中不免生出几分敬畏。从萨瓦到西班牙，这旅程仿佛没有尽头，距离远得难以置信，横亘在他与家之间的国土一望无际。他一个钟头又一个钟头地坐在车厢里，看榆树橡树渐渐让位给柏树和橄榄树，苹果园换成葡萄园，麦田换成玉米地，直到第二天傍晚天光散尽，火车才驶进巴塞罗那的法兰西车站（Estacion de Francia）。

夏蒙尼（Chamonix）博塞茹尔酒店的门房先前替他写下了两三家想必有人会讲英语的旅馆的名字。走出车站，弗伦奇把纸条递给一个出租车司机。那人先是满腹疑虑地端详着纸条，随即会意地一笑，迸出一大串不知什么语言的急话，拉开车门，躬身把客人让了进去，又飞快摇着引擎手柄，一头扎进夜色里。弗伦奇只觉车子顺着一条大路飞驰而下，那路比他生平所见的任何街道都宽，灯火通明，路中央并排栽着一行行的棕榈；随后车子转过一个开阔的广场，广场中央似乎立着一根纪念柱，再拐上一条略窄些的林荫大道，不多时车身一歪靠上路边，弗伦奇已到了目的地——东方酒店（Hôtel d'Orient）。

令他如释重负的是，旅馆的首席门房会讲英语。他请门房代为付清了车钱，接着舒舒服服地沐浴一番、吃罢晚饭，旅途的劳顿不多时便渐渐抛在了脑后。

他断定这一天做的已足够，不多时，一支雪茄下肚，身心舒泰，便出门走到旅馆前的大街上，两旁林木成行，弧光灯雪亮。他当时还不知道，这条微微倾斜的林荫大道竟是世界名街之一——兰布拉大道（Rambla），正如伦敦有皮卡迪利、巴黎有香榭丽舍（Champs Élysées）、纽约有第五大道（Fifth Avenue）。他闲逛了一个钟头，末了筋疲力尽，回到东方酒店，几分钟后便沉入无梦的酣睡。

===Original===
Early next morning he was seated with the manager, who also spoke English. But neither the manager nor any of his staff could help him, and French recognised that so far as the Orient was concerned he had drawn blank. He therefore set to work on the other hotels, taking the larger first, the Colon, in the Plaza de Cataluna, the Cuatro Naciones, and suchlike. Then he went on to the smaller establishments, and at the fourth he paused suddenly, thrilled by an unexpected sight.

The hotel was in a side street off the Paseo de Colon, the great boulevard through which he had been driven on the previous evening. The entrance door led into a kind of lounge in which were seated half a dozen people, evidently waiting for déjeuner. With one exception these were obviously Spaniards, but that exception, French felt he could swear, was the original of the photograph.

In spite of such a meeting being what he was hoping for, the Inspector was taken aback. But his hesitation was momentary. Passing immediately on to the little office at the back of the lounge, he said in English:

“Can I have lunch, please? Will it soon be ready?”

===Chinese===
第二天一早，他和经理坐在了一处，经理也会讲英语。可无论经理还是他手下的职员，都帮不上他的忙，弗伦奇明白，在东方酒店这一头，他是空手而归了。于是他着手查访其余旅馆，先从大的下手：加泰罗尼亚广场（Plaza de Cataluna）的科隆酒店（Colon）、四国酒店（Cuatro Naciones）等等。接着再查小些的，查到第四家时，他猛地顿住脚步，一副意想不到的景象叫他心头发热。

这家旅馆坐落在科隆大道（Paseo de Colon）旁的一条小街上——头天晚上，载他的车正是从那条大路上驶过的。大门进去是一间休息厅，厅里坐着五六个人，显然在等午餐。这些人一望而知都是西班牙人，只有一个例外；而那唯一的例外，弗伦奇敢起誓，正是照片上那人的真身。

尽管这样的巧遇正是他求之不得的，探长还是吃了一惊。但迟疑只有一瞬。他当即走到休息厅后首的小账房里，用英语说道：

「请问，这儿能用午餐吗？快好了吗？」

===Original===
A dark-eyed, dark-haired girl came forward, smiling but shaking her head regretfully, and murmuring what was evidently that she couldn’t understand.

“You don’t speak English, miss?” the detective went on, speaking loudly and very clearly. “I want to know can I have lunch, and if it will soon be ready?”

As the girl still shook her head, French turned back into the lounge.

“Excuse me,” he addressed the company generally, “but might I ask if any of you gentlemen speak English? I can’t make this young lady understand.”

The little ruse succeeded. The man resembling Vanderkemp rose.

“I speak English,” he answered. “What is it you want?”

===Chinese===
一个黑眼睛黑头发的姑娘走上前来，面带微笑，却遗憾地摇着头，嘴里喃喃说着什么，显然是说她听不懂。

「小姐，你不会讲英语？」侦探接着又说，嗓门提得很高，吐字也格外清楚。「我想知道能不能在这儿用午餐，是不是快好了？」

见姑娘仍旧摇头，弗伦奇便转身回到休息厅里。

「对不起，」他冲着满座的人笼统地说，「可否请问，哪位先生会讲英语？我实在没法让这位小姐明白我的意思。」

小小的计谋奏了效。那个貌似范德肯普的男子站起身来。

「我会讲英语。」他答道。「你有什么事？」

===Original===
“Lunch,” French returned, “and to know if it will soon be ready.”

“I can answer that for you,” the other declared, after he had explained the situation to the girl. “Lunch will be ready in exactly five minutes, and visitors are usually welcome.”

“Thank you.” French spoke in a leisurely, conversational way. “I am staying at the Orient, where one or two of them speak English, but business brought me to this part of the town, and I did not want to go all that way back to lunch. A confounded nuisance this language business! It makes you feel pretty helpless when you want to talk to people.”

“That’s true,” the stranger admitted. “In most of the larger hotels they speak French and English, but at practically none of the smaller. In this one, for example, one waiter has a few words of French only. No English or Italian or German. Some of the staff don’t even speak Spanish.”

French was interested in spite of the larger question which was occupying his mind.

===Chinese===
「午餐，」弗伦奇答道，「再有就是想知道是不是快好了。」

「这我可以代为答复。」那人向姑娘说明情形之后说道。「午餐整五分钟就好，外来客人一般都欢迎。」

「多谢。」弗伦奇用一种从容闲谈的口气说。「我住在东方酒店，那里有一两个人会讲英语，可有事到这一带来，总不能为顿午饭再跑那么远的回头路。这语言的事真是恼人！想跟人说句话，都叫你觉得束手无策。」

「这话不假，」陌生人承认道。「大多数大旅馆里都讲法语和英语，可小旅馆几乎一家也不讲。就拿这一家来说，只有一个侍者会几句法语。英语、意大利语、德语一概不会。有些伙计连西班牙语都不讲。」

尽管心里正盘算着那桩更要紧的大事，弗伦奇还是听得有了兴致。

===Original===
“Not Spanish?” he repeated. “How do you mean? What do they speak?”

“Catalan. This is Catalonia, you know, and both the race and the language are different from the rest of Spain. They are more go-ahead and enterprising than the people farther south.”

“That sounds a bit like Ireland,” French remarked. “I’ve been both in Belfast and in the south, and the same things seems to hold good. Though Dublin is a fine city, and no mistake.”

They continued discussing peoples and languages and the northerly concentration of energy to be found in most countries, until the hands of the clock pointed to noon and lunch time. Then French caught what he had been angling for. The stranger asked him to share his table.

The Inspector continued to make himself agreeable, and after they had finished invited the other to have coffee and a cigar with him in a deserted corner of the lounge. Then thinking his companion was by this time off his guard, he introduced a new subject after a lull in the conversation.

“It’s strange the different businesses people are engaged on,” he remarked ruminatively, as he poured himself out a second cup of coffee. “Now, I wouldn’t mind betting a ten-pound note you wouldn’t guess what I am, and what my business here is.”

The other laughed.

===Chinese===
「连西班牙语都不讲？」他重复道。「这话怎么讲？那他们讲什么？」

「加泰罗尼亚语（Catalan）。你要知道，这里是加泰罗尼亚（Catalonia），无论人种还是语言，都和西班牙其余地方不同。这里的人比更南边的人更有闯劲，更会经营。」

「这倒有几分像爱尔兰，」弗伦奇说。「贝尔法斯特和南方我都去过，情形似乎也是一样。不过都柏林（Dublin）确实是座好城，这没得说。」

两人继续谈论各国的民情语言，还有多数国家里元气总聚于北地的那条规律，直到时针指向正午，到了午餐时分。这时，弗伦奇下钩久候的东西上钩了：陌生人邀他同桌共餐。

探长继续一味承欢讨好，饭罢又请对方到休息厅一个无人的角落里，同他喝咖啡、抽雪茄。他估量同伴此时已卸下防备，便趁着谈话的一个间歇，抛出一个新话头。

「人的行当真是五花八门，说来奇怪。」他一面给自己斟第二杯咖啡，一面若有所思地说。「这样吧，拿一张十镑的票子打赌，你猜不出我是干什么的，也猜不出我来这儿办的是什么事。」

对方笑了。

===Original===
“I confess I was wondering,” he admitted. “I am afraid I should lose my money. I won’t guess.”

“Well, I’ll tell you, though our business is not a thing we speak of as a rule. I am a detective inspector from Scotland Yard.”

As he spoke French watched the other’s face. If this were the man of whom he was in search, he could swear he would make him exhibit some emotion.

But so far he did not succeed. His new acquaintance merely laughed again.

“Then I should have lost. I admit I never thought of that.”

French continued to observe, and he went on with more seriousness in his manner.

===Chinese===
「不瞒你说，我也正纳闷呢。」他承认道。「只怕我的钱要输掉，我就不猜了吧。」

「那我就告诉你，虽说干我们这一行的，平素不把营生挂在嘴上。我是苏格兰场来的探长。」

说话时，弗伦奇紧盯着对方的脸。倘若这正是他要寻的那个人，他敢起誓，总能叫他脸上露出些动静来。

但直到此刻他未能得手。这位新相识只是又笑了笑。

「那我的钱就输定了。说实话，我压根儿没想到这上头。」

弗伦奇继续观察着，接着开口，神色郑重了几分。

===Original===
“Yes, and I’m on rather important business, too. Man wanted for murder and robbery in the City. A bad affair enough. He murdered the confidential clerk of a diamond merchant in Hatton Garden and rifled the safe and got off with I don’t know how many thousand pounds’ worth of stuff.”

At the commencement of French’s reply the stranger had listened with but little more than a conventional interest, but at the mention of a diamond merchant in Hatton Garden he figuratively sat up and began to take notice.

“Hatton Garden?” he repeated. “That’s an extraordinary coincidence. Why, I belong to a firm of diamond merchants in Hatton Garden. I know them all. Who was the man?”

Inspector French was puzzled. Either Vanderkemp﻿—for there could no longer be any doubt of his identity﻿—was innocent, or he was an almost incredibly good actor. Anxious to observe the man further, he fenced a little in his reply.

“Is it possible you haven’t heard?” he asked in apparent surprise. “How long is it since you have heard from home?”

===Chinese===
「不错，而且我手上办的还是桩要紧案子。伦敦城里有个人因凶杀加劫案被通缉。案情够恶劣的。他害死了哈顿花园一位钻石商的机要职员，撬开保险柜，卷走了不知值几千镑的货。」

弗伦奇这话起头时，陌生人还只带着几分客套的兴味听着，可一听到「哈顿花园的钻石商」几个字，他简直像猛地坐直了身子，登时留了神。

「哈顿花园？」他重复道。「这可就巧得出奇了。我就是哈顿花园一家钻石商行的人，那里的人我个个认识。死者是谁？」

弗伦奇探长心里犯开了嘀咕。要么范德肯普——此人的身份如今已无可怀疑——是清白的，要么他就是个演技好得几乎叫人难以置信的戏子。弗伦奇急于再察看他一番，回话时便绕了个弯子。

「你竟然没听说过？」他做出满脸诧异地问。「你离家后，有多久没听到家里的音信了？」

===Original===
“Haven’t had a line of any kind since I left, and that’s nearly three weeks ago; on the night of the 25th of last month to be exact.”

“The 25th! Well, that’s a coincidence, too. That’s the very night poor old Mr. Gething was killed.”

Vanderkemp stiffened suddenly and his hands closed on the arms of his chair.

“What?” he cried. “Not Charles Gething of Messrs. Duke & Peabody?”

French, now keenly observing him without any attempt at concealment, nodded.

“That’s the man. You knew him then?”

===Chinese===
「自打动身，一个字的音信也没收到过，算起来快三个礼拜了；确切说，我是上月 25 日晚上走的。」

「25 日！嗬，这又是一桩巧事。可怜的老格辛先生遇害，正是那天夜里。」

范德肯普猛地僵住了，双手扣紧了椅子的扶手。

「什么？」他失声叫道。「不会是杜克与皮博迪公司的查尔斯·格辛吧？」

弗伦奇此时毫不掩饰地逼视着他，点了点头。

「正是此人。这么说，你认识他？」

===Original===
“Of course I knew him. Why, it’s my own firm. Good God, to think of poor old Gething! And you say the safe was rifled? You don’t tell me Mr. Duke’s collection of stones is gone?”

“All of it, and money as well. The murderer made a clean sweep.”

Vanderkemp whistled and then swore.

“Tell me about it.”

French was more than ever puzzled. The traveller’s manner, his evident emotion, his questions﻿—all seemed those of an innocent man. He felt doubts arising in his mind; possibly there might be an explanation.﻿ … He did not at once reply, as he turned over in his mind how he could best surprise the other into an admission of the truth.

But Vanderkemp also was evidently thinking, and suddenly an expression of deeper concern showed on his face. He made as if to speak, then hesitated and a wary look appeared in his eyes. He cleared his throat, then in a changed voice asked, “At what time did it happen?”

===Chinese===
「当然认识。那是我自家的商行啊。天哪，可怜的老格辛，竟出了这样的事！你是说保险柜被撬了？别告诉我，杜克先生收藏的那批钻石也没了？」

「全没了，连现款也一并卷走。凶手来了个一扫而光。」

范德肯普倒抽一口冷气，跟着又咒骂了一句。

「讲给我听听。」

弗伦奇愈发摸不着头脑了。这位旅行家的神态，他那股显而易见的激动，他那连珠炮似的追问——桩桩件件，都像出自一个清白人之口。他心里不由得犯起嘀咕：兴许事情另有解释。……他没有立刻作答，只在心里反复掂量，怎样才能最出其不意，逼得对方吐露实情。

可范德肯普显然也在转着念头。忽然，一层更深的忧色浮上他的脸。他张口欲言，又迟疑起来，眼里现出戒备的神气。他清了清嗓子，换了副声调问道：「案子是几点钟发生的？」

===Original===
French leaned forward swiftly and fixed his eyes on his companion as he said in a low, tense tone, “That’s what I want to ask you, Mr. Vanderkemp.”

The man started. He did not answer, and the wary look in his eyes changed into definite anxiety, which deepened as the moments passed. At last he spoke.

“It had just dawned on me from what you said, Inspector, that our meeting here was not such a coincidence as I at first imagined. I see that you suspect me of the crime. What has happened I don’t know, what you have against me I don’t know either, but I can tell at once that I am not only absolutely innocent, but until you told me just now I was ignorant that a crime had been committed. I will tell you my whole story and answer any questions you may like to ask, whether you believe me or not.”

French nodded. Certainly, if guilty, this man was a consummate actor. There was at least the chance that he might be innocent, and he answered accordingly.

“I don’t accuse you of anything, Mr. Vanderkemp. But there are certain suspicious circumstances which require an explanation. You may be able to account for all of them﻿—I hope you will. At the same time it is fair to warn you that, failing an explanation, your arrest is not impossible, and in that case anything that you may say now may be used against you in evidence.”

Vanderkemp was by this time extremely ill at ease. His face had paled and had already taken on a somewhat drawn and haggard expression. For a while he remained silent, buried in thought, then with a sudden gesture as of throwing further caution to the winds, he began to speak.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇霍地探身向前，目光钉在同伴脸上，压低嗓音、一字一顿地说：「范德肯普先生，这正是我要问你的。」

那人浑身一震。他没有作声，眼里的戒备变成了明明白白的惶恐，且一刻深似一刻。半晌，他终于开口。

「探长，听了你这句话我才恍然醒悟：你我在这儿碰面，并不像我起初想的那样是巧合。我看出你疑心这案子是我干的。出了什么事，我不知道；你手里拿住了我什么，我也不知道。可我此刻就能告诉你：我不但绝对清白，而且直到你方才说明之前，我压根不知道出了案子。我把我的经历原原本本讲给你听，你要问什么尽管问，信不信由你。」

弗伦奇点了点头。当然，此人若真有罪，那必是个炉火纯青的戏子。但他至少有可能是清白的，于是弗伦奇便顺着这层意思答道：

「我并没有指控你什么，范德肯普先生。只是有几处可疑的情节，需要一番解释。你兴许能把它们一一对上——我也盼你对得上。不过有句话须得说在前头：倘若解释不清，你被逮捕也不是不可能的事；果真到了那一步，你如今说的话，日后都可作为对你不利的证词。」

范德肯普这时早已六神无主。他脸色发白，已然带出几分形容枯槁的神气。他沉默了好一阵，埋首深思，随后猛一挥手，像是索性把小心谨慎统统抛到了九霄云外，开口说道：

===Original===
“I’ll tell you what I know, Inspector,” he said earnestly. “Whether, if you are going to arrest me, I am wise or foolish, I don’t know. But I can at least assure you that it is the literal truth.”

He looked at the Inspector, who nodded approval.

“Of course I can’t advise you, Mr. Vanderkemp,” he remarked, “but all the same I believe you are doing the wise thing.”

“I am in a difficulty,” Vanderkemp went on, “as I don’t know how much of the circumstances you are familiar with. It would therefore be better if you would ask me questions.”

“I shall do so, but first I should like your own statement. I am aware of your name and position in the firm. Also that Mr. Schoofs received a letter on the 21st of last month, asking him to send you to London to undertake an important commission in Sweden. Also that you left your lodgings in the Kinkerstraat at 8:30 on the evening of the 24th. I have since learned certain other facts as to your subsequent movements, which I need not mention at the moment. What I want you now to do is to let me have a detailed account of your experiences from the moment of your leaving your lodgings until the present time.”

===Chinese===
「探长，我知道的都告诉你。」他恳切地说。「若是你终究要逮捕我，我这么做是聪明还是糊涂，我自己也说不上。可我至少能向你担保：我说的句句是实。」

他望了望探长，探长颔首认可。

「我自然不便替你拿主意，范德肯普先生，」他说，「不过话虽如此，我还是相信你这样做是明智之举。」

「我有点为难，」范德肯普接着说，「因为这些内情你掌握了多少，我无从知道。这样吧，还是由你来问我，倒更好些。」

「我正要如此，不过先请你自行陈述一遍。你的姓名，以及你在商行里的职位，我已经知道了。我还知道，上月 21 日，斯霍夫斯先生收到一封信，要他派你到伦敦去，承办一桩瑞典的要务。我也知道，24 日晚 8:30，你离开了金克街（Kinkerstraat）的寓所。此后你的行踪，我又另行查明了若干，此刻不必细说。现在要你做的，是从你离开寓所的那一刻起，把你一路的经历原原本本、详详细细讲给我听。」

===Original===
“I will do so.” Vanderkemp spoke eagerly, as if now anxious to get through with the matter. “But there is one thing which comes earlier in point of time which I must mention. You have probably heard of it from Mr. Duke, but I shall tell you anyway. I mean about my further instructions as to my London visit﻿—the private instructions. You have seen a copy of them?”

French, always cautious, was not giving away information. He wondered to what the other was referring, but merely said, “Assume I have not, Mr. Vanderkemp. It is obvious that I must check your statement by the information in my possession.”

“Well, then, though you probably know it already, I may tell you I received additional instructions about my visit. Mr. Duke wrote me a private letter, addressed to my lodgings, in which he told me﻿—but I have it here, and you can see it for yourself.”

He took an envelope from his pocketbook and passed it across. It contained a note almost identical in appearance with the forged one which Mr. Schoofs had received. It was typewritten on a sheet of the firm’s cheaper memorandum paper, with the same kind of type and the same coloured ribbon. Examination with the lens showed the same defects in the *n* and the *g*, the signature was obviously forged, and the back of the sheet was marked from a heavy touch. Evidently both letters had been written by the same person, and on the Hatton Garden machine. The note read:

> “Dear Vanderkemp﻿—Further to my note to Mr. Schoofs re your call here on Wednesday morning, 26th inst., the business on which I wish to see you has turned out to be more urgent than I at first believed, and I shall therefore have to ask you to advance the hour of your interview, and also to leave London for Paris﻿—not Stockholm﻿—immediately after it. I shall return to the office after dinner on Tuesday evening, 25th inst., and shall be glad if you will call there at 8:30 p.m., when I shall give you your instructions. This will enable you to catch the 9:30 p.m. for Paris, via Southampton and Havre.
> “I wish to impress on you that as the business in question is exceptionally confidential, you will oblige me by keeping your change of plans to yourself.
> “Yours truly,
> “R. A. Duke.”

===Chinese===
「我这就讲。」范德肯普说得颇为急切，仿佛巴不得早些把事情了结。「不过有一件事，论时间在前头，我必须先提一句。你多半已从杜克先生那里听说了，可无论如何我都要讲。我说的，是我那趟伦敦之行的后续指示——那份密令。抄件你见过吧？」

弗伦奇素来谨慎，不肯白白漏出消息。他不明白对方指的是什么，只说：「只当我没见过，范德肯普先生。显然，我必须拿我掌握的材料，来核对你的陈述。」

「那么好吧，这事你多半也已知道：我那趟到访，另接了加派的指示。杜克先生给我写了封私信，寄到我的寓所，信里告诉我——不过信就在我身上，你自己看吧。」

他从皮夹里取出一个信封，递了过去。里面的便函，同斯霍夫斯先生收到的那封伪造信几乎一模一样：打字打在商行较廉价的便笺纸上，用的是同一副铅字、同色的色带。拿放大镜一验，字母 *n* 和 *g* 上有同样的缺陷；签名显然出自伪造；纸背也有下笔过重压出的印痕。两封信显然出自同一人之手，而且都是在哈顿花园那台打字机上打的。便函写道：

> 「亲爱的范德肯普——继我致斯霍夫斯先生的便函，关于你本月 26 日（星期三）上午来访一事：我要同你面谈的事务，已比我当初所想的更为紧急，因此不得不请你把会面的钟点提前，并在会面之后立即离开伦敦前往巴黎——不是斯德哥尔摩（Stockholm）。我将于本月 25 日（星期二）晚饭后回到办公处，请你于当晚 8:30 前来，届时我当面向你交代任务。这样你还能赶上当晚 9:30 经南安普敦和勒阿弗尔（Havre）开往巴黎的火车。
> 「此事非同寻常地机密，务请你对行程的改变守口如瓶，则感激不尽。
> 「你的忠实的
> 「R. A. Duke」

===Original===
Inspector French was keenly interested, but he recognised with exasperation how inconclusive the letter was as evidence. Either it had been sent to Vanderkemp as he stated, in which case he might be innocent, or the man had written it himself, in which case he certainly was guilty. It was true that in this instance an envelope was forthcoming which bore a London EC postmark and the correct date, but here again there was no proof that this was really the covering in which the letter had come. These points passed through the Inspector’s mind, but he banished them as matters to be thought out later, and turned once more to his companion.

“I shall keep this, if you don’t mind,” he declared. “Please proceed.”

“I carried out the instructions in the letter,” Vanderkemp resumed. “The change of hours necessitated my leaving Amsterdam by the night train on the 24th, and I spent the following day at my hotel in London, and in doing a matinee. At 8:30, with my luggage, I reached Hatton Garden. I found the outer office was in darkness, but a light shone out of the doorway of the inner office. Mr. Gething was there alone. He told me to come in and shut the door, and I did so, and sat down in the clients’ armchair. Mr. Gething was seated at Mr. Duke’s desk, which was open.”

===Chinese===
弗伦奇探长大感兴趣，可他也恼火地意识到，这封信作为证据，丝毫得不出结论。要么它确如范德肯普所言是寄给他的——那么他可能有清白之身；要么便是此人亲手伪造——那么他必定有罪。不错，这一回倒附了信封，盖着伦敦 EC 区的邮戳，日期也对，可这同样证明不了，信当真是装在这只信封里寄来的。这些念头在探长脑子里过了一遍，但他把它们按下不表，留待日后细想，重新转向同伴。

「这封信我要留下了，你不介意吧。」他说。「请继续讲。」

「我照信上的指示办了。」范德肯普接着说。「会面钟点一提前，我就只得搭 24 日的夜车离开阿姆斯特丹，第二天白天在伦敦的旅馆里歇着，还看了一场日场戏。8:30，我带着行李到了哈顿花园。外间办公室黑着，只有里间办公室的门口透出一方灯光。格辛先生独自一人在那儿。他叫我进去把门关上，我照办了，在客座的扶手椅里坐下。格辛先生坐在杜克先生的写字台前，写字台的抽屉开着。」

===Original===
“Was the safe open?”

“No, nor was it opened while I was there. Mr. Gething told me that Mr. Duke had intended to be present to give me my instructions in person, but at the last moment he had been prevented coming down, and that he had asked him, Mr. Gething, to do it instead. It seemed that Mr. Duke had got information from a confidential agent at Constantinople that a member of the old Russian aristocracy had escaped with his family jewels from the clutches of the Bolsheviks, and that he now wished to dispose of the whole collection for what it would bring. He was at one time Duke Sergius of one of the Ural provinces﻿—I have the name in my book upstairs﻿—but was now passing himself off as a Pole under the name of Francisko Loth. The collection was one of extraordinary excellence, and Mr. Duke believed it could be purchased for a third, or even less, of its real value. He had approached the duke through the agent, and had offered to deal. The trouble, however, was that the Soviet Government had learned of the duke’s escape, and were displaying immense energy in the hope of recapturing him. Their agents were scouring the whole of Europe, and Loth was in mortal terror, for discovery meant certain death. Mr. Gething told me straight also, that should I succeed in purchasing, my life would not be worth a tinker’s curse until I had handed over the stuff. He said that, recognising this, Mr. Duke considered that my commission should be substantially increased, and he asked me was I willing to take on the job.”

“And you agreed?”

===Chinese===
「保险柜开着吗？」

「没有，我在场的工夫，也没见人开过。格辛先生告诉我，杜克先生本打算亲自到场，向我面授机宜，可到了最后一刻，被什么事绊住没能下楼，便托了他格辛先生代劳。事情是这样：杜克先生从君士坦丁堡（Constantinople）的一名密探那里得到消息，说旧俄贵族中有一位公爵，带着家传的一批珠宝，从布尔什维克的魔掌里逃了出来，如今想把整批藏品脱手，能换多少算多少。他从前是乌拉尔（Ural）某省的谢尔盖公爵（Duke Sergius）——那省名我记在楼上的本子里——如今却化名弗兰西斯科·洛特（Francisko Loth），冒充波兰人。这批藏品件件都是精品，杜克先生相信，花真值三分之一的价钱、甚至更少，就能买下来。他已通过那密探同这位公爵接上了头，表示愿意做这笔买卖。麻烦在于，苏联政府已得知公爵出逃，正倾尽全力想把他捉拿回去。他们的密探撒遍全欧，洛特吓得魂不附体，因为一旦败露，必死无疑。格辛先生还直言相告：倘若我买成了，在把货交出去之前，我这条命也值不了一个铜板。他说，正因顾到这一层，杜克先生认为我的佣金该大大加码，并问我，这趟差事我愿不愿接。」

「你答应了？」

===Original===
“Well, what do you think? Of course I agreed. I asked for further details, and he let me have them. For both my own safety and Loth’s, I was to take extraordinary precautions. My name is pretty well known in dealers’ circles over Europe, and therefore would be known to the Soviet emissaries, so I was to take another. I was to become John Harrison, of Huddersfield, a tinplate manufacturer. I was not to write to the office direct, but to send my reports, if any were necessary, to Mr. Herbert Lyons, a friend of Mr. Duke’s, who lived not far from him at Hampstead. If I had to write, I was to be most careful to phrase my letter so that were I suspected and my correspondence tampered with, it would not give the affair away. Instructions to me would be sent to Harrison and written on plain notepaper, and would be worded in a similar careful way. Mr. Gething gave me a code by which I could wire the amount agreed on, when the money would be sent me by special messenger; that is, if we could come to terms.”

Vanderkemp paused and glanced at the Inspector, but the latter not speaking, he continued:

“Loth was hidden in Constantinople, but was trying to come west. He was not sure whether he could do so best by land or sea. If he could get out of Turkey by land, he would work his way up the Danube to Austria and Switzerland, and would stop eventually at the Beau-Sejour Hotel in Chamonix. If that proved impossible, he would try to leave by sea, and would travel by one of the Navigazione Generale Italiana boats to Genoa, and thence to Barcelona, where he would put up at the Gomez Hotel, that is, this one. He had let Mr. Duke know through his Constantinople friend that if he didn’t turn up at Chamonix by the 4th, it would mean either that the Bolsheviks had caught him, or that he was making for Barcelona. My instructions, therefore, were to go to Chamonix, put up at the Beau-Sejour, and look out until the 4th for a tall, white-complexioned, dark-haired man named Francisko Loth. If by that time he had not turned up, I was to move on here. I was to wait here for a fortnight, at the end of which time, if I had still heard nothing of him, I was to go on to Constantinople, look up Mr. Duke’s agent, and try for news of Loth’s fate.”

===Chinese===
「嗐，你说呢？我当然答应了。我又问了些细节，他都一一告知。为我自身同洛特两方面的安全起见，我得采取异乎寻常的防范措施。我的名字在欧洲珠宝同行的圈子里颇有几分响亮，苏联的密探必然也有所耳闻，所以我得改用别名。我要变成哈德斯菲尔德（Huddersfield）一个叫约翰·哈里森（John Harrison）的马口铁制造商。我不得直接写信给商行；若有报告须交，寄给杜克先生的一位朋友赫伯特·莱昂斯（Herbert Lyons），他住在汉普斯特德，离杜克家不远。万一非写信不可，措辞也要万分小心，务使我即便遭了怀疑、信件被人拆看，也不至于泄露内情。发给我的指示，将寄给哈里森，用素净的信纸书写，措辞同样字斟句酌。格辛先生还给了我一套电码，价钱谈妥后，可用它把数目拍报过来，款子届时由专差送来——前提是双方谈得拢。」

范德肯普顿了顿，瞥了探长一眼；见对方不作声，便接着说下去：

「洛特藏在君士坦丁堡，正设法西来。走陆路好还是走海路好，他自己也拿不准。若能从土耳其经陆路出境，他就沿多瑙河（Danube）一路上行，取道奥地利和瑞士，最终落脚在夏蒙尼的博塞茹尔酒店。若陆路走不通，他就设法从海路走，搭意大利通用航运公司（Navigazione Generale Italiana）的船到热那亚（Genoa），再转来巴塞罗那，住进戈麦斯酒店（Gomez Hotel）——就是眼下这一家。他已通过君士坦丁堡那位朋友知会杜克先生：倘若到 4 日他还没在夏蒙尼露面，那就要么是落进了布尔什维克手里，要么就是奔巴塞罗那来了。因此，给我的指示是：去夏蒙尼，住进博塞茹尔酒店，守到 4 日，留心一个名叫弗兰西斯科·洛特、身材高大、皮肤白皙、黑头发的人。届时他若还不露面，我便转移到这儿来，在这儿等上两个礼拜；期满仍无音讯，就继续赶往君士坦丁堡，寻着杜克先生的代理人，打听洛特的生死下落。」

===Original===
“And you carried out the instructions?”

“Yes. I went to Chamonix, and stayed there for a week. Seeing no one who could possibly be the man, I came on here, and have been waiting here ever since. Tomorrow I proposed to leave for Constantinople.”

French threw away the butt of his cigar and selected another.

“Such a trip could not be accomplished without money,” he said slowly. “How were you equipped in that way?”

“Mr. Gething handed me a hundred pounds in ten-pound notes. I changed two in Chamonix and I have the remaining eight in my pocket.”

===Chinese===
「这些指示，你都一一照办了？」

「照办了。我去了夏蒙尼，在那儿住了一个礼拜。始终没见着一个有半分可能是那人的，我便来到这儿，从此一直在这儿等。明天，我本打算动身去君士坦丁堡。」

弗伦奇掸掉雪茄的烟蒂，另点上一支。

「这样的远行，没有钱是办不成的。」他慢悠悠地说。「这一层，你是怎么备办的？」

「格辛先生交给我一百镑，都是十镑一张的票子。我在夏蒙尼兑掉了两张，剩下的八张就在我口袋里。」

===Original===
“You might let me see them.”

Vanderkemp readily complied, and the Inspector found, as he expected, that the eight notes were among those stolen from the safe. He resumed his interrogation.

“You say you reached the office in Hatton Garden about half-past eight?”

“Yes, and left about nine. My business occupied only half an hour.”

“And you saw no one except Mr. Gething?”

“No one.”

===Chinese===
「可以让我看看吗？」

范德肯普爽快照办。不出所料，探长发现那八张钞票，正在保险柜失窃的赃款之列。他重新开始盘问。

「你说你是八点半前后到的哈顿花园办公室？」

「是的，九点左右走的。我的事前后只花了半个钟头。」

「除了格辛先生，没见到别人？」

「没有。」

===Original===
French, having offered his possible future prisoner another cigar, sat silent, thinking deeply. He had no doubt that the story of the escaped Russian was a fabrication from beginning to end. Besides being an unlikely tale in itself, it broke down on the point of its authorship. Vanderkemp’s statement was that Gething had been told the story by Mr. Duke, and that Mr. Duke would have been present to tell it to him, Vanderkemp, in person, were he not prevented by some unexpected cause. This also was an obvious fabrication, but the reason of its insertion into the tale was clear enough. Without it, the story would have no authority. The use of Mr. Duke’s name was an essential part of any such scheme, just as the forging of Mr. Duke’s signature had been necessary for the letters of instruction to Schoofs and Vanderkemp.

But though French felt sure enough of his ground so far, on trying to take a further step he was held up by the same difficulty with which he had been faced in considering the forged letters. Was Gething guilty, and had he invented this elaborate plan to throw suspicion on to Vanderkemp, or was Vanderkemp the criminal, and the story his scheme for accounting for his actions since the murder? That was a real difficulty, and French sat wondering if there was no test he could apply, no way in which he could reach certainty, no trap which the victim would be unable to avoid?

For some time he could think of none, but presently an idea occurred to him which he thought might be worth while following up. Some information might be gained through the typewriting of the two forged letters. Could Vanderkemp type, and if so, was his work done with a light or heavy touch? He turned to his companion.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇又给这位日后可能的阶下囚敬了一支雪茄，自己坐在那里默默深思。那套俄国逃亡者的故事，他毫不怀疑是彻头彻尾的编造。这故事本身不仅听着不像话，来历上更有大破绽。照范德肯普的说法，这故事是杜克先生讲给格辛听的，而杜克先生本要亲自到场，讲给他范德肯普本人听，只因临时出了意外才被绊住。这显然也是编造，可把它编进故事里的用意却一清二楚：少了这一环，故事便没了权威。抬出杜克先生的名字，本是这类骗局必不可少的一环，正如写给斯霍夫斯和范德肯普的那两封指示信，非得伪造杜克先生的签名不可。

不过，尽管到这一步弗伦奇自觉脚跟已稳，再往前迈一步，他又被那道老难题拦住了——当初推敲那些伪造信件时，他碰见的正是同一道难题。是格辛有罪，编出这套周密的圈套，把嫌疑推到范德肯普头上？还是范德肯普才是真凶，这套说法正是他用来给自己凶案之后的行迹圆场的招数？这可真是块难啃的骨头。弗伦奇坐在那里琢磨：有没有什么法子可以检验，有没有一条路能走到确凿无疑，有没有一个陷阱，教那中计的人躲闪不开？

一时半晌，他想不出个所以然。可不久，他有了个主意，觉得值得试上一试。从那两封伪造信的打字上，没准儿能问出点名堂。范德肯普会不会打字？若会，他下手是轻还是重？他转向同伴。

===Original===
“I wish you would write me a short statement of your movements in London on the night of the crime, stating the times at which you arrived at and left the various places you visited. I should prefer it typed﻿—that is, if you can type. Can you?”

Vanderkemp smiled wanly.

“I think so,” he answered. “I type and write shorthand in four languages. But I’ve no machine here.”

“Borrow one from the office,” French suggested, as he expressed his admiration of the other’s prowess.

It took a personal visit to the office, but Vanderkemp, anxious to defer to the Inspector’s whims, managed to overcome the scruples of the languorous, dark-eyed beauty who reigned therein, and returned triumphant with the machine. Ten minutes later French had his timetable.

Instantly he saw that Vanderkemp typed as an expert﻿—with a light, sure touch that produced a perfect impression, but did not dint the paper. It was a point in the man’s favour. By no means conclusive, it was still by no means negligible.

===Chinese===
「我想请你写一份简短的陈述，说明案发当晚你在伦敦的行踪，注明你抵达和离开所到各处的时刻。我希望用打字机打出来——当然，前提是你会打字。会吗？」

范德肯普惨然一笑。

「大概还使得。」他答道。「打字和速记，四种语言我都拿得起来。只是我手边没有机器。」

「向账房借一台。」弗伦奇提议，同时对对方的本事称道了一番。

这一趟得亲自跑账房。不过范德肯普巴不得事事迁就探长的意，终于说动了那位坐镇账房、娇慵慵的黑眼睛美人，得意洋洋地抱着打字机回来了。十分钟后，弗伦奇拿到了那份时刻表。

他一眼便看出，范德肯普打字打得很在行——指触轻而稳，印字清晰，却不压伤纸面。这一点于他有利。虽远算不上定论，却也绝非无足轻重。

===Original===
Inspector French was puzzled. His experience told him that in this world the ordinary, natural and obvious thing happened. A man who secretly visited the scene of a crime at about the hour at which the crime was known to be committed, and who then left the country on a mysterious and improbable mission, the reality of which was denied by its alleged author, a man, further, who had in his pocket banknotes stolen from the scene of the crime, such a man in ordinary, prosaic, everyday life was the criminal. Such, French thought, was common sense, and common sense, he considered, was right ninety-nine times out of a hundred.

But there was always the hundredth chance. Improbabilities and coincidences *did* occasionally happen. He would have given a good deal at that moment to know if this case was the exception that proves the rule.

He saw clearly that his second explanation, if somewhat more far-fetched, was still quite possibly true. It certainly might be that Vanderkemp had been duped, that he had been sent on this wild goose chase by the murderer, with the object of drawing on himself just that suspicion which he had attracted, and thus allowing the real scent to cool. A good many of the facts tended in that direction, the forged letters, the keeping of the alleged deal from Schoofs, the fact that no Russian nobleman had turned up at either of the rendezvous named, the travelling under a false name, the warning against communications with the office, and last, but not least, Vanderkemp’s manner during the interview, all these undoubtedly supported the view that the traveller had been used to lay a gigantic false clue.

If so, it was a fiendish trap to set for the unfortunate dupe. French thought he could see how it was intended to pan out. Vanderkemp, while on these mysterious journeys﻿—certainly when he reached Mr. Duke’s agent in Constantinople﻿—would learn of the murder, and he would at once see how he had been victimised. The more he learned of the details, the more he would realise how completely he was in the toils. He would recognise that if he went home and told his story he would not have a dog’s chance of clearing himself, and he would turn his apparent flight into a real one, and so permanently fasten upon himself a tacit admission of guilt. It was an ingenious scheme, and if it really were the explanation of these mysterious happenings, it gave an indication of the character and mentality of the man who had devised it.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇探长又犯难了。经验告诉他：这世上发生的，多半是寻常、自然、顺理成章的那一种事。一个人，在明知案发的钟点前后秘密造访案发现场，随后又为一桩神秘而荒唐的差事出国远行，而那差事的所谓主使者又一口否认有此一事；何况此人兜里还揣着从现场偷出的钞票——这样的人，放到平平常常、柴米油盐的日子里，就是罪犯。弗伦奇想，这就是常识；而常识嘛，依他看，一百回里总有九十九回是对的。

可总还有第一百回。小概率的事、巧上加巧的事，也*委实*偶有发生。此刻他宁肯付出不小的代价，只求弄个明白：这桩案子，是不是那条「证明规则例外的例外」。

他看得很清楚：自己的第二种解释，虽说牵强一些，却仍旧大有可能成立。完全可能是范德肯普受了骗，被真凶支使得满世界乱跑，好教他把嫌疑引到自己头上，让真正的线索冷下去。不少事实都指向这一头：那两封伪造的信；所谓这笔买卖对斯霍夫斯瞒得滴水不漏；所谓那位俄国贵族，在两处约定的接头地点都杳无踪影；化名出行；不得与商行通信的告诫；末了还有顶要紧的一条——范德肯普在这次谈话中的态度。桩桩件件，都支持这个看法：这位旅行家是被人拿来铺设一条天大的假线索了。

倘若当真如此，这对那个倒霉的受骗者，可真是毒辣的陷阱。弗伦奇自觉看得出这出戏预备怎么收场。范德肯普在神秘奔波的途中——尤其到了君士坦丁堡杜克先生的代理人那里——必然得知凶案，随即恍然大悟自己是被人耍了。内情知道得越多，他越会明白自己已深陷罗网。他会意识到，倘若回老家照实讲出这段经历，洗刷自己的指望微乎其微；于是他会把表面上的潜逃弄假成真，从此在自己身上，永久钉下一枚默默认罪的钉子。这条计策真是巧妙；倘若这些蹊跷事当真缘由于此，那么设局之人的品性与心智，由此也可见一斑了。

===Original===
French was by no means decided as to the truth of the matter, but on the whole he thought that though he undoubtedly had evidence to justify him in applying for the arrest and extradition of the traveller, he would prefer to avoid this step if possible. If the man tried to give him the slip, the local police would get him in no time. Accordingly he turned once more to Vanderkemp.

“Mr. Vanderkemp,” he began, “I am strongly inclined to believe your story. But as a man of the world you will readily see that it must be more completely examined before it can be fully accepted. Now the question is, Are you willing to come back with me to London and give me your assistance towards finding out the truth? I can make you no promise that you will not be arrested on reaching British ground, but I can promise you that you will be fairly dealt with and get every chance and assistance to prove your innocence.”

Vanderkemp did not hesitate in his reply.

“I will go,” he said promptly. “I am aware that you can have me arrested here, if you want to, by applying to the Spanish authorities, so I have no choice. But I think I should go in any case. I have done nothing contrary to the law, and I have done nothing to be ashamed of. I cannot now rest until my innocence is admitted.”

===Chinese===
真相究竟如何，弗伦奇远没有拿定主意。但总的掂量下来，他觉得：虽然手上的证据无疑足以让他去申请逮捕并引渡这位旅行家，能不走这一步，还是不走为好。此人若想溜，当地警察转眼就能把他拿住。于是，他重新转向范德肯普。

「范德肯普先生，」他开口道，「我个人颇倾向于相信你的话。但你是见过世面的人，自然明白：这番话还须经受更彻底的核查，才能被完全接受。现在的问题是——你可愿意随我回伦敦，助我一臂之力，查明真相？我不能向你保证，踏上英国国土后你不会被逮捕；但我可以担保，你会得到公正的对待，并得到一切机会和协助，来证明你的清白。」

范德肯普的回答没有半分迟疑。

「我去。」他干脆地答道。「我明白，只要你愿意，向西班牙当局知会一声，就能在这儿把我逮捕，所以我别无选择。不过我想，即便如此，我也还是会去的。我没有做犯法的事，也没有做亏心的事。如今我的清白一天不被承认，我就一天不得安生。」

===Original===
French nodded gravely.

“Once again, sir, I think you are doing the wise thing. Let us go tonight by the Paris express. In the meantime come with me to the post office and help me to send a wire to the Yard.”

Two mornings later they reached London. Mr. Duke was naturally amazed at his subordinate’s story, and on hearing the evidence, gave it as his opinion that Vanderkemp was the dupe of some person or persons unknown. What was more to the point, Chief Inspector Mitchell, French’s immediate superior, took the same view, and Vanderkemp, therefore, was not arrested, though he was shadowed night and day. French undertook an investigation into his life and circumstances, which showed that these had been painted in somewhat darker colours than appeared justifiable, but which revealed no evidence about the crime. Furthermore, none of the jewels could be traced to him, nor any of the stolen notes other than those he had spoken of.

Once more the days began to slip past without bringing to light any fresh fact, and as time passed French grew more worried and despondent, and his superior officers more querulous. And then something occurred to turn his attention to a completely different side of the case, and send him off with fresh hope and energy on a new clue.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇神色庄重地点了点头。

「先生，我再一次说，你这样做是明智的。咱们今晚就搭去巴黎的快车动身。动身之前，先陪我上一趟邮局，帮我给苏格兰场拍封电报。」

两天后的一个早晨，他们抵达伦敦。杜克先生听了自己下属的这段经历，自然惊愕不已；听完证据之后，他表示依自己之见，范德肯普是中了某个或某些身份不明者的圈套。更要紧的是，弗伦奇的顶头上司、米切尔总探长（Chief Inspector Mitchell）也持同一看法。于是，范德肯普没有被捕，只是昼夜有人盯梢。弗伦奇对他的生平境况做了一番调查，查明此前的传闻把他涂抹得比实情更黑了几分，却没有查出半点与凶案相干的证据。此外，任何珠宝都追查不到他头上；被盗的钞票，除他自己提到的那八张之外，也一张不见踪影。

日子又开始一天天滑过去，带不出半点新的事实。随着时间流逝，弗伦奇愈发焦灼沮丧，他的上司们也愈发吹毛求疵。恰在此时，出了一件意外之事，把他的注意力引向案子全然不同的另一面，教他怀着新的希望和劲头，循着一条新线索上路了。

## XV The House in St. John’s Wood / 十五、圣约翰林的宅子

===Original===
It was one of Inspector French’s most constant grumbles that a man in his position was never off duty. He might come home after a hard day’s work looking forward to a long, lazy, delightful evening with a pipe and a book, and before he had finished supper some development at headquarters might upset all his plans and drag him off forthwith to do battle with the enemies of his country’s laws. Not for him was the eight-hour day, overtime at high rates, “on call” or country allowances, expenses.﻿ … His portion was to get his work done, or take the consequences in lack of promotion or even loss of such position as he held.

“And no thanks for what you carry off either,” he would complain, “though if you make a slip you hear about it before you’re an hour older.” But his eye would twinkle as he said it, and most of his friends knew that Mr. Inspector French was making an exceedingly good thing out of his job, and was, moreover, destined by his superiors for even greater and more remunerative responsibilities in the early future.

But on this evening his grouse was illustrated, if not justified. Scarcely had he sat down to his meal when a ring came to the door, and he was told that Constable Caldwell wished to speak to him.

“Let him wait,” Mrs. French answered before her better half could speak. “Show him into the sitting-room, Eliza, and give him the evening paper.”

French half rose, then sank back into his seat.

“Ask him if it’s urgent,” he called after the retreating girl, partly from genuine curiosity, and partly to preserve the fiction that he was master of his own movements in his own house.

===Chinese===
弗伦奇探长平日发得最勤的一句牢骚，就是干他这一行的人永远没有下了班的时候。他也许劳碌了一整天回到家，一心盼着抽一斗烟、读一本书，消磨一个悠长慵懒的惬意夜晚，可晚饭还没吃罢，总部那边一起变故便可能搅翻他的全盘打算，立刻把他拖出门去，同糟蹋本国法律的敌人开战。八小时工作日制、高价加班费、“随叫随到”津贴、下乡出差补贴……这些都轮不到他享受。他名下分得的只有一样：把差事办成；不然就自担后果——轻则升迁无望，重则连眼下的位子也保不住。

“抓了人也没人领你的情，”他总要这样抱怨，“可你要是失一回手，不出一个钟头就有人来训你。”不过他说这话时眼里总含着笑意。他的朋友们大多心里有数：弗伦奇探长先生这份差事实在是一桩极其划算的美缺，况且上峰已经属意于他，不久便要委以更重大、也更实惠的职责。

然而这天傍晚，他的牢骚即便说不上名正言顺，至少也得到了印证。他刚坐下要用饭，门铃便响了，随后有人来报，考德威尔巡警（Constable Caldwell）想同他说话。

“让他等着，”不等丈夫开口，弗伦奇太太先发了话，“领他到客厅去坐，伊莱扎（Eliza），再给他一张晚报。”

弗伦奇半欠起身，随即又坐了回去。

“问问他事情急不急，”他朝走出去的姑娘扬声说——一半出于当真的好奇，一半也是要维持那点体面的虚构：在自己的家里，他往哪儿挪动一步还得由自己做主。

===Original===
“It’s not so urgent as your supper. Just let him wait,” Mrs. French repeated inexorably. “What difference will a minute or two make anyway?”

Her view, it soon appeared, was upheld by the constable himself.

“He says it’s not urgent,” Eliza corroborated, reappearing at the door. “He can wait till you’re ready.”

“Very well. Let him wait,” French repeated, relieved that the incident had ended so satisfactorily, and for another fifteen minutes he continued steadily fortifying the inner man. Then taking out his pipe, he joined his visitor.

“’Evening, Caldwell. What’s wrong now?”

===Chinese===
“再急也急不过你的晚饭。就让他等着，”弗伦奇太太斩钉截铁地重申，“反正早晚这么一两分钟，有什么打紧？”

不多时便见分晓，巡警本人也持同样的见解。

“他说不急，”伊莱扎重新在门口现身，证实道，“您什么时候得空，他都能等。”

“很好。那就让他等着，”弗伦奇又说了一遍。事情这样圆满了结，他松了口气，便又足足用了一刻钟，继续踏踏实实地充实五脏庙。然后他掏出烟斗，去会他的客人。

“晚上好，考德威尔。这回又出了什么岔子？”

===Original===
Caldwell, a tall, heavy-looking man of middle age, rose clumsily to his feet and saluted.

“It’s that there circular of yours, sir,” he explained. “I’ve found the woman.”

“The deuce you have!” French cried, pausing in the act of filling his pipe and immediately keenly interested. “Who is she?”

Caldwell drew his notebook from his pocket, and slowly turned the well-thumbed pages. His deliberation irritated his quicker-witted superior.

“Get along, Caldwell,” French grumbled. “Can’t you remember that much without your blessed book?”

“Yes, sir,” the man answered. “Here it is.” He read from the book. “Her name is Mrs. Henry Vane, and she lives in a small detached house in St. John’s Wood Road; Crewe Lodge is the name.”

===Chinese===
考德威尔是个身材高大、面目敦实的中年人，笨拙地站起身来，敬了个礼。

“是您发的那张通缉传单，长官，”他说明来意，“那个女人，我找着了。”

“真有你的！”弗伦奇失声喊了出来，装烟丝的手也停住了，登时来了兴致，“她是谁？”

考德威尔从口袋里掏出笔记本，慢条斯理地翻动那几页翻得起了毛边的纸。他这份从容，惹得心思更快的那位上司心头火起。

“痛快些，考德威尔，”弗伦奇嘟囔道，“就这么点儿事，离了你那本宝贝簿子就记不住么？”

“记得住，长官，”对方答道，“喏，在这儿。”他照着簿子念：“她名叫亨利·文恩太太（Mrs. Henry Vane），住在圣约翰伍德路一幢独门独院的小房子里；宅名叫克鲁别墅（Crewe Lodge）。”

===Original===
“Good!” French said heartily. “I suppose you’re sure about it?”

“I think so, sir. I showed the photograph to three different parties, and they all said it was her.”

This sounded promising, particularly as French remembered that Dowds, the ex-doorkeeper at the Comedy, had stated that Miss Winter’s admirer was named Vane. He invited the constable to sit down and let him hear the details, offering him at the same time a fill of tobacco.

Constable Caldwell subsided gingerly into a chair as he took the proffered pouch.

“Thank you, sir, I don’t mind if I do.” He slowly filled and lighted his pipe, ramming down the tobacco with an enormous thumb. “It was this way, sir. I had that there circular of yours with the woman’s photo in my pocket when I went off duty early this afternoon. On my way home I happened to meet a friend, a young lady, and I turned and walked with her. For want of something to say, so to speak, I showed her the photo, not expecting anything to come of it, you understand. Well, the moment she looked at it, ‘I know that there woman,’ she said. ‘You what?’ I said. ‘You know her? Who is she, then?’ I said. ‘She’s a woman that comes into the shop sometimes,’ she said, ‘but I don’t just remember her name, though I have heard it,’ she said. ‘I should say the young lady, her I was speaking to, worked in a drapery shop until a couple of weeks ago, though she’s out of a job at the moment. ‘Well,’ I said, ‘I’d like to know her name. Can’t you remember it?’ ‘No,’ she said, she couldn’t remember it. She’d only heard it once, and hadn’t paid much attention to it.”

===Chinese===
“好！”弗伦奇说得由衷，“这事你拿得准么？”

“我想是拿得准的，长官。那照片我请三个人看过，他们个个都说就是她。”

这话听来大有指望，何况弗伦奇记得，喜剧剧院原先那个门房道兹说过，温特小姐的那位相好姓文恩。他请巡警落座细谈，同时把烟丝袋递过去，请他装一斗。

考德威尔接过烟袋，小心翼翼地陷坐进椅子里。

“多谢长官，那我就不推让了。”他慢吞吞地装烟、点烟，用一根大得出奇的拇指把烟丝死死摁实。“事情是这样的，长官。今天下晌我早早下了班，兜里正揣着您那张带照片的传单。回家路上凑巧遇上一位朋友，一位年轻小姐，我便掉转身陪她一道走。可以说是一时没话找话吧，我把照片掏给她瞧——您晓得，原本没指望能问出个什么。嘿，她一眼扫过去，就说：‘那女人我认得。’‘你认得？’我说。‘你认得她？那她是谁？’我说。‘她是时常上我们铺子来的一个太太，’她说，‘只是名字我一时记不起，听倒听见过的，’她说。依我看，方才同我说话的那位年轻小姐，原在一家绸布庄做工，做到两个礼拜前才离的职，眼下正闲着呢。‘好，’我说，‘我倒想知道她的名字。你就记不起来么？’‘记不起来，’她说，实在记不起来。那名字她只听过一回，也没大放在心上。”

===Original===
“Yes?” French murmured encouragingly as the constable showed signs of coming to an end.

“I said that if she couldn’t remember, that maybe some of the other young ladies might know it. She wasn’t having any at first, for I had promised to take her to tea and on to the pictures, and she was set on going. But when she saw I was in earnest, she gave in, and we went round to the shop she used to work in. After asking three or four of the girls, we found one that remembered the woman all right. ‘That’s Mrs. Vane,’ she said. ‘She lives up there in St. John’s Wood; Crewe Lodge is the name. I’ve made up her parcels often enough to know.’”

“Good,” French approved once more in his hearty voice.

“I thought I had maybe better make sure about it,” went on the constable in his slow, heavy way, “so I asked Miss Swann﻿—that was the young lady that I was with﻿—to walk round that way with me. I found the house near the Baker Street end, a small place and very shut in. I didn’t want to go up and make inquiries, so I asked Miss Swann if she’d go next door and ask if Mrs. Vane was in. She went and asked, and they told her to go next door; that was to Crewe Lodge. So when I saw it was all right, I put off going to the pictures for this evening and came straight here to tell you.”

French beamed on him.

“You’ve done well, Constable,” he declared. “In fact, I couldn’t have done it better myself. I shall see that you don’t lose by it. Take another fill of tobacco while I get ready, and then call a taxi and we’ll go right out now.”

===Chinese===
“后来呢？”眼见巡警的话头将近尾声，弗伦奇便低声相催。

“我说，她既然记不起，兴许旁的年轻小姐里有人知道。起初她说什么也不肯——我原答应带她去吃茶点、看电影，她一门心思全在这上头。可等她见我是认真的，就让了步，我们便绕到她先前做工的那家铺子去。前前后后问了三四位姑娘，问到一位，把那太太记得真真切切。‘那是文恩太太，’她说。‘她家就住在圣约翰林那一头；宅名叫克鲁别墅。她买的货，我替她打包过不知多少回，保准错不了。’”

“好，”弗伦奇又用那副热乎乎的嗓音赞了一声。

“我想这事还是办得牢靠些好，”巡警仍旧拖着他那副缓慢沉重的腔调说下去，“便请斯旺小姐（Miss Swann）——就是方才陪我的那位年轻小姐——陪我绕到那一带去。那宅子在靠贝克街（Baker Street）的一头，我寻着了，小小一处，四面遮得严严实实。我不想自己上门打听，便问斯旺小姐肯不肯替我去隔壁问问文恩太太在不在家。她去了，人家让她再往隔一家问——那便是克鲁别墅。我见确实无误，今晚的电影也不瞧了，径直上您这儿来回话。”

弗伦奇满面笑容地看着他。

“你干得漂亮，巡警，”他宣称，“说句实在话，换了我自己也不过如此。这事少不了你的好处。趁我收拾的工夫，再装一斗烟，然后叫辆出租车，咱们即刻就出动。”

===Original===
He rang up Scotland Yard, asking for certain arrangements to be made, with the result that by the time he and Constable Caldwell reached the great building, two plain clothes men were waiting for them, one of whom handed French a small handbag and a warrant for the arrest of Mrs. Vane, alias Mrs. Ward, alias Mrs. Root of Pittsburg, U.S.A. Then the four officers squeezing into the taxi, they set off for St. John’s Wood Road.

Big Ben was striking half-past nine as they turned into Whitehall. The night was fine, but there was no moon, and outside the radius of the street lamps it was pitchy dark. The four men sat in silence after French had in a few words explained their errand to the newcomers. He and Caldwell were both in a state of suppressed excitement, French owing to the hope of an early solution of his difficulties, the constable to the possibilities of promotion which a successful issue to the expedition might involve. The other two looked upon the matter as a mere extra job of work, and showed a lamentable lack of interest in the proceedings.

They pulled up at St. John’s Wood Road, and dismissing the taxi, followed Constable Caldwell to the gate of a carriage drive which there pierced the high stone wall separating the houses from the street. On the upper bar of the gate were the words, “Crewe Lodge.” To the right hand was a wicket gate, but both it and the larger one were closed. Inside the wall was a thick belt of trees through which the drive curved back, and, lit up through the interstices of the branches by the street lamps, the walls and gable of a small house showed dimly beyond. No light was visible from the windows, and, after a moment’s hesitation, French opened the wicket gate and all four entered.

“Wait here among the trees, Pye and Frankland,” he whispered. “Caldwell, you come on with me.”

The drive was short, not more than forty yards long, and the complete outline of the house was speedily revealed. It seemed even smaller than the first glance had shown, but was charmingly designed, with a broken-up roof, large bow windows, and a tiny loggia into which opened a glass panelled door. To be so near the centre of a great city, it was extraordinarily secluded, the trees and wall, together with some clumps of evergreen shrubs, cutting off all view of the road and the neighbouring houses.

===Chinese===
他打电话到苏格兰场，交代了几项安排。待他和考德威尔巡警赶到那座大楼时，两名便衣已在等候，其中一个交给弗伦奇一只小手提包，还有一张拘捕文恩太太——化名沃德夫人、再化名美利坚合众国匹兹堡的鲁特夫人——的逮捕证。于是四名警官挤进出租车，向圣约翰伍德路驶去。

车子拐进白厅（Whitehall）时，大本钟（Big Ben）正敲响九点半。夜里天色晴朗，只是没有月亮，路灯光圈以外一团漆黑。弗伦奇三言两语向后来的两人交代完来意，四人便都默默坐着。他和考德威尔都憋着一股压不住的兴奋：弗伦奇是盼着自己的难题早早了结，巡警是想着此行若能旗开得胜，升迁未必没有指望。另外两位却只把这事当成额外派下来的一桩活计，那副意兴阑珊的样子，着实叫人不敢恭维。

他们在圣约翰伍德路停了车，打发走出租车，跟着考德威尔巡警来到一道车马大门前。大门开在高高的石墙上，把一座座宅院同大街隔开。门的上档上钉着“克鲁别墅”几个字。右手边有一扇步行的小门，可小门大门都关着。墙内围着一带密树，车道从树丛里弯着绕向纵深，隔着枝叶的缝隙，借街灯的光，隐隐显出一所小房子的墙身和山墙。窗内望不见一点灯火。略一踌躇之后，弗伦奇推开小门，四个人鱼贯而入。

“派伊（Pye）、弗兰克兰（Frankland），你们俩就在这树丛里等着，”他低声说，“考德威尔，你跟我来。”

车道不长，至多四十来码，房子的全貌很快显了出来。它比初看时还要显得小些，可设计得精巧可人：屋顶错落起伏，几扇凸窗宽大，还有一座小小的凉廊，一扇嵌玻璃格的门正开向廊下。离大城市的中心这么近，却幽僻得出奇——树木同高墙，再加上几丛常绿灌木，把街上和四邻八舍的视线遮了个滴水不漏。

===Original===
The front of the house was in complete darkness, and instinctively treading stealthily, the two men moved round to the side. Here also there was no light, and they pushed slowly on until they had completed the circuit and once more reached the front door.

“Looks as if the place is empty,” French whispered as he pressed the electric bell.

There was no response to his repeated rings. The house remained dark and silent. French turned again to the constable.

“Call up those other two men,” he ordered, and soon Pye was posted at the corner between the front and side, and Frankland at that diagonally opposite, with orders to keep out of sight and to allow anyone who came to enter, but no one to leave the building.

Electric torch in hand, French then began a guarded survey of the doors and windows. Finally fixing on the door opening on the loggia, he made Caldwell hold the light while, first with a bunch of skeleton keys, and then with a bit of wire, he operated on the lock. For several minutes he worked, but at last with a snap the bolt shot back, and turning the handle, the two men cautiously entered the room and closed the door behind them.

They found themselves in a small, expensively-furnished sitting-room, evidently a lady’s. It was fitted up in a somewhat flamboyant and pretentious manner, as if costliness rather than good taste had been the chief consideration in its furnishing. It was unoccupied, but looked as if it had been recently used, there being ashes in the grate and books lying about, one of which lay open face downwards on a chair. On an occasional table stood an afternoon tea equipage with one used cup.

===Chinese===
房子正面黑得严严实实，两个人不由得放轻脚步，绕向侧翼。这边也不见灯火，他们慢慢往前蹭，直把整座房子兜了一圈，重新回到正门前。

“瞧这光景，屋里像是没人，”弗伦奇一边低声说，一边按响了电铃。

他按了一遍又一遍，毫无回应。房子依旧又黑又静。弗伦奇重新转向巡警。

“把那两位叫过来，”他命令道。不多时，派伊守住了正面同侧面夹角的墙角，弗兰克兰守住斜对角，各自领了吩咐：藏好身形，来人一概放入，宅中之人一个也不许出去。

弗伦奇手里拿着电筒，开始小心探看各处门窗。末了他选中那扇通凉廊的门，叫考德威尔替他掌灯，自己先使一串万能钥匙、后使一截铁丝，在锁上下起功夫来。捣鼓了好几分钟，末了“咔哒”一响，锁舌弹了回去。他一拧把手，两人蹑手蹑脚进了屋，随手把门掩上。

他们置身于一间陈设讲究的小客厅，一望便知是女眷的屋子。屋里的布置颇为俗艳浮夸，仿佛当初置办家具，头一条讲究的是价钱，而非趣味。屋里没有人，却看得出新近还有人用过：壁炉里有灰，几本书随手摊放，其中一本摊开着、面朝下扣在椅子上。一张茶几上摆着一套下午茶具，里头有一只用过的茶杯。

===Original===
French did not remain to make any closer examination, but passed on to a tiny hall, off which opened three other rooms, and from which the staircase led to the first floor. Beneath the stairs was a row of clothes-hooks on which were hanging a man’s garments, a couple of hats and coats, and a waterproof.

Rapidly he glanced into the other rooms. The first was a smoking-room, a man’s room, furnished with dark-coloured, leather upholstery, and walls panelled in dark oak. Next door was a dining-room, also small, but containing a quantity of valuable silver. The fourth door led to the kitchen, scullery, pantry, and yard. Here also there were evidences of recent occupation in the general untidiness, as well as in the food which these places contained.

Satisfied that no one was concealed on the ground floor, French led the way upstairs. In the largest bedroom, evidently that of the mistress of the house, there was a scene almost of confusion. Drawers and wardrobe lay open, their contents tumbled and tossed, while the floor was littered with dresses, shoes, and other dainty articles of feminine apparel. French swore beneath his breath when he saw the mess. Things were beginning to look uncommonly like as if the bird had flown. However, it was possible that someone might arrive at any minute, and he hurriedly continued his search.

Next door was a man’s dressing-room and bedroom. Here there was not the same litter, nor was the unoccupied bedroom adjoining other than tidy, but in the maids’ room, which he next entered, it was evident there had been a recent clearing out. Here the wardrobe drawers were pulled out and the door of a hanging press in the wall was standing open. Papers and a few obviously worn-out garments littered the floor. But the room differed from Madame’s in that everything of value had been taken.

French swore again. There seemed no doubt that he was late. Mrs. X, alias Mrs. Vane, had taken fright and fled. If so, what hint, he wondered, had she received of her danger?

He stood for a moment in the disordered room, thinking. Under these new circumstances, what was his proper course?

===Chinese===
弗伦奇未作细看，径直走进一间小门厅。厅里另有三扇门分通别的房间，楼梯也打这里通向二楼。楼梯底下钉着一排衣钩，挂着男人的衣装：两顶帽子、两件外衣，还有一件雨衣。

他飞快朝各个房间里扫了一眼。头一间是吸烟室，男人的房间，摆着深色皮面的家具，墙板也镶着深色橡木。隔壁是餐室，同样不大，却陈着一批贵重的银器。第四扇门通向厨房、洗涤间、配膳室和后院。这几处也一样留有人新近起居的痕迹：处处凌乱不说，还堆着各色吃食。

确认底层没人藏身，弗伦奇便领头上了楼。最大的一间卧室显然是宅子女主人的，屋里几乎是一片狼藉：抽屉、衣柜全数敞开，里面的东西翻得乱七八糟，地板上散落着连衣裙、鞋子，还有种种精致的女人衣物。眼见这副光景，弗伦奇低声咒了一句。事情越看越像“鸟儿已经飞了”。不过人说不定什么时候就回来，他不敢耽搁，赶忙接着搜下去。

隔壁是男主人的一间更衣室兼卧室。这里没有那样满地狼藉，旁边那间空着的卧室倒也齐整，可是他随后走进的女仆房，明摆着新近刚清空：衣柜的抽屉一个个拉了出来，墙上一口吊柜的门敞着，地板上摊着纸片和几件分明穿旧了的衣裳。只是这间屋子同太太那间有别：但凡值钱的东西，全都带走了。

弗伦奇又咒骂了一声。看来毫无疑问，他是迟了一步。X太太——化名文恩太太的那位——已经受惊逃走了。果真如此，他暗自纳罕，她是得了什么风声，知道自己大祸临头了呢？

他在那间狼藉的屋子里立了片刻，思量着。情势至此，他下一步该走的正着是什么？

===Original===
First, it was obvious that he must make absolutely sure that this Mrs. Vane was really the woman he sought. Next, he must learn if she had really gone, and, if so, why, and, if possible, where. If her departure was a flight, he must find out how or by whom she had been warned. Lastly, he must follow her to her hiding-place and arrest her.

But he must not end with Mrs. Vane. Her husband must also be found. If she was Mrs. X, the receiver of the stolen diamonds, possibly the murderer of old Gething, Mr. Vane must be in it, too. It was inconceivable that he could have avoided becoming involved.

His first job must therefore be to make all the inquiries he could as to the mysterious occupants of Crewe Lodge. There were several obvious lines of research. First there was the house itself. People left the impress of their personalities on the houses they inhabited, and a careful search of this one must yield considerable information as to the pair. Next there were the servants. If they could be found, their testimony might prove invaluable. From the neighbours and local tradesmen and dealers he did not expect so much, but among them all some useful hints would surely be gleaned. Lastly, there were the house agents. They might or might not be able to help.

It was by this time nearly eleven, but he decided that his obvious duty was then and there to begin the search of the house, even if it meant an all-night job. He therefore called in Pye and Frankland, who were experienced in such work, leaving Constable Caldwell to patrol the grounds.

Then commenced an investigation of the most meticulous and thorough description. Taking the house room by room, the three men went over with the utmost care every piece of furniture, every book, every paper, every article of clothing. Hour after hour the search proceeded in spite of a growing weariness and hunger, and it was not until half-past six on the following morning that it was complete. Then in the growing daylight the three Yard men slipped out one by one on to the road, and joining forces round the corner, walked to the nearest tube station, and went to their several houses for breakfast. French rang up the Yard from the first exchange they came to, and arranged for a man to be sent to relieve Caldwell, who had been left in charge.

===Chinese===
第一，明摆着他必须查个铁实：这位文恩太太究竟是不是他要寻的那个女人。第二，他必须查明她是不是真的走了；若是走了，为的什么，可能的话，去了哪里。倘若她这一走是畏罪潜逃，他就得查清她是怎样、又是被谁得了信儿的。最后，他必须追到她的藏身之处，将她捉拿归案。

可这笔账不能到文恩太太就了结。她丈夫也必须寻着。倘若她就是X太太——那批失窃钻石的窝主、说不定还是杀害老格辛的凶手——那么文恩先生必定也在里头。他居然能不沾包，那是不可想象的。

因此，他的头一件事，就是把克鲁别墅这来历不明的宅中人的底细，能打听的尽量打听来。眼下就有几条现成的研究路子。其一是房子本身。人住过的屋子，总脱不开留下主人脾性的印记，把这所房子细搜一遍，对这一对男女必能摸到不少底。其二是仆人。倘若寻得着，他们的证词说不定贵重得很。对左邻右舍和本地的大小商贩，他不敢指望这么多，可七嘴八舌之间，总能捡出些有用的线索来。最后是房屋经纪人。他们帮不帮得上忙，倒还在两可。

这时已近十一点，可他认定，自己分内当做的事，就是当场动手搜查这所房子，哪怕干个通宵也在所不惜。于是他把办这种差事颇有经验的派伊和弗兰克兰唤了进来，留考德威尔巡警在外巡查园子。

于是一场至为缜密、至为彻底的搜查开始了。三个人把这所房子逐屋过筛，每一件家具、每一本书、每一张纸、每一件衣物，无不细细翻检。搜查一小时一小时地往下延，尽管乏劲和饥肠一阵紧似一阵，直到次日早晨六点半才算功德圆满。随后，趁着渐亮的天光，三个苏格兰场的人一个接一个溜上大街，在拐角处会齐，步行到最近的地铁站，各自回家用早饭。弗伦奇在到站的第一处电话局给苏格兰场挂了电话，安排再派一人去接替留下来看家的考德威尔。

===Original===
As French smoked his after-breakfast pipe before returning to the Yard, he jotted down in his notebook a list of the points which had struck him during the search. There was nothing that led him to either Mr. or Mrs. Vane, but there was a certain amount that was suggestive.

In the first place, it seemed evident that the departure of the lady had been sudden and unexpected. There was the evidence of the disordered bedrooms, of the used-looking sitting-room with the book evidently laid down where it could be picked up again without losing the place, of the ashes in the sitting-room fireplace and range, the used tea tray, and of the kitchen. There it appeared that cooking had been just about to begin, for a number of saucepans were on the range, and various kinds of food lay on the table as if ready for the saucepans. There was a good deal of food of various kinds about the kitchen and larder, and some wine and whisky in the dining-room sideboard. On the other hand, there was no evidence of any hurried departure on the part of the master of the house.

The date of the departure French thought he could roughly fix from the condition of the food. The milk, of which there was a bowl and two jugs, was sour, but not thick. Some fresh meat hanging in the larder was good. The bread was rather dry and hard. Some lettuces lying on a shelf in the scullery had gone limp. But some bunches of chrysanthemums standing in water in the sitting-room, were quite fresh.

On the whole, he thought the evidence pointed to a flight some four days earlier, and this view was supported by another piece of evidence on which he had come.

In the letter box at the back of the hall door he had found a letter addressed “Mrs. Vane, Crewe Lodge, St. John’s Wood Road.” The postmark showed that it had been posted in London on the 3rd. It had, therefore, been delivered on the evening of the 3rd or morning of the 4th. But this was the 8th. Therefore the lady had gone at least four days earlier.

===Chinese===
早饭之后、回苏格兰场之前，弗伦奇一边抽着饭后的烟斗，一边把搜查中记下的疑点誊了一份清单在笔记本上。没有一条能把人引到文恩先生或太太身上，可耐人寻味之处着实有一些。

首先，那位太太的出走，显然突如其来、全无预兆。证据俱在：凌乱的卧室；那间一看便知方才还有人待过的客厅，那本书分明是随手撂下的，撂的地方一伸手便可再拾起来、还不致丢失页码；客厅壁炉和灶膛里的余灰；用过的茶盘；还有厨房。厨房里看光景正要开火做饭——灶上坐着好几口锅，桌上摊着各样食物，仿佛专候下锅。厨房和食物柜里存着不少各色吃食，餐室碗柜里还有几瓶酒和威士忌。反过来，宅子男主人那边，却看不出半点仓促动身的痕迹。

出走的日期，弗伦奇想，凭食物的状况大致能够断定。牛奶——满满一碗外加两罐——已经酸了，却尚未凝滞。食物柜里挂着的鲜肉还好好的。面包已经干硬。洗涤间搁板上的几棵生菜蔫软了。可客厅里养在水中的几束菊花，还相当精神。

大体看来，他认为这些迹象指向约莫四天前的一次出走；而他随后到手的一项证据，也印证了这个判断。

在正门背后的信箱里，他寻到一封信，收信人是“圣约翰伍德路克鲁别墅文恩太太”。邮戳显示，此信是 3 日在伦敦付邮的。那么，它或在 3 日晚间、或在 4 日早晨送到了。可当天已是 8 日。可见那位太太至少已经走了四天。

===Original===
The letter itself had considerably intrigued him. It was simply a list of certain sales and purchases of stock, covering a large number of transactions, and running into some thousands of pounds in value. The items were not dated, and there was no accompanying letter nor any intimation of the sender. It was clear that someone was engaged in complicated financial operations, but there was nothing to indicate his or her identity.

That the Vanes were at least comfortably off seemed certain from the general appointments of the house. The furniture and fittings were heavy and expensive. The sitting-room was small, as has been stated, but French reckoned that the carpet would not have been bought for less than £120. Madame’s dresses were of rich silks, and while no actual jewellery had been left behind, there were costly ornaments and personal knickknacks. Moreover, the half-empty box of cigars in the smoking-room contained Corona Coronas. There was, however, no garage and no car, but it was obvious that a car might have been kept at some neighbouring establishment. Altogether it looked as if the couple had been living at the rate of two or three thousand a year. But this was a matter that could easily be tested, as the name of Mrs. Vane’s bank was among her papers.

One other point struck the Inspector as curious. Neither the master nor the mistress of the house seemed to have literary tastes. There was a number of well-bound “standard works” in a bookcase in the smoking-room, but it was evident from their condition that they were there purely as part of the decorative scheme. Of actually read books in the smoking-room there were none. In the sitting-room were a number of the lighter type of novels, together with a number in French and Spanish with extremely lurid and compromising jackets. But among these, as out of place as an Elijah at a feast of Baal, lay a new copy of *The Concise Oxford Dictionary*.

There were several old bills in Madame’s inlaid davenport, but save for the names of firms with whom the lady had recently been dealing, French had learned nothing from them. In the sitting-room also was an excellent cabinet photograph of a lady who seemed to him the original of Mrs. Root’s steamer snapshot, and this he had slipped into his jacket pocket.

===Chinese===
那封信本身，很叫他费了一番猜疑。它无非是一纸股票买卖清单，罗列的交易足有几十笔，金额累到数千镑。各笔底下不注日期，没有附函，也没有半点能看出寄信人身份的表示。分明有人在做着一摊繁复的金融买卖，可此人是男是女、姓甚名谁，却无从捉摸。

文恩夫妇至少衣食无忧，这一点从宅中的通体陈设看得出来。家具装修厚重而昂贵。客厅不大，前文已说过，可弗伦奇估摸，单是那张地毯，没有 120 镑怕就置办不来。太太的衣裙都是上好丝绸；虽说真正的首饰一件也没留下，值钱的饰物和随身小玩意儿却也不少。再者，吸烟室里那半盒雪茄，装的是科罗娜·科罗娜（Corona Coronas）牌。只是既没有车库，也没有汽车，不过一辆车寄存在左近哪家车行，本也是明摆着的事。通盘算来，这对夫妇过日子的谱儿，一年怕不要两三千镑。好在这一点不难查证，因为文恩太太的文件里，就写着她那家银行的名字。

还有一点叫探长觉得蹊跷。这一家的男女主人，似乎都没有读书的雅兴。吸烟室书柜里倒有一排装帧堂皇的“标准名著”，可一看成色便知，它们纯是充作屋中装饰的一部分。真正读过的书，吸烟室里一本也无。客厅里有若干轻艳一路的小说，还有一批法文、西班牙文的，封面香艳露骨，很不体面。可是夹在其中，恰似巴力筵席上的以利亚一般格格不入，端端正正躺着一本崭新的《简明牛津词典》（The Concise Oxford Dictionary）。

太太那张镶嵌细工的斜面小书桌里收着几张旧账单，只是除了一些她新近打交道的商行名号，弗伦奇没能从中问出什么。客厅里还有一张上好的橱柜式照片，照的是位太太，在他看来，正是鲁特夫人那张邮船快照上的本人。这一张，他已顺手揣进了上衣口袋。

===Original===
Having completed his notes, he knocked the ashes out of his pipe and set out upon the business of the day. Returning to St. John’s Wood Road, he interviewed Esler, the constable who had been sent to relieve Caldwell, and learned that no one had as yet approached the house. Then he began to call at the adjoining houses and nearer shops. At each he stated that he was looking for Mrs. Vane, but that her house was shut up, and asked if anyone could tell him how he might find her.

Aware that in a great city neighbours might live beside each other for years without ever meeting, he did not hope for much result, and at the first two houses at which he called he did not get any. But at the third he had an unexpected stroke of luck. The maid who opened the door seemed to know something about the Vane household. But she was suspicious, and on French’s putting his usual questions, showed evident unwillingness to give away information. Keeping any suggestion of eagerness out of his manner, French went on conversationally:

“I wanted to see Mrs. Vane about a question of the ownership of a field in the country near Canterbury, where she used to live. I represent Messrs. Hill & Lewesham, the solicitors of Lincoln’s Inn, and we want some information about the boundaries of her father’s place. It’s not exactly important, but it would be worth five shillings to me to get in touch with her, and if you could see your way to help me, you’d have very fairly earned it.”

The girl seemed impressed. She glanced back into the hall, came out into the porch, and drawing the door to after her, spoke rather hurriedly.

===Chinese===
笔记做完，他把烟斗里的灰磕了出来，着手办这一天正事。他回到圣约翰伍德路，先见了奉派来换班的巡警埃斯勒（Esler），得知至今没有人挨近过这所房子。然后他开始挨门拜访紧邻的人家和附近的铺面。每到一处，他都说自己在寻文恩太太，她家却是门户紧闭，又问可有人能指点他上哪儿找她。

他心里有数：在大都市里，比邻而居的人也许多年互不见面，所以并不抱多大指望；头两家登门，果然一无所获。可到第三家，他意外交上了好运。开门的女仆对文恩家的事似乎知道一二。只是她满腹狐疑，弗伦奇照例发问，她便露出大不情愿走漏消息的神气。弗伦奇丝毫不让语气里带出半点急切，只管像闲谈似的说下去：

“我想见文恩太太，是为着她从前住过的坎特伯雷（Canterbury）近郊乡下、一块田地的归属。我是林肯律师学院的希尔与刘易舍姆律师事务所（Messrs. Hill & Lewesham）的人，我们想打听她父亲那份产业的界址。事情算不上顶要紧，不过若能同她接上头，我情愿破费五先令；你要是肯想法子帮这个忙，这五先令你就挣得再公道没有了。”

姑娘像是被打动了。她回头朝门厅里望了一眼，走到门廊上，随手掩上房门，说得颇为急促。

===Original===
“I don’t know much about it,” she explained, “but I’ll tell you what I can,” and she went on to say that on the previous Friday, that was five days earlier, Mrs. Vane had got a cable that her husband in New York had met with a serious accident and was dying, and for her to go at once. She had packed hurriedly and driven off to catch the boat train for Liverpool, closing the house. As to Mr. Vane himself, the girl knew nothing. She seemed to consider him a negligible part of the establishment. He was but seldom at home, and even then was rarely to be seen.

French asked her how it came that she knew so much about the family, and she explained that she and Mrs. Vane’s housemaid had become acquainted over her young gentleman’s model aeroplane, which had flown over the dividing wall into the grounds of Crewe Lodge, and which had been ignominiously handed back by the girl in question. As a result of the incident an acquaintance had grown up between the two, in the course of which much information as to their respective employers had been exchanged. On that Friday evening Mrs. Vane’s maid had called the narrator to the wall by means of a certain signal which they had devised, and had hurriedly told her of her mistress’s sudden call to America, and also that the house was being closed and the services of herself and the cook dispensed with. “She’s in a most terrible fluster to catch the boat train,” the girl had said, “and we have to be out before her so that she may lock up the house.” The girl had breathlessly bid her friend goodbye and had vanished.

Though French was delighted to have learned these facts, they were not in themselves all that he could have wished. The story of the husband in New York might be true, in which case a good deal of the theory he had been building up would fall to the ground. It would, however, be an easy matter to find out whether the lady really did sail on the date in question. He turned back to the servant.

“I should like very much to find that friend of yours,” he said. “Could you give me her name and address?”

Her name, it appeared, was Susan Scott, but her address was not known. For a moment French was at a loss, then by judicious questions he elicited the facts that Miss Scott spoke like a Londoner, and that she probably patronised one of the several registry offices to be found in the region surrounding the Edgware Road.

===Chinese===
“这事我知之不多，”她解释道，“可我能说的都说与您。”接着她讲：就在上一个礼拜五，也就是五天前，文恩太太接到一封电报，说她那在纽约的丈夫遭了严重事故、命在旦夕，叫她即刻动身。她匆匆收拾了行装，赶赴利物浦的邮船列车而去，把宅子也锁闭了。至于文恩先生本人，姑娘一概不知。她似乎把这一位当作这户人家中无足轻重的一部分：他难得落家，便是在家，也轻易见不着人影。

弗伦奇问她，怎么会晓得这一家这许多事。她便解释说，她同文恩家的室内女仆结交起来，起头是为着她那位小情人的模型飞机：那飞机飞过隔墙，落进了克鲁别墅的园子，又被那位女仆灰溜溜地送还回来。经这一桩事，两人渐渐熟络，闲谈之间，各自东家的底细也交换了不少。礼拜五那天傍晚，文恩太太的女仆用两人约定的暗号把她唤到墙边，急匆匆告诉她：女主人得了急信要立时赴美，宅子要关门，她自己和厨娘的差事也都散了。“她赶邮船列车，急得七荤八素，”那姑娘当时说，“我们还得赶在她前头搬出去，她才好锁门。”姑娘气都没喘匀，同她的朋友道了别，转身便没了影儿。

这些实情虽然叫弗伦奇喜出望外，本身却未必尽如他的心意。她丈夫在纽约出事的说法或许属实；倘若属实，他垒起的那套推论便要塌掉一大截。好在查明那位太太究竟是否真在所说的日子上了船，并非难事。他回身又向着那女仆。

“我很想寻着你说起的那位朋友，”他说，“她的姓名住址，你能给我么？”

姓名倒是问出来了：苏珊·斯科特。住址却无人知晓。弗伦奇一时没了主意，随即靠几个问得恰如其分的问题，套出两条实情：斯科特小姐说话是地道伦敦口音；她多半在埃奇韦尔路左近那几家职业介绍所里的一家挂过号。

===Original===
“Now there is just one other thing,” he added. “Can you tell me the name of the landlord or agents of Crewe Lodge?”

The girl was sorry she couldn’t.

“Then of this house?” French persisted. “As they are close together, the two places may belong to the same man.”

The girl did not know that either, but she said that her master would know, and that he had not yet gone out. French asked for an interview, and on stating his identity, received the information that the agents for both houses were Messrs. Findlater & Hynd, of Cupples Street, behind the Haymarket.

Thinking he had got all the information he could, French paid over his five shillings to the maid and took his departure.

===Chinese===
“如今只剩一件事了，”他又添一句，“克鲁别墅的房东或者经纪人姓甚名谁，你能告诉我么？”

姑娘很抱歉，说不知道。

“那这所房子的呢？”弗伦奇追问，“两家紧挨着，说不定同属一个东家。”

这个姑娘也不知道，只是说她的东家想必晓得，而东家还没有出门。弗伦奇求见，一报身份，便得了信儿：两所房子的经纪人，都是海马克特（Haymarket）后身卡普尔斯街（Cupples Street）的芬德莱特与海恩德事务所（Messrs. Findlater & Hynd）。

想来问得到手的已尽于此，弗伦奇把那五先令付与女仆，起身告辞。

===Original===
The next item on his programme was a visit to Mr. Williams, and twenty minutes later he pushed open the door of the office in Cockspur Street. Mr. Williams greeted him with what with him took the place of enthusiasm.

“Good day, Inspector,” he exclaimed, “I’m glad to see you. You bring me some good news, I hope?”

French sat down and drew from his pocket the cabinet photograph of Mrs. Vane which he had found in that lady’s sitting-room.

“I don’t know, Mr. Williams,” he answered quietly, “whether that will be news to you or not.”

Mr. Williams’s eyes flashed with excitement as he saw the portrait.

===Chinese===
他日程上的下一项，是拜望威廉斯先生（Mr. Williams）。二十分钟后，他推开了科克斯珀街那间办公室的门。威廉斯先生迎上前来招呼，那副神情，在他已算得上热情奔放了。

“日安，探长，”他嚷道，“见到你真高兴。我盼着你是给我捎好消息来的吧？”

弗伦奇落座，从口袋里取出他在那位太太客厅里寻到的文恩太太的橱柜式照片。

“威廉斯先生，”他不慌不忙地答道，“这个于你是否算得上新闻，我说不准。”

一见那张相片，威廉斯先生的眼睛里霎时迸出兴奋的光。

===Original===
“Bless my soul!” he cried. “Have you found her at last? Mrs. Root!”

“That’s what I wanted to ask you. Are you sure it is Mrs. Root?”

“Sure? Absolutely positive. At least, that’s the woman who got my three thousand pounds, whatever her name may be. Have you found her?”

“Well no,” French admitted. “I’ve not found her yet. But I’m in hopes.”

“Tell me about it.”

===Chinese===
“我的老天爷！”他叫出声来，“你们总算寻着她了？鲁特夫人！”

“这正是我要请教的。你拿得准她是鲁特夫人？”

“拿得准？千真万确。至少，骗走我三千镑的就是这个女人，不管她姓甚名谁。你们把她寻着了？”

“这倒没有，”弗伦奇承认，“还没有寻着。不过我有指望。”

“说与我听听。”

===Original===
“Unfortunately, there’s not much to tell. I’ve got information to the effect that this woman, the original of the photograph, left for New York last Friday. I don’t know if it’s true. If it is, the American police will get her on the ship.”

Mr. Williams pressed for details, but French was reticent. However, before leaving he promised to let the other know the result of his further inquiries.

From Cockspur Street it was but a short distance to the office of the house agents, Messrs. Findlater & Hynd. Here French saw Mr. Hynd, and learned that the firm were agents for Crewe Lodge. But beyond this fact he learned little of interest and nothing helpful. The house had been taken five years previously by Mrs. Vane, though Mr. Vane had signed the lease. They were very desirable tenants, paying their rent promptly and not demanding continual repairs.

“One more call before lunch,” French thought, and a few minutes later he turned into the office of the White Star line. Here, though it did not exactly surprise him, he received some information which gave him considerably to think, and incidentally reassured him that at last he was on the right track. No steamer, either of the White Star or of any other line, had left Liverpool for America before the previous Saturday afternoon, and there was no boat train from Euston on the Friday night.

Mrs. Vane was therefore without any doubt the woman of whom he was in search, and her departure was definitely a flight.

===Chinese===
“不巧，没什么可多说的。我得到的消息是，这照片上的女人，上礼拜五动身去了纽约。是真是假，我不知情。若是真的，美国警方自会在船上把她拿下。”

威廉斯先生追问底细，弗伦奇却守口如瓶。不过临走之前，他答应把后续查访的结果告知对方。

从科克斯珀街到那房屋经纪人事务所不过几步路。在芬德莱特与海恩德那里，弗伦奇见到了海恩德先生（Mr. Hynd），得知该行正是克鲁别墅的经纪人。可除这一条外，再没打听到什么有意思或有用场的东西。那房子是五年前文恩太太租下的，租约上署名的却是文恩先生。他们是再称心不过的房客：租金按期照付，也不三天两头索讨修缮。

“午饭前还能再访一处，”弗伦奇想。几分钟后，他拐进了白星公司的办公室。在这里，虽说算不得全出意料，他得着的一番话却够他好生琢磨一阵，顺带也叫他安了心：自己到底走上正路了。上个星期六下午之前，无论白星公司还是任何别家轮船公司，都没有一班轮船从利物浦启程赴美；礼拜五晚间，尤斯顿也没有开出任何一班邮船列车。

因此，文恩太太毫无疑问正是他寻查的那个女人，而她的出走，铁定是畏罪潜逃。

## XVIII The S.S. *Enoch* / 十八、“伊诺克号”

===Original===
In the vast organisation of Scotland Yard the indexing of information on every available subject has been brought to something more than a fine art. If French had wished to know the number of inhabitants of Prague, the favourite recreations of the Elder Brethren of Trinity House, or the width of the Ganges at Allahabad, some notes or books of reference would immediately have been forthcoming which would have fully supplied the desired information. How much more when the question was merely one of trains and steamers. He had not long to wait for an answer to his telephone, and this revealed the fact that the Booth liner *Enoch*, which had left Liverpool on the previous afternoon, called at Havre, Oporto, Lisbon, Madeira, and Para, before completing her voyage to Manáos by a sail of a thousand miles up the Amazon. Moreover, she awaited at Havre the arrival of the Southhampton boat, the connection of which left Waterloo at 9:30 on the night of the 27th.

“Tonight!” French thought as he hastily glanced at his watch. It was just 8:42. *What* a stroke of luck! He would travel by it, and with any reasonable good fortune he would have these Vanes safe in his clutches before another dozen hours had passed.

As a man of action French was unsurpassed. Within five minutes he had called an assistant, a keen, efficient young sergeant named Carter, and instructed him to join him that night on the 9:30 Continental train from Waterloo, had sent another keen, efficient helper posthaste to have extradition warrants and other necessaries sent to the same train, and had rung up for a taxi to take him home to tell his wife of his change of plan and to put two or three things together for the journey. In short, thanks to his energy, the hands of the Waterloo station clock had scarcely reached 9:25 when he and Sergeant Carter reached the platform from which the boat train was about to start. Awaiting them was Manning, the other keen and efficient assistant, who handed over warrants for the arrest and extradition of Mr. and Mrs. Vane, passports, English and French money, as well as an introduction to the French police at Havre.

“Good, Manning! That’s all right,” French approved as he took over the munitions of war. In another couple of minutes the train drew slowly out of the station, and increasing its speed as it passed the myriad lights of South London, was soon roaring through the darkness of the open country beyond.

===Chinese===
在苏格兰场这座庞大的机构里，把天下万物的情报编列索引，早已修炼得不止是一门精妙的技艺。倘使弗伦奇想知道布拉格有多少居民、三一公会（Trinity House）的元老们平素有哪几样雅好，或是恒河在安拉阿巴德（Allahabad）处江面多宽，立刻便会有札记或参考书奉上，把所需资料供应得一应俱全。何况眼下这问题不过是火车轮船的时刻，那更不在话下。电话里的答复没有让他久等：布斯轮船公司的班轮“伊诺克号”（*Enoch*）已于前一天下午驶离利物浦，沿途停靠勒阿弗尔、波尔图、里斯本、马德拉和帕拉，最后再溯亚马孙河上行一千英里，完成这趟前往马瑙斯的航程。而且，她要在勒阿弗尔等候南安普敦来的邮船衔接，而接驳这条水路的火车，二十七日晚上 9:30 从滑铁卢车站（Waterloo）开出。

“今晚就有一班！”弗伦奇急忙瞥了一眼手表，心里盘算着。此刻正是 8:42。运气*何等*凑巧！他要搭这班车走，只要时运不算太坏，不出十二个钟头，这对文恩夫妇便稳稳落进他的掌心了。

论雷厉风行，弗伦奇无人能及。不出五分钟，他已唤来一名助手——一个名叫卡特（Carter）的精明干练的年轻警士——吩咐他当晚到滑铁卢车站会合，同乘 9:30 的欧陆列车；又派了另一个同样精明干练的帮手火速去办，把引渡令和其他必备物件送上同一班列车；还打电话叫了一辆出租汽车，送他回家一趟，把计划的变动告知太太，顺带收拾两三件行装。总而言之，多亏他这份干劲，滑铁卢车站大钟的指针还没走到 9:25，他和卡特警士已经踏上了邮船联运列车即将发车的站台。另一个精明干练的助手曼宁（Manning）正在站上候着，交来逮捕并引渡文恩先生和文恩太太的令状、护照、英镑和法郎，还有一封致勒阿弗尔法国警方的介绍函。

“好样的，曼宁！这就齐了。”弗伦奇一面接过这套“作战军械”，一面赞许道。不到两分钟，列车徐徐驶出车站，掠过南伦敦的万家灯火，越开越快，不久便咆哮着冲进郊野的黑暗之中。

===Original===
Fortunately, the night was calm and the boat was not crowded, so that the detectives were able to get berths and a sleep to prepare them for their toils on the following day. They reached Havre on time, and jumping into a taxi were driven to the berth of the *Enoch*, which was some distance down the docks. French hurried on board and asked to see the Captain, while Carter remained at the gangway lest the quarry, seeing French and knowing his appearance, might take fright and attempt to slip ashore.

Captain Davis saw French immediately.

“Sit down, Mr. French,” he said pleasantly when he had examined the other’s credentials, “and let me know what I can do for you.”

French took the proffered seat as he drew from his pocket Mrs. Vane’s photograph as well as her description and that of her husband.

===Chinese===
幸而夜里风平浪静，船上也不拥挤，两位侦探得以弄到铺位，睡了一觉养足精神，好应付次日的辛劳。他们准时抵达勒阿弗尔，跳上一辆出租汽车，驶往码头深处*伊诺克号*的泊位。弗伦奇匆匆上船求见船长，卡特则留在舷梯口守着——生怕猎物撞见弗伦奇、认得他的相貌，惊慌之下企图溜上岸去。

戴维斯船长（Captain Davis）立刻接见了弗伦奇。

他验看过来者的证件，和颜悦色地说：“弗伦奇先生，请坐。有什么我能效劳的，尽管吩咐。”

弗伦奇在让出来的椅子上落座，一面从口袋里掏出文恩太太的照片，还有她本人及其丈夫的体貌描述。

===Original===
“I’ll tell you, Captain,” he answered. “I’m after a man and woman who are wanted for murder and robbery. They call themselves Mr. and Mrs. Vane, though I don’t know if this is their real name or even if they are married. I have learned that they booked with you from Liverpool to Manáos, but I only found that out last night, so I came over by Southampton in the hope of making an arrest. There,” he passed over his photograph and papers, “are the descriptions.”

The Captain glanced at him as he took the papers. He did not speak until he had looked through the latter, then he said gravely:

“I’m afraid, Mr. French, they’ve been one too many for you this time. A Mr. and Mrs. Vane did book passages and even came on board at Liverpool, but they left the ship almost immediately and didn’t turn up again. I assumed that some accident had prevented their return, and that they would follow by Southampton as you did, but from what you tell me it looks as if they had learned you were on their track and made a bolt for it. But we had better see the purser. He will tell us details.”

French was aghast. Once again had happened to him what he had so often previously experienced. When he was most sure of himself and most confident of success, that was the time of failure! How often had he taken a sporting chance, doubtful of himself and his ability to meet a situation, and the occasion had resulted in a brilliant coup. And how often, alas, had his certainty of success ended in disaster!

===Chinese===
“船长，是这么回事，”他答道，“我正在追捕一男一女两人，他们因谋杀和抢劫受到通缉。他们自称文恩先生和文恩太太，不过我不知道这是否真姓，甚至不知道两人究竟是不是夫妻。我打听到他们在贵公司订了利物浦到马瑙斯的舱位，可我昨天夜里才查出来，于是赶紧从南安普敦渡海过来，指望能把人拿住。喏，”他把照片和材料递过去，“这是体貌描述。”

船长接过材料，瞥了他一眼。他把材料从头到尾看完，这才开口，语气凝重：

“弗伦奇先生，恐怕这一回是他们棋高一着，把您将住了。的确有一位文恩先生和文恩太太订了舱位，甚至在利物浦上了船，可他们几乎立刻就离了船，再没有露面。我原以为是有什么意外绊住了他们回船的脚步，他们会像您这样从南安普敦赶过来，可照您这么一说，倒像是他们得知您盯上了他们，索性溜之大吉了。不过，我们最好还是见见事务长，细节他都清楚。”

弗伦奇大惊失色。他从前屡屡尝过的滋味，这一回又来了。每逢他最自负、最有把握的时候，偏偏就是败下阵来的时候！多少回，他自己都没底，疑心应付不了局面，硬着头皮搏上一把，结果偏偏大获全胜；可叹又有多少回，他自以为胜券在握，到头来却一败涂地！

===Original===
By the time the purser arrived, he had to some extent recovered his equanimity. “Mr. Jennings—Inspector French of the C.I.D.,” the Captain introduced them. “Sit down, Jennings, and hear what the Inspector wants. It’s about that Mr. and Mrs. Vane that came aboard at Liverpool and left again before we sailed. Ask him what you want to know, Mr. French.”

Mr. Jennings was a shrewd, efficient-looking man of about forty, and as French began to speak he felt a comfortable assurance that at least he would receive in answer to his questions concisely-worded statements of accurately observed facts.

“It’s this way, Mr. Jennings,” he explained. “These Vanes are wanted for murder and robbery. I traced them to your ship, and crossed last night from London, hoping to arrest them here. But the Captain tells me I have missed them. Perhaps you’ll give me any information you can about them.”

“There’s not much to tell,” the purser answered. “They came aboard about noon on Thursday, and Mr. Vane showed me their tickets and asked for their stateroom. The tickets were singles from Liverpool to Manáos, all OK. An upper deck stateroom, No. 12, had been reserved at the London office, and I gave the number to their cabin steward and saw him leading the way there with the luggage. About half an hour later they came back to my office and asked what time the ship sailed. I told them three o’clock. Mr. Vane said they had to go ashore to complete some business, but would be back in good time. They then left in the direction of the gangway.”

===Chinese===
等到事务长来到，他已多少恢复了镇定。“詹宁斯先生——这位是刑事调查部的弗伦奇探长。”船长替二人引见。“詹宁斯，坐下，听听探长要问什么。就是在利物浦上船、开船前又离船的那对文恩夫妇的事。弗伦奇先生，您想问什么尽管问。”

詹宁斯先生四十上下年纪，一副精明干练的模样。弗伦奇刚一开口，心里便踏实了几分：至少他问出来的，会是措辞简洁、观察确凿的实况陈述。

“是这么回事，詹宁斯先生，”他说明道，“这对文恩夫妇因谋杀和抢劫受到通缉。我追查到他们上了贵船，昨天夜里从伦敦渡海过来，指望在这里把他们逮捕。可船长告诉我，我扑了个空。你若知道他们的情况，还请尽量见告。”

“要说的情况不多，”事务长答道，“他们是星期四中午前后上船的，文恩先生给我看了船票，问他们的特等舱在哪儿。票是利物浦到马瑙斯的单程票，一切无误。上层甲板 12 号特等舱是伦敦营业部预留的，我把舱号交给他们的舱室侍者，亲眼看着他领路把行李送了过去。约莫半个钟头后，两人回到我的办公室，问船几点开。我告诉他们三点。文恩先生说他们必须上岸把几件事办妥，但会及早赶回船上。说完便朝舷梯那边去了。”

===Original===
“Did you actually see them go ashore?”

“No, you can’t see out on deck from the office.”

“Yes? And then?”

“After dinner their cabin steward asked me if I knew anything about them. He said they hadn’t been down for dinner, and he couldn’t find them anywhere about the ship. We had a look round, and then I spoke to Captain Davis, and he had a thorough search made. They have never been seen since, and they’re certainly not on board now.”

“They couldn’t have hidden somewhere and slipped ashore here in Havre?”

“Quite impossible. There’s not the slightest doubt they missed the boat at Liverpool.”

===Chinese===
“你亲眼看见他们上岸了吗？”

“没有，从办公室里望不见外面甲板。”

“哦，后来呢？”

“晚饭后，他们的舱室侍者来问我知道不知道他们的去向。他说两人没下来用晚饭，船上各处也寻不见人影。我们四下里找了一遭，我又禀明了戴维斯船长，他下令做了一次彻底搜查。从那以后再没人见过他们，如今他们肯定不在船上。”

“他们会不会躲在什么地方，到勒阿弗尔这儿才溜上岸？”

“绝无可能。毫不含糊，他们是在利物浦误了船。”

===Original===
“Intentionally or unintentionally?” the Captain interjected.
